# Help verification

## Firewall: Import and Export use mPanel's text format (30 October 2026, after 1.0.63-beta.2)

Branch: `feat/firewall-mpanel-format`, on top of #351 (Firewall Export saves through Android's Save as dialog, the fix for #346) because it changes the same function. Fixes #349. Code: new `lib/firewallMpanel.ts` (the text format, pure), `readImportedRules` in `lib/firewallRules.ts`, `FirewallManager.tsx` (what Export writes, the Import dialog's hint and placeholder, and the rules it passes to Import); help: the Export and Import paragraphs of `docs/help/firewall.md`; tests: new `scripts/test-firewall-mpanel.mjs`, run by `npm run test:firewall-rules`. No API calls added, no dependency.

mPanel's Firewall Import/Export dialog shows one JSON array whose rules have `sourceaddr`, `destaddr`, `destport`, `action`, `description` and `protocol` (the sample in #349). BLDesk's Import refused it at the first rule and its Export wrote the API's own fields, so neither read the other's files. Import now reads both, told apart by the keys, and Export writes mPanel's. Documentation addresses only (192.0.2.0/24, 198.51.100.0/24, 203.0.113.0/24) appear in the tests, the help and the evidence.

| String | Rendered by | Result |

In real Electron the file written for rules imported from the sample is byte-identical to `JSON.stringify(sample, null, 2)` of it, which is the layout of the sample in #349 (two-space indent, one key per line) apart from the sample file's final newline, which Export, like the one it replaces, does not write; its keys are in the same order as the sample's. |

A rule with ports 22 and 80 wrote `"22,80"`, one without wrote `""`. |

An address with another netmask that is not a whole range is written by Export as the card has it and refused by Import (unit tests; not run in Electron). |

Message seen in the dialog: `Rule 2 has "source_addresses", which is BLDesk's and the API's format, but Rule 1 has "sourceaddr", which is mPanel's. Use one format for every rule in the file.` |

| “A rule with a key other than the six, such as `enabled`, refuses the whole file, naming the rule and the key, rather than leave out something that could change what the rule does” | `readMpanelRule`: any key outside `MPANEL_KEYS` returns an error, so nothing is imported | New. Message seen: `Rule 2 has "enabled", which BLDesk does not know. Nothing was imported, rather than drop a field that could change what the rule does. …` In the API's own format an unknown key is still left out, as this page already said. |
| “`sourceaddr` must name at least one address, and a rule with an empty `destaddr` is written with 0.0.0.0/0, like a rule with no destination addresses in the other format” | `readMpanelRule` refuses an empty source; an empty destination list goes through `toRuleRequest`, which writes `ANY_ADDRESS` for a rule with none (the same function the other format uses) | New. Decision: an empty destination is read like a missing `destination_addresses` of the API's own format, because the reference requires a destination on every rule and 0.0.0.0/0 is the value that adds no condition; an empty source is refused, as in the other format, so that an address emptied by accident does not open a rule to everyone. |
| “An address with a netmask is written as CIDR when the mask is a whole range, so `192.0.2.0/255.255.255.0` is imported as `192.0.2.0/24`” | `readNetmask`: a mask of ones then zeros becomes its prefix length | New. Only a change of notation, to the form the reference documents. Unit tests cover masks of ten lengths from /0 to /32 and seven masks with gaps; the address part is kept as written. |
| “One other address with a netmask, `0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255`, which mPanel's text uses in its multicast rule, is kept when the server already has that rule: as a destination address (`destaddr` or `destination_addresses`) in a rule exactly like one the server has now, it is written back exactly as it is, and BLDesk does not interpret it. Import never adds a new rule with it: in a rule the server does not have, or has with anything different, it is refused, naming the rule and the key, and so it is as a source address. It is not in the API reference, which documents only an IPv4 address or a CIDR range.” | `PASSED_THROUGH_DESTINATION`, `isDestinationAddress` and the check at the end of `readImportedRules` in `lib/firewallRules.ts`: a rule whose destinations have the string is accepted only if `describeFirewallRule` (`lib/diff.ts`, the text the confirmation's diff compares) of it equals that of a rule the server has now, and each rule the server has covers one rule of the file, so a copy is new; `FirewallManager.tsx` passes the card's rules (`currentRules`). `openapi.json`: `source_addresses` and `destination_addresses` both say “Each address may be an individual IPv4 address or a range in IPv4 CIDR notation.” | Changed in the third commit; the second accepted it as a destination in any rule. The reference does not document this form, so BLDesk does not add it to anything: it keeps a rule the server already returned, as it came. The grounds are observations: on a throwaway server on 29 October 2026 the API accepted it as a destination address and returned it unchanged (see Observations below), mPanel's text uses it (the sample in #349), and a server that holds it returns it, so an export of such a server imports again on that server. Seen in real Electron: the sample file exactly as pasted imports on a server that has those rules, with the write body `["224.0.0.0/4","0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255"]` for the first rule; the same file is refused on a server that has not, and so is the rule with another description, with a port added, copied, or new, each naming the rule and the key (message: `Rule 1: "destaddr" has 0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255, which BLDesk keeps only in a rule this server already has, as it is, and does not add to a new or changed rule. Nothing was imported: remove that address from "destaddr" and import again.`). |

| “Any other address whose netmask is not a whole range, such as `192.0.2.0/255.0.255.0` or `0.0.0.254/0.0.0.255`, cannot be written as CIDR, so the file is refused with the rule and the address named and nothing is imported: remove that address from the rule, or replace it with addresses or ranges, and import again” | `readNetmask` returns `gap` and `isDestinationAddress` is false for it; `netmaskRefusal` is the message | New. The API reference documents only an IPv4 address or a CIDR range, so BLDesk does not import this form (the one exception is 0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255, as a destination address). Nothing was imported: remove that address from the rule, or replace it with addresses or CIDR ranges, and import again.` `isIpv4OrRange` was not changed. ### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (guards, help guard with 194 quotes, all unit tests), `npm run build` and `npm run test:terminal` in a throwaway Ubuntu 24.04 VM, on the committed code. `npm run test:firewall-rules` runs 28 tests, 19 of them new, in `scripts/test-firewall-mpanel.mjs`: the sample imports to the expected API-shaped rules; the API's own format is unchanged; mixed formats, unknown keys, bad types and empty items refused with the message; either case; empty ports, destination and description; whitespace; port ranges kept; contiguous masks written as CIDR; `0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255` kept as a destination in both formats and written unchanged only in a rule the server has, refused in a rule it has not or has differently (another description, a port, a copy, a new rule), refused as a source in both, and any other mask with a gap (several, including one with a gap in the middle and near misses of it) refused; the 100-rule limit; the key order, indentation and joins of Export; Export then Import and Import then Export are exact.
- Faults put into the code on purpose, each making tests fail, then taken out: the gap check removed (2 fail), the prefix off by one (2), the unknown-key refusal removed (1), the mixed-format refusal removed (2), upper case no longer read (13), Export's key order changed (3), Export not upper-casing (3), ports joined with a space (3), items not trimmed (3), an empty source accepted (2), the gap refusal in the reader removed (2); and, for the netmask destination: the source refusal removed (1), the value accepted as an mPanel source (1), every netmask form accepted as a destination (3), the value matched loosely (1), no longer accepted anywhere (3), converted instead of passed through (2), dropped silently (2), the API-shape destination check not using the helper (2), the API-shape source refusal removed (1), API-shape sources accepted like destinations (1). For the keep-what-exists rule: the check removed (1 fails), the count removed so a copy is covered again (1), any rule allowed once the server has one with the address (1), the description ignored (1); each makes the one test that covers the rule fail, which has many assertions.
- Real Electron against the GUI mock (fictitious token), with the mock's request log checked by `check-requests.mjs` (0 invalid bodies). First the previous build (4 checks) and this branch (37 checks, run on the first commit), then this branch for the netmask change (26 checks, the second commit), then for the rule that the server must already have the masked rule (22 checks, the third commit, all passed, no page problems):
  - Before: Export wrote the rules in the API's own fields; Import of the mPanel sample was refused with `Rule 1: "action" must be "accept" or "drop".`
  - The sample file exactly as pasted, with the multicast rule's `0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255`, on a server that already has those three rules: no refusal; the confirmation read “Replaces the current 3 rules with the 3 imported.” with every rule unchanged in its diff; Import wrote one request whose body is the three API-shaped rules (lower case, lists, no mPanel keys, no `destination_ports` or `description` for the empty strings), the first with `destination_addresses` exactly `["224.0.0.0/4","0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255"]`.
  - Export on that server wrote a file equal, byte for byte, to the sample file as pasted but for the sample file's final newline; its keys are `sourceaddr`, `destaddr`, `destport`, `action`, `description`, `protocol` in that order, with `"22,80"` and `""` for the ports. Chosen again through “Choose JSON File...” on the same server it was accepted (“Replaces the current 3 rules with the 3 imported.”; Cancel wrote nothing). Chosen on another server that does not have the masked rule it was refused in the dialog with the message above, naming Rule 1 and `destaddr`, with no confirmation and no write.
  - Refused in the dialog, with no confirmation and no write: the masked address in `sourceaddr` (mPanel text) with the source message; `192.0.2.0/255.0.255.0` in `destaddr` with the other-masks message, which now ends its exception “as a destination address in a rule this server already has”. (The masked address in `source_addresses` and `0.0.0.254/0.0.0.255` are covered by the unit tests, not run in Electron this time.)
  - On the server that has the masked rule: the masked rule changed (another description, a port added), copied, or a new rule with the masked address added were each refused, naming the rule (1, 1, 4, 4) and `destaddr`, with no confirmation and no write; another rule edited (a port and its description) with the masked rule unchanged was accepted, and the write body kept the masked rule exactly as it was and had the edit. The API's own fields with `["224.0.0.0/4","0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255"]` and the same rule otherwise: accepted on the server that has it (confirmation shown, cancelled) and refused on another naming `destination_addresses`. A file written by the previous build's Export (the API's own fields) imported in the earlier run and is unchanged code, not run again.
  - A server whose rules (set through the mock's firewall endpoint) are the multicast rule with the masked address and an SSH rule imported and exported as above; this and the refusals are in `mpfmt-run3.mjs` (22 checks).
  - The rule editor was not changed and still behaves as in the first run's check: for a server with `0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255` as a destination and `192.0.2.0/255.255.255.0` as a source, the card shows both as given and flags no row; editing the description of a different rule and pressing Save changes is allowed, and the two rules go back exactly as returned; editing any cell of the rule with the masked destination, even only its description, flags “"0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255" is not an IPv4 address or range, such as 192.0.2.1 or 192.0.2.0/24.” and turns Save changes off; moving the rule leaves it on. That is the editor's own address check (`problemsOf` in `lib/firewallDraft.ts`), a separate matter from Import.

### Observations on a throwaway server (29 October 2026)

One disposable server (the smallest size, created and deleted within a few minutes, nothing else on the account touched), its firewall rules set with `change_advanced_firewall_rules` and read back with `GET /v2/servers/{id}/advanced_firewall_rules`. These are observations, not documented behaviour; the reference does not describe them.

- A drop rule with destinations `224.0.0.0/4` and `0.0.0.255/0.0.0.255`, and an accept rule with source `192.0.2.0/255.255.255.0`, were accepted (HTTP 200, the action completed) and returned exactly as sent: neither form was converted to CIDR. BLDesk still writes a mask that is a whole range as CIDR on import, which is the notation the reference documents.
- A TCP port `1000-2000` was refused with HTTP 400, `firewall_rules[0].destination_ports[0]: The port value must only contain digits and an optional single : if a range is being specified.`; `1000:2000` was accepted and returned as written. BLDesk does not check ports on import, so a file with a hyphen range reaches the API and is refused there.

Not tested: mPanel's own Import dialog reading what Export writes, or its Export producing anything beyond the sample (no mPanel access: only the sample in #349 was used); how mPanel writes a port range; whether BinaryLane documents the netmask form: the reference does not, and BLDesk only keeps a rule the server already has and never adds one with it, but Copy to another server (not changed here) writes the rules of one server to another as they are, including such a rule; the Android app for this change (Export passes the new text to the same `saveTextFile` as before, whose Save as dialog was checked for #346); Windows and macOS.

## Network map: Export PNG and Export SVG save in the Android app (29 October 2026, after 1.0.63-beta.1)

Branch: `feat/android-export-network-map`, on top of the tags export (#345), which brought the Android Save as dialog. No new runtime dependencies and no API calls. `docs/help/map.md` has one changed paragraph, “Navigate and export”.

The two export buttons used a download link, which an Android WebView ignores (#348). They now go through `lib/saveFile.ts`: `saveTextFile` for the SVG (a text file) and a new `saveBlobFile` for the PNG, which in the Android app hands the plugin the file's bytes as base64 in pieces (`FileSavePlugin.stageBase64`) and then opens the same `ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT` dialog as `saveText` (`saveStaged`), and everywhere else keeps the download link. The bytes cannot travel in the call that opens the dialog: see “Found on the phone” below. A text longer than 100,000 characters now takes the same route in `saveTextFile` (it used to go in the `saveText` call, with the same limit); a shorter one is saved exactly as before. `NetworkMap.tsx` draws the PNG as before and reports a failed export with `notifyFailure('Could not export the network map', …)`.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “the files are named bldesk-network-map.svg and bldesk-network-map.png” | The two literals passed to `saveTextFile` and `saveBlobFile` in `exportAsSvg` and `exportAsPng` (`NetworkMap.tsx`) | New. Unchanged names: they are the ones the download link used. |
| “the PNG is twice the size of the map” | `renderPng` in `NetworkMap.tsx` (`scale = 2`; the canvas is `layout.width * 2` by `layout.height * 2`); the button's title is “Export PNG (2×)” | New sentence for an unchanged behaviour. Checked on the exported files: 2592 × 1824 for a 1296 × 912 map. |
| “Drawing the PNG can take several seconds on a phone”; the buttons are dimmed and the header says “Preparing the PNG…” | `runExport` in `NetworkMap.tsx`: `disabled={exporting}` with `disabled:opacity-50`, and a status set 600 ms after a PNG export starts (so a desktop, where drawing takes a blink, does not flash it) and replaced when the export ends | New. Measured on the phone: 13 seconds, see below. |
| In the Android app the export opens Android's Save as dialog; backing out saves nothing and shows no error | `saveBlobFile` and `saveTextFile` call the plugin when `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()`; `saved` is false on Back, which `runExport` does not report | New. Run on a phone, see below. |
| Android adds a number to the name if it is taken, and “the map's header says the name the file was saved under” | `FileSavePlugin.displayName` reads the name back from the provider; `runExport` sets `Saved as <name>.` in the paragraph under the header counts (`role="status"`) | New. Run on a phone, see below. |
| “Could not export the network map” card | `notifyFailure` in the `catch` of `runExport`: a plugin rejection, a map that could not be drawn (`renderPng`), or a map that is not on screen (`mapSvg`) | New. The drawing failures were forced on desktop; a plugin write failure was not forced. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (guards, help guard with 188 quotes, 492 tests of which 3 are new in `scripts/test-save-file.mjs`), `npm run build` and `npm run test:terminal`, in a throwaway Ubuntu 24.04 VM. The new tests encode every byte value and every length around a slice and an 8 MB file, and compare with `Buffer`'s base64; making a slice as long as the file makes the large-file test fail with “Maximum call stack size exceeded”, and putting it back makes it pass.
- Real Electron against the GUI mock (28 servers, 3 VPCs, 3 load balancers; fictitious token), driven over the debugging port, the downloads caught with `Browser.setDownloadBehavior`, on the build before this change and on the build after it:
  - Export PNG and Export SVG in the light and the dark theme: the four files are byte-identical before and after (the PNG is 2592 × 1824, 527,119 bytes light and 510,055 dark; the SVG is 1296 × 912, 46,869 and 46,794 bytes). The PNG shows the map. No status line appears on desktop.
  - A double click on either button gives one file. With `canvas.toBlob` forced to answer nothing, and again with an image that cannot load, the PNG button gives a “Could not export the network map” card saying “The map could not be drawn as an image.”, and both buttons work again. (Before this change the buttons did nothing visible in these cases.) With the draw delayed by 1.8 s the header said “Preparing the PNG…” 1.2 s in and both buttons were dimmed and disabled, and the line and the dimming were gone when it ended; an ordinary desktop export never showed the line.
- A phone (Samsung SM-S948B, Android 17), a throwaway build of the branch under its own application id (never the real app's) against the same mock, driven by real touches (`adb shell input tap`) and the WebView's debugging port; the files were pulled off the phone with `adb pull`:
  - The Network Map page opens from More (the drawer's “Network Map”), shows the header counts “28 servers · 3 VPCs · 3 load balancers · SSH reachable from the internet on 1”, draws the map and the PNG and SVG buttons. The first view does not fit the whole map across the width; the page was not changed.
  - **Before** (the tags export build, 4daef06): real taps on PNG and SVG opened no dialog, wrote no file (nothing named `bldesk-network-map*` anywhere under the phone's storage) and showed no status or failure.
  - SVG: Android's Save as dialog opened with `bldesk-network-map.svg` suggested; SAVE wrote 46,794 bytes (dark theme) and 46,869 (light). Both parse as XML (Python's `xml.dom.minidom`; `xmllint` is not installed here) with the root `svg`, the `xmlns`, 103 `text` elements, the width, height and viewBox 1296 × 912 and the background `#212529` (dark) or `#f8f9fa` (light). Each is byte-identical to the desktop SVG of the same theme. The header said “Saved as bldesk-network-map.svg.”
  - PNG: the dialog opened with `bldesk-network-map.png` suggested; the saved file is a PNG (`file`, signature bytes, 8-bit RGBA), 2592 × 1824, which is twice the 1296 × 912 of the SVG's width and height. Looked at, it shows the whole map in the theme the page was in: dark corner pixel (33, 37, 41) and light (248, 249, 250), about 40% of the image in the background colour in both. 464,320 bytes (dark), 489,026 and 506,175 (light, two exports).
  - Back out of the dialog (PNG and SVG): no file, no status line, no failure card, both buttons usable again.
  - The same name again: Android saved `bldesk-network-map (1).png` (and `(2)`, `(3)`, and `(1).svg`) and the header said “Saved as bldesk-network-map (1).png.” The second PNG was byte-identical to the first.
  - Three quick taps on PNG started one export: one dialog, one `stageBase64`/`saveStaged` sequence in the bridge log, one file.
  - **Drawing the PNG took 13 seconds**: `drawImage` 3 ms, `canvas.toBlob` 13.2 s on two runs (the SVG is rasterised inside `toBlob`); the dialog opens after that. The change adds the dimmed buttons and “Preparing the PNG…” for this wait; checked on the phone 4 seconds after a tap. The old code spent the same time on a phone and showed nothing, because it never saved anything.
  - The mock fleet's PNG is about 0.5 MB (0.46 to 0.51 MB depending on the theme and the device's fonts), so 0.6 to 0.7 MB as base64.
  - **Found on the phone and fixed:** the first version of the PNG export put the base64 in the one plugin call that opens the dialog. The app closed itself the moment the dialog opened: `TransactionTooLargeException: data parcel size 2656404 bytes`, the options of that call (1,326,612 bytes) being kept twice in the activity's saved state by Capacitor. The bytes are now staged in the plugin in 256 KB pieces and the call that opens the dialog carries only the name and type. A 12 MB file of random bytes (16.8 MB of base64, staged in 0.6 s) went through the plugin and the dialog and was saved with the same SHA-256. The same limit applies to the `saveText` call: a text of about a quarter of a million characters would close the app, so `saveTextFile` now stages anything over 100,000 characters as bytes (the SVG of this fleet is 46,000 characters and goes as before). That route was exercised on the phone with the 12 MB file through the plugin, not through the SVG button, which has no map big enough here.
  - Afterwards the test files (by exact name), the throwaway app (including its copy in the phone's second user space) and the port forwards were removed; the real app was not touched.

Not tested: a write that fails inside the Android save (so the “Could not export the network map” card was not seen for a plugin error, only for the two drawing failures above); a cloud provider such as Drive in the dialog (only Downloads); other Android versions and phones; a map large enough for the canvas limit (the PNG is drawn at twice the map's size, and a very large fleet may exceed what the WebView can allocate, which would show the failure card rather than a file); the SVG button on a map of more than 100,000 characters of SVG; the browser build on a device; Windows and macOS.

### After review of #353 (29 October 2026)

The reviewer found one blocking problem; nothing else in the review needed a change.

| Finding | Evidence | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Two exports could swap their bytes.** The plugin kept one staging buffer for the whole app, started over by any piece with `first`, and the dialog's answer wrote whatever the buffer held. `saveBlobFile` did not make the pieces and the dialog one step, and the guard in `NetworkMap` is per screen: leaving the map and coming back during the 13-second PNG draw, then tapping SVG, left two exports under way. | The reviewer's runs on a phone: stage A, open its dialog, stage B with `first`, answer A's dialog: the file was B and A's call said it saved A's name. | `lib/saveFile.ts`: every save in the Android app is one transaction, and transactions take turns at module level, first come first served, across screens and remounts (`createFileSaver`: the text call, or the pieces and then the dialog, including the long-text route). One that fails, or is backed out of, gives its turn up like one that succeeds. The browser download is not held up. `FileSavePlugin`: the staged bytes are kept under the id of their file (`stageBase64` and `saveStaged` carry it) and the answer takes exactly its own call's; at most three staged files are kept, the oldest let go; a second dialog is refused while one is open. |
| **Capacitor gives a dialog's answer to the last call that opened a dialog.** | `Plugin.java` of `@capacitor/android` 8.5.2 (the version in `package-lock.json`): `startActivityForResult` sets one field, `lastPluginCallId`, and `triggerActivityCallback` looks up `bridge.getSavedCall(lastPluginCallId)` whoever opened the dialog being answered. A second dialog opened while the first is open therefore takes the first's answer, and the first call never settles. | The turn-taking prevents it; the plugin now refuses the second dialog as well. |

What a person sees while a save waits its turn (the order below was seen on a phone; the “Saved as” line for the PNG was not, since it belongs to the screen that was left): nothing different, because a save only waits when a second export is started on another screen while one is still going; the one on the screen they left keeps its own state. If they leave the map during the PNG draw and tap SVG, the SVG dialog opens first (the PNG has not reached the plugin) and the PNG's dialog follows when that one is answered; the PNG's “Saved as” line goes to the screen that was left and is not shown. Starting the PNG a second time on the new screen queues a second PNG dialog.

Checks performed, in the Ubuntu 24.04 VM: `npm run typecheck` (503 tests, 11 more than before, all passing) and `npm run build` and `npm run test:terminal`. `scripts/test-save-file.mjs` gives the saver a fake plugin that behaves as the plugin and Capacitor do (one dialog's answer goes to the last call that opened a dialog; a dialog stays open until the test answers it), once as the plugin was when reviewed (one buffer for the whole plugin, no limit on dialogs) and once as it is now (bytes by id, one dialog at a time). Each runs: two overlapped saves (one of three pieces) each end with their own bytes under their own name, and the second stages nothing while the first dialog is open; save, back out, save leaves no bytes of the first in the second and writes nothing for the first; a stage that fails, and a write the provider refuses, do not hold up the saves behind them; text, long text and images in any order get one dialog each in the order asked; eight saves with mixed answers all settle, each with its own result; and the browser download starts at the call with the plugin untouched. Taking the turn-taking out makes ten of the fourteen fail (the others being the base64 tests and the browser one); making a failed save hold the queue makes four fail; with both put back all fourteen pass.

On a phone (a Samsung SM-S948B on Android 17, throwaway builds under their own application id, real touches, the 28-server mock, with the PNG conversion held for 25 s from the page so the overlap is deterministic): on a build of 5bbc861 the reviewer's sequence (tap PNG, leave the map and come back, tap SVG, let the PNG finish) stacked two Save as dialogs; once both were answered the PNG held the right bytes and the SVG file was **empty (0 bytes)**, its call never settled, and both Export buttons on the new screen stayed disabled until a reload. The direct plugin sequence (stage A, open A's dialog, stage B with `first`, answer A) wrote B's bytes under A's name and resolved `saved: true`, as the reviewer found. On this commit the PNG sent nothing to the plugin while the SVG dialog was open; the SVG dialog came first and then the PNG's; the SVG is byte-identical to the desktop SVG (same SHA-256) and the PNG is a valid 2592×1824 image; the direct sequence refused the second dialog (“Another Save as dialog is still open. Finish or close it, then try again.”) and A's file held A's bytes; and with Back on the SVG dialog the queued PNG's dialog opened next and saved. Regression on this commit: SVG and PNG saves, Back (no file, no message, buttons usable), the numbers Android adds, a 12 MB file through the staging with an id (SHA-256 equal), and Export tags in Settings through the same lock; and a normal save still worked after a refused second dialog and after Back, so the dialog flag is not left set.

Not tested: a dialog whose answer never arrives while the page is alive (the next save would wait for it until the page is reloaded; judged unreachable, and given no timeout because a timeout could release the turn while a dialog is still open), a provider write failure inside the Android save, process death while a dialog is open, a cloud provider in the dialog, and other Android versions or phones.
## Templates on Android: Export and Export all save through Save as, and Import file can choose .yaml files (29 October 2026, after 1.0.63-beta.1)

Branch: `feat/android-export-templates`, stacked on `feat/tags-import-export` (#345), whose `saveTextFile` it uses. No new runtime dependencies and no API calls. One help page changed (`templates.md`), by one added paragraph. Fixes #347 (the second commit also covers the round trip the issue asks for): in the Android app, “Export” and “Export all” saved nothing and said nothing, because a WebView ignores a download link (the same defect #345 fixed for Export tags). The sources are `TemplatesView.tsx` (`exportYaml`, `handleExport`, `handleExportAll`, `yamlType`), `lib/saveFile.ts` (`saveTextFile`), `FileSavePlugin.java` and `lib/serverTemplates.ts` (`templateToYaml`, `bundleToYaml`, unchanged); for the chooser, the `accept` of the file input (`TEMPLATE_FILES`).

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Export” saves the selected template as a YAML file named after it, such as export-demo-web.yaml | `handleExport`: `${item.slug}.yaml`, content `templateToYaml(item.template)`; the slug is `templateSlug(name)`, a starter's is its own (`starter-docker-host`) | New sentence; the name and the content are what the desktop app already wrote. |
| “Export all” saves your own templates, not the starters, as one bundle named bldesk-templates- followed by today's date in UTC | `handleExportAll`: `stored.filter(!error)`, `bundleToYaml`, `bldesk-templates-<YYYY-MM-DD>.yaml` (UTC date, `toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, as before) | New sentence. |
| With none of your own it says so and saves nothing | The notice “Nothing of your own to export yet — the starters ship with the app.”, returned before any save | New sentence, about an existing behaviour. |
| “Import file” and “Paste” read either kind back | `templatesFromImport` accepts a bundle, a single template document, or raw cloud-init | New sentence, about an existing behaviour. |
| On desktop and in the browser build the file is downloaded | `saveTextFile` keeps the download link when `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()` is false | New sentence; the desktop path is unchanged (below). |
| In the Android app both buttons open Android's own Save as dialog; backing out saves nothing and shows no error | `saveTextFile` calls `FileSavePlugin.saveText` (`ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT`); `saved: false` clears the notice and shows nothing | New sentence. Run on a phone, see below. |
| The page says the name the file was saved under, because Android adds a number when the name is taken | `exportYaml` shows “Saved as <name>.” from the name `FileSavePlugin` reads back from the provider | New sentence. Run on a phone, see below. |
| In the Android app “Import file” opens Android's own file chooser, which does not know .yaml as a type, so BLDesk asks it to offer files of unknown types as well as .json and .txt | The `accept` of the hidden file input: `.yaml,.yml,.json,.txt`, with `application/octet-stream` added when `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()` | New sentence. Run on a phone, see below. |

What the page does around the save, which the help does not describe: Export and Export all are greyed out and a second tap is ignored until the save has finished or the person has backed out (`exportingRef` stops the second tap, `exporting` greys the buttons); a failed write shows its message in the page's red banner (`setError`, the way the page's other failures are shown, with “Could not export.” when there is no message).

### The file type given to Android

`FileSavePlugin` passes the type to `intent.setType(...)`, and Android's dialog decides the file name from it, because Android does not know `.yaml`. #347 asked for the saved name to be checked and the alternatives tried. On the phone (Samsung SM-S948B, Android 17), exporting “Export Demo Web” (suggested name export-demo-web.yaml, Downloads) with each type, then again with the name taken:

| Type passed | Saved as, name free | Saved as, name taken (2nd, 3rd) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `application/x-yaml` (what the download used) | export-demo-web.yaml | export-demo-web.yaml (1) |
| `text/yaml`, `text/x-yaml`, `application/yaml` | export-demo-web.yaml | export-demo-web.yaml (1) |
| `text/plain` | export-demo-web.yaml.txt | export-demo-web.yaml (1).txt |
| `application/octet-stream` | export-demo-web.yaml | export-demo-web (1).yaml, export-demo-web (2).yaml |

So no YAML type loses the extension when the name is free, but all four put the number after it when the name is taken, which leaves a file whose name no longer ends in `.yaml`; `text/plain` adds `.txt`. The generic type keeps `.yaml` last in both cases, because Android does not know the extension and so accepts the one in the name. The Android app therefore passes `application/octet-stream` (`yamlType`); the desktop app and the browser build keep `application/x-yaml` for the download.

### The Import file chooser on Android

The issue asks that the exported file imports back through Import file. On the phone it could not be chosen: the chooser's intent, read from `dumpsys activity`, was `GET_CONTENT` with type `application/json`, because the input's `accept` is `.yaml,.yml,.json,.txt` and Android knows only .json and .txt, and every .yaml file in the folder was greyed out; tapping one did nothing. That is the same before this change, so a .yaml exported from the desktop app could not be imported on a phone either (Paste worked). Adding `application/octet-stream` to the list on Android (the type Android reports for an extension it does not know) changed the intent's type to `application/octet-stream` and made the files choosable. The desktop app and the browser keep the plain extension list; the added type there would only add a generic filter to the open dialog.

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (guards, the help guard with 191 quotes, 489 tests), `npm run build` and `npm run test:terminal`, in a throwaway Ubuntu 24.04 VM.
- Desktop, real Electron against the GUI mock, driven over the debugging port, before (the #345 head) and after: Export all with nothing of your own gave the notice and no file; a pasted two-template bundle imported (“Imported 2 templates.”); Export on an own template, on another own template and on a starter, and Export all, each caught as a download with CDP `Browser.setDownloadBehavior`. All four files from the two builds are identical apart from the `updated_at` and `exported_at` timestamps, which differ per run.
- The page's Android path in real Electron with a fake Capacitor bridge whose `FileSave.saveText` stays pending until answered: a double tap (two clicks in one task, then two more) made one call, with the file name, the YAML and the type, and both buttons were greyed out meanwhile; “Saved as starter-docker-host (1).yaml.” after an answer with a name; Back (`saved: false`) left no message and no error; a rejected write showed its text in the red banner; the buttons were usable again after each. Without the ref, a double tap in one task made two calls (found first, then fixed).
- On a phone, with a throwaway build of the branch under its own application id (never the real app's), against the GUI mock, driven by real touches (`adb shell input tap`) and the WebView's debugging port. Before the change (the #345 head) a real tap on Export and on Export all fired the download link once with a `blob:` URL and nothing else happened: no dialog, no file in Downloads, no message. After: a real tap on Export opened Android's Save as dialog with export-demo-web.yaml suggested; SAVE wrote 590 bytes, the page said “Saved as export-demo-web.yaml.”, and the file pulled off the phone parses as YAML and equals what the desktop wrote for the same template (identical text apart from the timestamps). Export all: bldesk-templates-2026-10-29.yaml, 1422 bytes, a `bldesk/template-bundle@1` with the two own templates and none of the starters, identical to the desktop's apart from the timestamps. Back out of the dialog: no file, the earlier “Saved as” line cleared, no error, Export usable. Exporting the same template twice more: “Saved as export-demo-web (1).yaml.” and “(2)”, three identical files. Three taps in one `adb shell` made one plugin call and one dialog.
- The file types above, one at a time with the real button (a throwaway build read the type from local storage so each could be tried without a rebuild; the branch's code, passing `application/octet-stream`, was then built and run with the same checks).
- Round trip on the phone, through Android's own chooser with the real tap on Import file: the exported single file imported (“Imported 1 template.”), and its stored document equals the exported text apart from the name (“Export Demo Web (imported)”, as the page already names a copy) and the timestamp; the exported bundle imported (“Imported 2 templates.”) and both stored documents equal the bundle's entries. Fixtures of each accepted extension, `.json`, `.txt`, `.yaml` and `.yml`, were each listed, chosen and imported (“Imported 1 template.”, four new templates), so adding the generic type did not take .json or .txt out of the chooser.
- On the desktop build the input's `accept` is still `.yaml,.yml,.json,.txt`; in the fake-Android page it is `.yaml,.yml,.json,.txt,application/octet-stream`.
- Afterwards the test files, the throwaway app (including its copy in the phone's second user space) and the port forwards were removed; the real app was not touched.

Not tested: a cloud provider such as Drive in Android's Save as or Open dialogs (only Downloads was used), or a file the provider reports with a type other than the ones above; a write failure inside the Android save (the page's handling of a rejected write was run with the fake bridge, not a real failure); other Android versions and phones; Windows and macOS; the browser build on a device; what the operating system's save dialog does after Export on desktop (a CDP download setting wrote the file directly).
## Firewall: Export saves through Android's Save as dialog (29 October 2026, after 1.0.63-beta.1)

Branch: `feat/android-export-firewall`, stacked on `feat/tags-import-export` (#345), whose `saveTextFile` in `lib/saveFile.ts` and `FileSavePlugin` it uses. Fixes #346. One code change, in `FirewallManager.tsx` (`handleExportJson` and the Export button), and one paragraph in `docs/help/firewall.md`. No API calls, no new dependency.

The Export button on a server's Firewall tab built a `Blob`, made a `blob:` URL and clicked an `<a download>` link. An Android WebView ignores that link, so in the Android app the button did nothing and said nothing (reproduced on a phone, below). It now calls `saveTextFile` with the same file name and the same text; the Android app opens Android's Save as dialog, and the desktop app and the browser build still download the file.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Export saves the rules on the card, as BinaryLane returned them, as a JSON file named firewall-rules- followed by the server's name (or its number when it has no name) and .json” | `handleExportJson`: `JSON.stringify(currentRules, null, 2)`, `currentRules` is the list `fetchFirewallRules` read from `advanced_firewall_rules`; the name is `` `firewall-rules-${activeServer?.name || activeServerId}.json` `` | New sentence. File name and content are unchanged from before (see below). |
| “in the form Import reads back” | The file chosen in Import's file chooser fills the same box that `readImportedRules` checks | New. Imported back on a phone through Android's picker, see below. |
| “It is off while the server has no rules, while changes are waiting and while its Save as dialog is open” | `disabled={currentRules.length === 0 || pending || exporting}` on the Export button; `exporting` is set for as long as `saveTextFile` has not settled | New for the last clause; the first two were already so (the existing sentences naming Export in “While changes are waiting” and when the rules cannot be read are unchanged and still true). |
| “In the Android app it opens Android's own Save as dialog instead of a download, where you choose the folder and can change the name” | `saveTextFile` in `lib/saveFile.ts` calls `FileSavePlugin.saveText` (`ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT`) when `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()` | New. Same sentence as the tags help in `settings.md`, checked again for this button below. |
| “Backing out saves nothing and shows no error” | `saveTextFile` resolves `saved: false` and `handleExportJson` shows nothing for any result | New. |
| “Android adds a number to the name if a file with that name is already there” | Android's own behaviour, read back from the provider by the plugin; this button does not show the name that was used (unlike the Tags card) | New. Seen on the phone: the second Save made `firewall-rules-<name> (1).json`. |
| “If the file cannot be written, “Could not export firewall rules” says why” | `notifyFailure('Could not export firewall rules', err)` | New. Checked in real Electron with a failing write and with a rejecting fake plugin, not on a phone. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (guards, help guard, tests), `npm run build` and `npm run test:terminal` in a throwaway Ubuntu 24.04 VM, on the committed code.
- Desktop unchanged, in real Electron against the GUI mock (fictitious token), before (the base) and after (this branch): Export on server 8100 (`edge-web-syd-01`, four rules) with a CDP download setting wrote `firewall-rules-edge-web-syd-01.json`; the two files are byte-identical (966 bytes, equal to the mock's rules, `JSON.stringify(rules, null, 2)` with no trailing newline). With `URL.createObjectURL` made to throw, the base build raised an uncaught page error and showed nothing, and this branch showed “Could not export firewall rules” with the reason and left Export usable.
- The Android path, simulated in Electron with a fake `FileSave` plugin: one call per tap with the file name, the MIME type `application/json` and the text; Export is off while the call is pending and a second tap makes no second call; Save and Back (`saved: false`) show nothing and turn Export back on; a rejection shows the failure card with the reason. Taking `|| exporting` out of `disabled` made the double-tap checks fail (3 of 11).
- On a phone (Samsung SM-S948B, Android 17), a throwaway build of the base and then of this branch, each under its own application id (never the real app's), against the GUI mock, driven by real touches (`adb shell input tap`) and the WebView's debugging port:
  - Base: a real tap on Export fired the click handler once, which clicked an anchor with `download="firewall-rules-edge-web-syd-01.json"` and a `blob:` URL; no dialog appeared, nothing was added to Downloads, no failure card showed.
  - This branch: the same tap opened Android's Save as dialog with `firewall-rules-edge-web-syd-01.json` suggested; SAVE wrote a 966-byte file, pulled off the phone: it parses, holds the four rules exactly as the mock returns them, and is byte-identical to the file the desktop app wrote; the Firewall tab showed nothing and Export was usable again.
  - Back out of the dialog: no file, no message, no failure card, Export usable again.
  - Export again under the same name: Android saved `firewall-rules-edge-web-syd-01 (1).json` (same content); the Firewall tab says nothing about the name.
  - Repeated clicks in separate event turns (what a double tap is) after the first: the button was already disabled each time, one Back returned to the app, so one dialog had opened.
  - Import back: with the mock's rules for that server replaced by a different single rule, Import, “Choose JSON File...” opened Android's own picker, the exported file was chosen, Apply Rules showed “Replaces the current 1 rule with the 4 imported.” with the four added and the one removed, a real tap on Import applied it, and the mock's rules were equal to the original four.
  - Afterwards the two test files (by exact name), the throwaway app (including its copy in the phone's second user space), the port forwards and the mock were removed; the real app was not touched.

Not tested: a write failure inside the Android save (the failure card was seen only with a failing write on the desktop and a rejecting fake plugin); a cloud provider such as Drive in the Save as dialog (only Downloads was used); two taps in the same event turn, which a person cannot make (a script that clicks three times in one turn opened three dialogs; separate turns, as above, opened one); the browser build on a device; Windows and macOS; what the operating system's save dialog does after Export on the desktop (a CDP download setting wrote the file directly). The Firewall tab shows no “saved” message in the Android app, so the name Android used for a second file is not reported there.

## Tags: export and import as a JSON file (29 October 2026, after 1.0.63-beta.1)

Branch: `feat/tags-import-export`. No new runtime dependencies and no API calls. `docs/help/settings.md` has a new “Tags” section and a new summary and keywords; `servers.md` and `server-settings.md` each gained a sentence pointing at it; `docs/HELP_SPEC.md` and `FEATURES.md` each gained a clause.

Tags are local (the API has none), kept per account profile in local storage. Settings is where the notification switches live, so the Tags card sits beside them. The file format is `lib/tags.ts` (`buildTagsFile`, `readTagsFile`, `planTagsImport`, all pure and unit-tested); `SettingsView.tsx` is the card, the file chooser and the confirmation; `applyTagsImport` in `lib/serverGroups.ts` writes the result.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Tags”, “Export and import tags”, “Export tags”, “Import tags” | `SettingsView.tsx`: the `Card` title, the row heading, the two buttons | New. |
| “how many tags are on how many servers, or that no server has a tag yet, or that the account has no servers listed” | `tagSummary` in `SettingsView.tsx`; the count is `liveTagCounts` over the listed servers, so a tag left on a deleted server is not counted | New. |
| Export is greyed out while no listed server has a tag; Import while no server is listed | `disabled` on the two buttons | New. |
| “a file named bldesk-tags- followed by today's date” | `TAGS_FILE_NAME` (local date, `YYYY-MM-DD`) | New. |
| “one entry for each listed server that carries a tag, with its id, name and tags, and the colour of each of those tags that has one”; tags of deleted servers not exported | `buildTagsFile` | New. The example file has the shape of the file the real button wrote (checked against it); its ids, names and time are invented. |
| “up to 1 MB” | `TAGS_FILE_MAX_BYTES`, checked against `file.size` before the file is read | New. |
| A file that is not a tags file, is not valid JSON, or has an entry it cannot read is “refused as a whole”, “Could not import tags” | `readTagsFile` (every refusal returns its reason, none imports in part); `notifyFailure('Could not import tags', …)` | New. |
| “Prod and @prod both become prod” | `normaliseTag`, the same function the tag editor uses, called by `readTagsFile` | New. |
| Adds, never removes, never changes a colour a tag has, skips servers this account does not list, refuses a file in which none match | `planTagsImport` (union of the two lists; a colour is set only `if (!now)`; `liveIds` check; `plan.missing === read.servers.length` in the card) | New. |
| A new tag with no colour in the file “gets a colour the way any new tag does” | `applyTagsImport` writes colours first, then `saveTags` with its default `autoColor` | New. Seen: `batch` took `slate`, the first preset no tag in use had. |
| “Nothing to import: the servers in the file already have these tags.” | The literal in `importTags`, shown when no server changes and no colour is set | New. |
| The “Import tags” dialog, “Before → after”, the three notes | `confirmAction` in `importTags` (`severity: 'normal'`, `log: false`, `helpSlug: 'settings#tags'`), `diff` and `notes` built from the plan | New. Not written to History: `log: false`, as for other local tag changes. |
| “Afterwards the card says how many tags were added to how many servers”, naming the account profile | The `setTagStatus` after `applyTagsImport` (`Imported into <profile>: added …`); the dialog's summary has `in <profile>` | New. |

Run on a phone, see below. |
| If the device cannot save the tags, “Could not import tags” and nothing is imported; anything written is put back | `applyTagsImport` in `lib/serverGroups.ts` (strict writes, rollback of the keys it changed, the two error texts) | New, after review of #345 (below). |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (guards, help guard with 185 quotes, 60 tag tests of which 10 are new) and `npm run build`, in a throwaway Ubuntu 24.04 VM. Breaking the logic on purpose made the tests fail each time and restoring it made them pass: importing onto servers the account lacks (3 fail), replacing instead of adding (1), a file colour overwriting an existing one (1), exporting colours of tags not in use (1).
- Real Electron against the GUI mock, fictitious token, isolated user data, driven over the debugging port, with seeded tags (including one on a server that does not exist):
  - The card with no tags (Export off, Import on), and with tags (“3 tags on 3 servers in Atlas Demo (fictitious).”, the deleted server's tag left out of the count).
  - Export through the real button wrote `bldesk-tags-<date>.json`: three servers in id order with sorted tags, colours for the two tags that had one, none for the deleted server's tag.
  - Import of a file with an added tag, `Prod` and `@Batch` (other case, and an @), a server this account lacks, and colours that agree, disagree and are missing: the dialog read “Adds 3 tags to 2 servers from valid.json. No tag is removed.” with the three notes and the two changed servers; Cancel left local storage as it was; Import added exactly those tags, the one colour from the file (`db`) and a colour for the new tag the file left uncoloured (`batch`), and the card said “Imported: added 3 tags to 2 servers.”
  - The same file again, and a file whose servers already have its tags: “Nothing to import…”, no dialog. Refused with the reason in a failure card and local storage unchanged: not JSON, another kind of file, a tag with no usable characters, a colour that is not a colour, no matching server (one server and several), no tagged server, a file over 1 MB.
  - Round trip: export, clear every tag (the card went back to “No server has a tag yet.” and Export to off), import the file: the tags and colours of the listed servers came back exactly.
  - The mock saw no request other than reads during the import checks.
  - The card and the dialog in both themes. `layoutReport` was clean at 1600×1000, 1024×672, 853×560, 683×453 (1024×680 at 150%) and 390×844 (phone), and the dialog with a 28-server, 84-tag diff at 683×453 scrolled its list with Cancel and Import still on screen.

Not tested: the browser build on a device; other Android versions and phones, and a cloud provider such as Drive in Android's Save as dialog (only Downloads was used); a write failure inside the Android save; Windows and macOS; what the operating system's save dialog does after Export (a CDP download setting wrote the file directly, so the real dialog was not seen). A file from another account has no matching server and is refused; whether a server id could ever repeat across accounts is not something the API reference says, so the help does not claim it either way.

### After review of #345 (29 October 2026)

The reviewer found two blocking problems and suggested one clarity change. All three were checked before and after the fix.

| Finding | Evidence | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Import reported success when the device did not save it.** `applyTagsImport` called `saveTags` and `saveTagColors`, which catch and drop a storage error, so the card said “Imported: added 1 tag to 1 server.” with nothing kept and no failure card. | The reviewer's injected fault, reproduced here in real Electron: `Storage.prototype.setItem` made to throw `QuotaExceededError` for the tag-map key only, then a valid import adding a tag and a colour. On the build under review the card claimed success, the stored tags did not change, and the colour had been written. | `applyTagsImport` now writes strictly, puts back any key it had changed when a write fails, tells the tag views to read again and throws, so the card shows “Could not import tags” and no success line. Seven tests in `scripts/test-tags-import.mjs` run the function the card calls against a storage that fails (tag map, colours, both, rollback failing, reads failing, empty store, a tag changed meanwhile). Putting the bug back (swallowing the failure, or no rollback) makes them fail. The first version of the rollback rewrote a key it had never changed, which also fails on a full device; the tests caught that. |
| **Export tags did nothing in the Android app.** A WebView ignores `<a download>`; the button was on and no failure showed. | The reviewer's native run. The old build was not run on a phone here. | Export now saves through a small native plugin of the app's own, `FileSavePlugin` (Android's Save as dialog, `ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT`): no storage permission, no new dependency, nothing written unless the person confirms. The desktop app and the browser build keep the download link. Run on a phone, see below. |
| **The profile was not named** (clarity, not blocking). | The reviewer showed a delayed file read after an account switch writes only to the profile it was chosen in. | The confirmation summary says `in <profile>` and the result says `Imported into <profile>`. |

Checks after the fixes, in the VM: `npm run typecheck` (guards, help guard with 186 quotes, 489 tests across the suites, 7 of them new), `npm run build` and `npm run test:terminal`. Real Electron against the mock with the reviewer's injected fault: on the build under review the card said “Imported: added 1 tag to 1 server.”, no failure card, stored tags unchanged and the colour written; on the fixed build, “Could not import tags” with “BLDesk could not save the imported tags on this device, so nothing was imported.”, no success line, and the stored tags and colours exactly as before. With storage back to normal the same file imported and the card said “Imported into <profile>: added 1 tag to 1 server.”

On a phone, with a throwaway build of the branch under its own application id (never the real app's), against the GUI mock: a Samsung SM-S948B on Android 17, driven by real touches (`adb shell input tap`) and the WebView's debugging port.

- Export tags: Android's Save as dialog opened in Downloads with `bldesk-tags-<date>.json` suggested. SAVE wrote a 658-byte file, pulled off the phone: `kind`, four servers with the seeded tags and the four colours, exactly as seeded. The card said it was saved under that name and no failure card appeared.
- Back out of the dialog: no file, no status line, no failure card, Export usable again.
- Export again with the name already taken: Android saved `<name> (1).json` and the card reported that name, not the one suggested.
- Import tags, through Android's own file picker: with every tag cleared, a real tap on Import tags opened the picker, the exported file was picked, the confirm dialog listed the four servers, a real tap on its Import tags button put back the tags and colours exactly as they were, and no error appeared.
- Afterwards the test files, the throwaway app (including its copy in the phone's second user space) and the port forwards were removed; the real app was not touched.

## Firewall: the Add Rule form, the address and port pickers, and the 100-rule limit (28 October 2026)

`FirewallManager.tsx` (the Add Rule form and the header), `FirewallRulesEditor.tsx` (the rows), the new `PickMenu.tsx` and `lib/firewallPicks.ts`, and `lib/firewallRules.ts` (`MAX_RULES`, `RULE_LIMIT_NOTE`, `rulesAllowed`, `ruleLimitNote`, the import check). `docs/help/firewall.md` has a new paragraph under Edit, reorder and remove rules and two rewritten paragraphs under Add, import and clone. The Add form had Action, Protocol, Port(s), Source CIDR and Label, with no destination, and a single-line source and ports. Every rule row, and mPanel's rule row, has source, destination, destination ports, protocol, action and description, and under each address and ports cell a magnifier with a caret that fills the cell from a list. A server at 100 rules could still open the Add form and was refused by the API (“Failed to save rule: Firewall rules are limited to 100 per server; 101 were submitted.”).

What changed that a user can see:

- The Add form has Source, Destination, Destination ports, Protocol, Action and Description, in that order (mPanel's and the table's), each address and ports field a multi-line box with a magnifier. Source and Destination start empty, with 0.0.0.0/0 as the placeholder; one value a line, commas and spaces work too. An empty source or destination is written as 0.0.0.0/0 as before. The presets put 0.0.0.0/0 in the source; the accept presets put all of this server's addresses (“Server All Addresses”) in the destination, and Drop All puts 0.0.0.0/0 there (see the review of #344 below). The form's header and presets wrap on a phone (the presets were cut off at 411 px).
- Every rule row has the same three magnifiers. A choice is added to what the cell holds, after its values and without repeating one, and is an edit like any other (the row is marked, nothing is sent until Save changes); a cell that holds the any-address keeps it (see the review of #344 below). They are off with the cell: a removed rule, a save in progress, and the ports of an ICMP rule.
- At 100 rules Add Rule is off, with its tooltip and a note under the header saying “Firewall rules are limited to 100 per server.”; for a server that already has more than 100 the note goes on “This server has 110, so its rules can be kept, edited or removed but not added to.” An import is refused before the confirmation when it has more rules than the server may have, which is 100 or what the server has now, whichever is more: “The file has 101 rules. Firewall rules are limited to 100 per server.” on a server with 100 or fewer, and the longer note after the count for one over 100 (111 over 110). A server with 110 can be given 105 or 110. Removing a rule and saving brings Add Rule back once the server is under 100. If the Add form is open when the list reaches 100, Save Rule is off and the same line shows in the form.
- The menus are placed against the window (below the button, or above it when there is more room), no wider than the screen, and have taller entries on a touch screen (36 px; the magnifier is 44 × 36).

What the menus offer, and where each entry comes from:

- Address menu, in this order: “Any address” (0.0.0.0/0), “My Other Addresses”, “My Other Public Addresses”, “My Other Private Addresses” (every other server on the account), then each address of each other server as an entry of its own (the name and the address in brackets), then “Server Public Addresses”, “Server Private Addresses”, “Server All Addresses” and each of this server's own addresses, public first. Ports menu: “80,443 (HTTP+HTTPS)”, “80 (HTTP)”, “443 (HTTPS)”, “22 (SSH)”, “25 (SMTP)”, “3389 (Remote Desktop)”, “System ports”, “User ports”, “Dynamic ports”. The entries and their order are what mPanel's rule row shows: the first version was taken from screenshots of its menus, and the order was then corrected from the markup of mPanel's own menu, which the person testing pasted (28 October). In it the other servers are in plain character order by name (AAAfieldtest, BLNMAP-ControlRoom, GS1, cPenial, gitlab-01ax, …: capitals before lower case, not a locale order), each with its public addresses and then its private one as separate entries; the cell values of the “other” choices follow the same server order (every address of every other server, the public ones, the private ones). Public and private come from `networks.v4[].type` of the public reference's `Server`; a server's `failover_ips` are not offered in any list: `publicAddressesOf` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts` reads only the public networks, so a failover address is typed in when a rule needs it (the fleet audit's `publicAddressesOf` in `lib/firewallMatrix.ts` still counts failover addresses, for what is exposed, and is not changed). An entry with no address behind it is left out; an address is listed once.
- The port ranges are mPanel's: “System ports” 0:1023, “User ports” 1024:49151 and “Dynamic ports” 49152:65535, as the person testing read them from mPanel's ports menu (28 October). 0:1023 is also the value recorded below for mPanel's suggestion of that name, from the 23 October firewall check, which found `start:end` accepted and a hyphen refused by the live API; the other two were not sent to the live API by this change's checks, but mPanel writes them.
- Not shown by mPanel's menus as seen, so chosen here and to be checked in mPanel by a read-only look (asked of the reviewer): that an entry for one address puts in exactly that address; that a new rule's Source and Destination start empty and a choice is added to a cell that holds only the any-address (this branch does both); and that no list offers a failover address (this branch offers none). That a choice is added to a populated cell is from the reviewer's read-only check of mPanel.
- mPanel's address menu also has an entry for the address the person is on, shown as the address with “(Current IP)”, straight after any address. BLDesk leaves it out: the API reference has nothing that returns the caller's address, the app has no way to learn it, and asking a third-party service would add a request to someone else's server and a host to the content security policy's `connect-src`, which allows only BinaryLane, GitHub and the update host. Raised in the pull request for a decision.
- Not live-populated, as in mPanel. The list of servers is the one BLDesk already holds; opening a magnifier does not ask BinaryLane for it. In mPanel, with a rule list open in one tab, a server created in another tab is not in the menu until the page is loaded again (reported by the person testing, who watched it). BLDesk's own server list refreshes about every 15 seconds while it is on screen (`refetchInterval: 15000` in `useServers`), so a new server appears in the menu once that has happened, without a reload, and an open menu keeps the entries it opened with (`PickMenu` takes the list as it opens). Reading the list on every open was left out as too heavy.
- The 100-rule limit is in the public reference from 0.46.0: `ChangeAdvancedFirewallRules.firewall_rules` says a server may have up to 100 rules, which together may match up to 1000 combinations of address, protocol and port, and that “a server that already exceeds either limit may keep, edit or remove its rules, but not exceed what it already has”. The vendored `openapi.json` (0.45.0) did not have this when the branch was first written and the first version said the reference did not state it; the review of #344 pointed it out, and the vendored reference and `schema.d.ts` are now 0.46.0 (the only differences from 0.45.0 are that sentence and the version; `npm run generate-api` gives `schema.d.ts` as committed). It is also stated by mPanel beside its New Rule button (“Firewall rules are limited to 100 per server.”, seen in the person's screenshot of a server at 100 rules) and by the API's own refusal, whose text is quoted above and was shown by BLDesk on a real account's server at 100 rules when a 101st was submitted. The allowance for a server over 100 is `rulesAllowed(saved)` = the larger of 100 and the rules it has now; the 1000-combination limit is not counted by BLDesk (the API refuses it, and the refusal is shown). The GUI harness mock refuses a list over that allowance (`mock.mjs`; the refusal text for a server that already has more than 100 is the mock's own, the live wording was not seen), and `/__mock/firewall` seeds a server that has more than 100, which a write cannot make.

Help sentences, and what each was checked against:

| Sentence | Checked against |
| --- | --- |
| Under each source, destination and destination ports cell is a magnifier with a caret; it opens a list and the choice is added to the cell (after what is there, not repeating a value; a cell that holds the any-address keeps it), as an edit | `PickMenu` (`onPick`), `withChoice` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts`, its use in `FirewallRulesEditor.tsx` (`patch(i, { sources: withChoice(row.sources, c.values) })`) and in `FirewallManager.tsx` (`setRuleSource((held) => withChoice(held, c.values))`) |
| The lists, their entries and order | `addressChoicesFor` and `PORT_CHOICES` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts`; every quoted entry is a literal there (the help guard checks it) |
| An entry with no address behind it is left out; failover addresses count as public; other servers' addresses are entries of their own, public before private, the servers in plain character order by name | `add()`, `publicAddressesOf` and `privateAddressesOf`, `others.forEach` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts` |
| Escape or a click elsewhere closes a list; arrow keys, Home, End and Enter work in it | the key and mouse handlers in `PickMenu.tsx` |
| Opening a magnifier does not ask BinaryLane again; a server created after BLDesk last read its list is not in the menu; BLDesk reads the list about every 15 seconds; an open list does not change | `PickMenu` (`shown` set on open, no query), `refetchInterval: 15000` in `useServers` (`api/queries.ts`); `fw-add.mjs` section F |
| mPanel's menu works the same way (a server created in another tab is not in it until the page is loaded again) | reported by the person testing; not something BLDesk's code can show |
| A magnifier is off when its cell is, and for the ports of an ICMP rule | `disabled={off}` and `disabled={off || row.protocol === 'icmp'}` in `FirewallRulesEditor.tsx` |
| Add Rule opens a form with Source, Destination, Destination ports, Protocol, Action and Description, a magnifier under each address and ports field | the form in `FirewallManager.tsx` (`fw-add-*` fields) |
| Source and Destination start as 0.0.0.0/0; one value a line, commas and spaces work; a blank one is written as 0.0.0.0/0 | `useState('0.0.0.0/0')`, `splitList` and `toRuleRequest` in `handleAddRule`; the source's own fallback to `['0.0.0.0/0']` |
| A TCP or UDP rule needs ports; ports are off for ICMP and ALL | `handleAddRule` (“Destination port(s) are required for TCP/UDP rules.”), `disabled` on the ports box |
| The presets fill the form in, ports and description included, put 0.0.0.0/0 in Source and all of this server's addresses in Destination, the same as “Server All Addresses”; 0.0.0.0/0 when they are not known | `applyPreset` (`presetDestination`, from `ownAddressesOf` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts`, which also gives the menu's “Server All Addresses”) |
| Save Rule asks for one confirmation and writes the whole list with the new rule in its place | `handleAddRule` (`insertRule`, `confirmAction`, `updateFirewall.mutateAsync`) |
| At 100 rules Add Rule is off with the note beside it (a longer note for a server over 100); an import of more than the server may have is refused before the confirmation (more than 100, or more than a server over 100 has now); removing a rule and saving brings Add Rule back | `atRuleLimit`, `limitNote` in `FirewallManager.tsx`; `rulesAllowed`, `ruleLimitNote` and `readImportedRules` in `lib/firewallRules.ts`; `fw-add.mjs` section A, `fw-add-review.mjs` section G |

How it was checked, in real Electron against the GUI harness mock (fictitious data), every write read from the mock's own request log:

- `scripts/test-firewall-picks.mjs`: 11 tests (the entries and their order, this server against the others, public against private, every address of another server as an entry of its own, the servers in plain character order by name with capitals first and ties in id order, a server with no public address or whose networks were not read, all of a server's addresses for the presets, failover and duplicate addresses, IPv4 only, the ports and their ranges, the limit and the import over it). Twenty-one deliberate faults (this server counted among the others; private read as public; a server with only its private address as an entry, or its private address before its public ones; any address as a bare 0.0.0.0, or missing; two port ranges; no de-duplication; the other-private list holding public addresses; this server's private address left out; all of the server's addresses with the private ones first, or only the public ones; the servers in locale order, with ties out of id order, or not sorted; all addresses as every public one then every private one; empty entries kept; a limit of 101; an import of exactly 100 refused; the note changed) were each caught. Run read-only against the real account's 14 servers (the person testing pasted the markup of mPanel's own menu for a server on it), `addressChoicesFor` gives the same 36 labels in the same order as that menu, apart from mPanel's Current IP entry.
- 108 steps in `fw-add.mjs` (97 before the review of #344, and 11 for the adding of a choice, below). The limit: 99 rules, the 100th added through the form (confirmed like any other, one write of 100), Add Rule then off with its note and tooltip and a forced click doing nothing, an import of 101 refused with the limit and nothing sent, an import of exactly 100 going on to its confirmation, and Add Rule back after one rule is removed and saved. The form: the fields in the order of mPanel and the table and side by side on a wide window; a rule with two sources, a destination and two ports written as typed; a blank destination written as any; ports still accepted comma separated; a bad destination, a bad source and a UDP rule with no ports refused with nothing confirmed or sent; the presets (each fills Server All Addresses in the destination, the same as the menu's entry, and a WireGuard rule saved from one is written to all of the server's addresses); ICMP and ALL switching the ports and their magnifier off. The menus, checked against the mock's own server list (28 servers there): the 65 entries and their order, every address of every other server as an entry of its own, every address entry and every ports entry putting in exactly what it should, a choice going into its own field and leaving the other alone, the cell growing to a line a value, Escape, a click elsewhere, the focus, arrow keys, Home, End and Enter (Enter on a menu that has just opened takes any address, the first entry), one menu at a time, and a rule built from the menus written as chosen. On the rows: three magnifiers a rule, named for the rule and the field; a choice is an edit; two edited rules in one write with the others byte for byte as returned; an ICMP rule's ports magnifier off; a removed rule's off. The list not being read again: six openings cost no read, a server made after the page loaded is not in the menu, it is after BLDesk's own refresh, and an open menu does not change when the list is refreshed behind it. The placement: inside the window below the button, upward on the last rule at the bottom of the window, no wider than a 411 px screen, entries 36 px and the magnifier 44 × 36 with a coarse pointer, a mouse press on a magnifier not arming the rule's drag, and a held finger on one not lifting the rule (and on the rule's number it does).
- The earlier suites on the new build: 59 desktop (`fw-edit.mjs`), 30 touch, 12 edge-scroll, 48 review. The touch suite's hold in a text field first failed: each rule is taller now, and its fixed touch points landed on the phone's bottom bar and left the table; the suite scrolls the rule into view before touching it.
- 1024×680 and 1280×840 windows at 80, 100, 125 and 150% by the app's own Ctrl+- and Ctrl+=, with the Add form open and a magnifier menu open (`fw-zoom-add.mjs`): no horizontal scroll, no field cut off or overlapping, the narrowest field 150 px, Save Rule on screen and the menu inside the window at every size; the existing table check (`fw-zoom.mjs`) is clean as well.
- `npm run typecheck` (every guard, the new tests, the help guard) and `npm run build` pass in the VM.
- Not run: a real phone (the pointer was emulated with Chromium touch emulation, and the menu was not tried on Android's WebView), Windows and macOS (renderer code only), the 49152:65535 and 1024:49151 ranges and the 100-rule refusal against the live API by the automated checks (the 100-rule text was seen from the API by the person testing), and a comparison of the single-address entries with mPanel (that a choice is added to a cell was read from mPanel by the reviewer).

### The review of #344 (28 October 2026)

Two blocking findings from the review (Freewheelin, on `ccbacd9`), both reproduced first and then fixed.

- **An import was refused above 100 whatever the server has.** The reference (0.46.0) lets a server that already has more than 100 rules keep, edit or remove them but not exceed what it has, so reducing 110 to 105 is valid and the first version refused it before the confirmation. `readImportedRules(parsed, saved)` now allows `rulesAllowed(saved)` rules (the larger of 100 and what the server has now); `FirewallManager.tsx` passes the rules the server has. Add Rule stays off at 100 or more (one more would exceed what the server has), and its note is the plain one up to 100 and the longer one above it. The help paragraph, the mock, its tests and the statement that the reference does not give the limit were changed with it (above). Not changed: the 1000-combination limit is left to the API, as the review said was acceptable; Clone and the matrix copy a list to other servers and have no client check, as before.
- **Android Back skipped an open magnifier list.** `PickMenu.tsx` sets `role="menu"` and `data-dismissible="true"` and closes on Escape, but the Back handler in `App.tsx` looked only for `[role="dialog"][data-dismissible="true"]`. The selector is now `DISMISSIBLE_SELECTOR` in `androidBack.ts`, `[role="dialog"][data-dismissible="true"], [role="menu"][data-dismissible="true"]`, and `App.tsx` uses it; the menu keeps its role (it is not turned into a dialog), and a dialog that has to be answered (`data-dismissible="false"`) is still not matched. `scripts/check-ui-guards.mjs` now fails on an element with `data-dismissible` whose role is not a string literal or is not one of the roles that selector lists, and on `App.tsx` not using the constant; five deliberate faults (the menu role taken out of the selector, a `listbox` popup, a computed role, no role, `App.tsx` going back to its own selector) were each caught.

What was run, in the VM against the mock:

- `npm run typecheck` (every guard and unit test) passes; `scripts/test-firewall-picks.mjs` was 12 tests before the adding of a choice (the new one covers a server over 100: 105 and 110 over 110, 111 refused with the longer note, 101 over 101, the plain note at 100); the mock's own tests are 31 (the new one seeds 110 rules and checks 110, 105 accepted, 106 over 105 refused, and 101 refused again at 100). The picks and rules faults were 27 at that point (the earlier 21, one replaced, and seven new: the import over 100 refused whatever the server has, which is the finding; the allowance ignoring what the server has, the lesser of the two, only what the server has; exactly the allowance refused; the longer note at exactly 100; the plain note for a server over 100); all 27 were caught (34 with the adding of a choice, below).
- `fw-add-review.mjs` in real Electron, 31 checks, every write read back from the mock's request log. Server with 110 rules: Add Rule off with the longer note as text and tooltip; 111 refused with the reason and nothing sent; 110 over 110 and 105 over 110 go on to the confirmation (cancelling 110 sends nothing); 105 is written once and accepted; Add Rule stays off at 105 and the note counts 105; 106 over 105 is refused (the allowance is what the server has now); an edit of one rule on the 105-rule server is written and accepted; at 100 the plain note, 101 refused with it, and Add Rule back at 99. Back: with a rule's magnifier open, the app's old selector finds nothing (the reported fault) and the selector read from the built bundle finds the list and Escape closes it, with Firewall still shown, focus back on the magnifier, nothing edited or sent; with an Add form half filled (source, ports, description typed) and its destination list open, Back closes the list and keeps the three values; a ports list closes the same way; with nothing open Back finds nothing, so it goes on to change screen as before; an open dialog is still found and closed. The same script against the build of `ccbacd9` (with the new mock) passes 12 of 31: the 105-over-110 import is refused there and the selector has no menu entry.
- The earlier suites on the fixed build: `fw-add.mjs` 97, `fw-edit.mjs` 59 (58 on a first run that came after the other suites in the same app, which had left more History entries than its “one entry” check allows; 59 on a fresh app), `fw-review.mjs` 48, `fw-touch.mjs` 30, `fw-edge.mjs` 12.
- The longer note on a server with 110 rules, 1280 px wide, 683 px (a 1024 window at 150%) and 411 px: one line on the desktop, two lines under the header on the narrower widths, no horizontal scroll.
- On the phone (a Samsung SM-S948B, Android 16, 411 × 891 CSS px at 3.5, the debug app of each commit installed under a throwaway application id with the fictitious mock token, so the person's own BLDesk was not touched; real taps with `adb shell input tap`, and Back as `adb shell input keyevent KEYCODE_BACK`, which is the path the hardware and gesture Back take). The fixed build (`0ebf353`'s code) passes 15 of 15. With a rule's source list open, one Back closes it, Firewall stays on screen, the rule is not edited, the focus is on the magnifier and the app stays in front.
