With the Add Rule form half filled (source `192.0.2.77`, ports `8443`, description “keep me”) and its destination list open, one Back closes the list and the three values are still there; a second Back, with nothing open, goes to Servers as before. A rule's ports list closes the same way, and the Import dialog is still closed by Back and leaves Firewall on screen. The build of `ccbacd9` (the reviewed head), run the same way, reproduces the finding: with a rule's list open one Back goes to Servers, and with the half-filled form and its destination list open one Back goes to Servers and returning to Firewall shows the form gone. The second scenario was run on its own for that build, because the first one had already left Firewall.
- Not run: Windows and macOS (the renderer code is the same, but Back is Android only), and Back as the Android gesture rather than the key event.
- **A choice is now added to the cell, as mPanel does** (the reviewer's read-only check of mPanel found that it adds a choice to a populated address or ports cell; the first version of this branch replaced the cell, which is not parity). `withChoice(current, values)` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts`, used by all six magnifiers (the three on the Add form and the three on every rule): the choice goes after the values already in the cell, in order, and a value the cell already has (the same text) is not added again; what was typed is not reordered, and commas and spaces in a cell are written back one to a line. The any-address is a value like any other: a cell that holds only 0.0.0.0/0 keeps it and the choice is added after it, and Any address chosen for a cell that has other addresses adds 0.0.0.0/0 after them, so the rule then matches every address. A second round on this: the first form of the change replaced a cell that held only 0.0.0.0/0, and started the Add form with 0.0.0.0/0 in Source and Destination so that a new rule did not stay matching every address, as the reviewer noted. Following a further read of how mPanel's lists behave, the Add form now starts with Source and Destination empty (the placeholder still shows 0.0.0.0/0, and an empty one is written as 0.0.0.0/0, shown in the confirmation's diff, as before), which removes the trap without a special case, and the choice is added plainly; the presets still set Source and Destination directly. The same round stopped offering a server's failover addresses in any list (`publicAddressesOf` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts` reads only the public networks): “Server Public Addresses”, “Server All Addresses”, “Server Address: …”, the other servers' entries and the presets' destination leave them out, and one is typed in if a rule needs it; whether a rule's destination matches a failover address was not tested here. Run: `scripts/test-firewall-picks.mjs` is 14 tests (two for adding a choice, the any-address as a value, and the failover test now checks that a failover address is in no entry, of this server or of another, and that a server with only a failover address has no public entry); the picks and rules faults are 34, each caught (among them: the choice replacing the cell, a repeated value added again, the choice put first, a cell holding only the any-address replaced, the any-address dropped from a cell, a failover address counted as public, private addresses counted as public). `fw-add.mjs` is 110 steps: each entry of both menus is still tried on an emptied field, the mock's server with a failover address (192.0.2.150) is in no list, and new steps check that a fresh form starts with Source and Destination empty and the placeholder 0.0.0.0/0, that an empty cell takes a choice, that a second choice is added after it and Server All Addresses then adds nothing, that Any address goes after the others and a choice after that is added to the cell that has it, that a cell holding only 0.0.0.0/0 keeps it and has the choice after it, that a typed address keeps its place, that ports are added to (22, then 80 and 443, then 443 again changes nothing) and the any-address is only text in a ports cell, and that Any address on a lone any-address stays one line; on the rules, rule 1's source (192.0.2.25/32) had Server Public Addresses added after it and rule 2's ports (443) had User ports added after them, saved as one write with the other rules unchanged; on the last rule, whose destination is the any-address, the last entry went after it. The other suites pass on the same build, each on a fresh app: `fw-edit.mjs` 59, `fw-review.mjs` 48, `fw-touch.mjs` 30, `fw-edge.mjs` 12, `fw-add-review.mjs` 31. The help paragraphs, `PickMenu`'s comment and the pull request description say the same. The phone run above was of the commit before this change, which touches what goes into a cell and which addresses are offered, not Back or placement, so it was not re-run there.
- **Drop All is for any destination (the reviewer's third finding, on `3705897`).** Every preset had put all of the server's addresses in Destination, as “Server All Addresses”, and the list no longer offers a failover address, so Drop All did not name an assigned failover address: the reviewer reproduced it in the real Add form on the multi-IP fixture (the request had a `drop` / `all` rule for the NIC addresses and not for 192.0.2.150) and the app's own `exposureLabel` then said `none` for the NIC addresses and `all` for the failover address. On `main` the presets named no destination, which is written as 0.0.0.0/0, so Drop All was for any address; this branch had changed that. Drop All is now source 0.0.0.0/0, destination 0.0.0.0/0, every protocol, no ports (“Default Drop Inbound”), which needs no list of addresses and covers a failover address or one added later. The presets are one function, `presetRule(preset, server)` in `lib/firewallPicks.ts`, that the form uses, so a test can check what each preset writes and not what the picker lists. The accept presets still name the server's own addresses, the same as “Server All Addresses”, so a failover address is typed in when an accept rule should cover it (the help says so); with Drop All last, an accept preset does not open a failover address by accident. Run: `scripts/test-firewall-picks.mjs` is 16 tests (two new: Drop All reaches the NIC addresses, the failover address and an address the server does not have yet, and, through the production `exposureLabel` on a server with a failover address, the rule it writes leaves `none` exposed, while the same rule for only the picker's addresses does not, which is the reviewer's case as a control; and every accept preset's rule, with the failover address not reached and any address when the server's addresses are not known); four more deliberate faults (Drop All for the server's addresses only, an accept preset that reaches the failover address, an accept preset for any destination, Drop All for TCP only) were each caught, 38 in all. `fw-add.mjs` is 116 steps: Drop All puts 0.0.0.0/0 in the destination, drops every protocol and has no ports, and a new section runs the presets on the mock's server with a failover address (192.0.2.150, not among its networks): Drop All saved from the form writes one `drop` / `all` rule for source and destination 0.0.0.0/0, an accept preset names the server's own four addresses and not the failover one, is written so, and is placed ahead of the drop-all rule. The other suites pass on the same build, each on a fresh app: `fw-edit.mjs` 59, `fw-review.mjs` 48, `fw-touch.mjs` 30, `fw-edge.mjs` 12, `fw-add-review.mjs` 31. Not tested: a failover packet on a live account (failover IPs are not available for a test, and the public API has no endpoint for one).

## Backup download HTTP refusals (28 October 2026, #343 follow-up)

Checked the new download guidance in `docs/help/backups.md` against `useImageDownloadMutation` in `api/queries.ts`, `apiFailure` in `api/errors.ts`, and `handleDownload` in `BackupManager.tsx`. An empty JSON-labelled HTTP failure previously reached the caller as an absent link and showed the missing-URL error. Download now checks `response.ok` as well as `error`, preserving the API reason or HTTP-status fallback. Successful link selection and the genuine successful-response-without-a-URL fallback are unchanged. Download is a read-only link request and creates no History entry.

Renamed the actual-hook suite to `scripts/test-backup-mutations.mjs`: its six existing Take Backup tests remain, with four Download tests added. They cover empty JSON-labelled HTTP 400/403/429/500/504, a structured field reason, an unchanged successful link with one correctly targeted GET and no cache invalidations, and successful responses without a link. The empty-error Download regression fails without the guard and passes with it. `npm run typecheck` passes both TypeScript projects and guards (396 tests pass, nine platform skips); `test:backup-slots` passes 43/43, the mock suite 30/30, and `test:terminal` and `build` pass.

Real Electron 44.9.3 with isolated API fixtures: empty JSON-labelled HTTP 400 and 504 and a structured 400 show the correct failure card after one GET, without opening a URL or changing images or History. Successful compressed and raw-only links select the expected URL; a successful response without a URL retains the genuine missing-URL error. The URL-opening call was intercepted, not an external download transfer. Failure text wraps and its dismiss button works in both themes at 1024x680 and 1280x840 with actual 80%, 125% and 150% Electron zoom (12 layouts); representative captures were inspected. No new live API, Android or native-install coverage is claimed. The earlier Take Backup, palette and Change Plan GUI evidence below remains evidence for those unchanged paths, not a fresh run of them.

The default mock Download response was also corrected from a bare link string to the public `ImageDownloadResponse` / `ImageDownload` / `ImageDiskDownload` shape, including the requested image ID, expiry and disk URLs. Its new regression validates the actual default response against the referenced schemas, checks image and disk identities including a multi-disk fixture, and leaves backups and actions unchanged; it fails on the old string response. The mock suite now passes 31/31. Through the real Electron Download buttons, two images return a schema-valid default response after one GET each and select its compressed URL without a failure card, History entry, cloud write or backup/action change. No response routes were overridden. Only URL opening was stubbed, so no external transfer was made. This follow-up changes the mock and its tests, not application runtime behaviour; earlier injected-response and layout evidence retains its original scope.

## Firewall rules: edit in place, move by drag or hold, save explicitly (27 October 2026)

`FirewallManager.tsx` (Server mode of the Firewall area, and the server's Firewall Rules tab, which renders the same component), the new `FirewallRulesEditor.tsx` and `lib/firewallDraft.ts`. A server's rules were rows that could only be moved one place with the arrows, deleted, or added to, and a move was written to BinaryLane at once with a History entry and no confirmation. Rows are now editable in place, and every change is held until Save changes. The Add form, Import, Clone, the Fleet matrix and the API calls are unchanged: a save is the same `change_advanced_firewall_rules` action with the whole list, as every earlier write was.

What changed that a user can see:

- Each rule is a row with editable source, destination, destination ports, protocol, action and description, move arrows and a remove button, and the whole row can be dragged. A phone shows each rule as a card, label on the left and field on the right.
- Edits, moves and removals wait in a bar headed “Unsaved changes” with “Discard” and “Save changes”. Save changes shows the existing confirmation with the whole list's diff and writes once, with one History entry. A save that fails keeps the edits.
- Reordering no longer writes at once. This was the only change to the list that did not ask first.
- A rule is dragged by any part of it that is not a field, dropdown or button. A mouse drag is placed by where the pointer is in the list, not by which rule it is over, and the place is shown as the space between rules it will go into (above the first or below the last included): a bar in a space that opens from 8 to 28 px, so the neighbours part around it. No rule is highlighted. A row is made draggable only while the mouse is down outside a control, because a draggable row would take over selecting text in its fields.
- On a touch screen a rule lifts only after a finger holds still on it, outside its fields and buttons, for 0.4 seconds (the phone vibrates, where it can). A finger that moves first is scrolling. A rule carried near the top or bottom of the part of the page that can be seen scrolls the list: slowly at first, faster the longer it is held there (260 px a second rising to at most 6000, by time and not by frame, so a 120 Hz phone is no quicker), and faster nearer the edge. Not before the finger has moved. "The part that can be seen" leaves out anything fixed over the list, found by looking at what is on top, because a phone's bottom navigation bar covers the lower part of the page. The same loop serves a mouse drag, and the whole scrolling area, not only the list, accepts the drop, so letting go below the last rule or above the first puts the rule there.

Decisions, so a reviewer can disagree with them:

- Unsaved changes are kept in memory, one set per server, rather than warning when you leave the screen. That needs no hook into the app's navigation and loses nothing; nothing is ever applied by itself. Closing BLDesk drops them. The server picker marks a server that has some.
- Add Rule, Import, Clone, Export and Disable Firewall are off while changes wait, because each reads or writes a list built without them. If the server's rules change while edits wait, Save changes is withdrawn and the edits can only be discarded, so the old rules are never written back over the change. What is on screen can be old, and nothing reads it again by itself (the list is cached for 20 seconds, with no polling and no read on focus), so Save changes reads the server's rules itself: before the confirmation, so that it shows the list that will be replaced, and again once it is confirmed, because a change can be made while the confirmation is open. If a read fails, or says the rules are not the ones the edits began from, nothing is sent, the reason is shown and the edits stay on screen (when the rules had changed the bar then says so). A refusal after the confirmation is recorded in History as failed, with the detail “Not sent”. This narrows the window rather than closing it: the API has no revision to write against, so a change between the last read and the write is still not caught. The server picker is off while a save is under way, because the write is made for whichever server is chosen when it goes out.
- A cell keeps what is typed even when it says what the rule already says: a line break typed before the next address, or a space before the next word. A first version dropped the draft whenever nothing differed from the server, so the separator was deleted as it was typed (“Operations SSH”, a space, then “updated” gave “Operations SSHupdated”); the reviewer found this in Electron and in Android's WebView. The draft is now kept while it was typed against the rules the server still has (`draftApplies`), the bar and Save follow only real changes, and layout is trimmed when the rule is written.
- A save that BinaryLane accepts and then reports as failed puts the edits back on screen as unsaved changes. The rules are as they were, so the edits are still valid against them; before, the table went back to the old rules and the edits were gone, unlike a write that is refused at once, which always kept them. A save whose outcome is not known (the action tracker lost track of it) is not put back, because it may have landed, and the rules are read again.
- A cleared source or destination is refused, not read as 0.0.0.0/0, because an address emptied by accident would open the rule to everyone. Unlike the Add form, which fills in 0.0.0.0/0 for a blank source, an edit is more likely to be a slip.
- A rule that was not edited is written exactly as BinaryLane returned it, so a rule made elsewhere that BLDesk would not have written (ports on “ALL”, say) is not rebuilt or refused. Only edited rows are checked.
- After Save changes the list shows the saved rules at once. BinaryLane applies the write when its action completes, and a read before then gave back the old list, which made the table look as though the save was lost. The action tracker's read when the action completes still decides what is shown.

Help sentences, and what each was checked against:

| Sentence | Checked against |
| --- | --- |
| “Edited”, “Removed”, “Undo”, “Unsaved changes”, “Discard”, “Save changes” | the strings in `FirewallRulesEditor.tsx` and the bar in `FirewallManager.tsx` |
| Rows change source, destination, ports, protocol, action and description; one value to a line, commas and spaces too | the cells in `FirewallRulesEditor.tsx`; `splitList` in `lib/firewallDraft.ts` |
| The bar says how many rules were edited, how many removed, and whether the order changed | `describePending` and `pendingChanges` in `lib/firewallDraft.ts` |
| Drag by any part that is not a field, dropdown or button, or use the arrows; a bar marks the space it will go into, and that space opens; hold such a part on a touch screen until the rule lifts; a finger that moves first scrolls; dragging to an edge scrolls the list | `isControl`, the row's mouse and drag handlers, `gap`; `dropGapAt`, `edgePull` and `edgeScrollSpeed` in `lib/firewallDraft.ts`; `startTouch`, `followFinger` and `visibleBand`; `ROW_HOLD_MS`, `holdMovedTooFar` |
| Save changes: one confirmation with the whole list, one History entry; saving with every rule removed is confirmed by typing the server's name | `handleSaveDraft` in `FirewallManager.tsx` (`confirmAction`, severity `irreversible` for an empty list) |
| Save changes reads the server's rules before the confirmation and again once it is confirmed; if a read fails or the rules are not the ones the edits began from, nothing is sent and the edits stay; “Couldn't save the firewall changes” | `rulesUnchanged` and `handleSaveDraft` in `FirewallManager.tsx`; `fetchFirewallRules` in `api/queries.ts` |
| If the write fails, or BinaryLane accepts it and then reports that it failed, the changes are back on screen as unsaved changes | `notifyFailure` without clearing the draft in `handleSaveDraft`; the `sentDrafts` effect on the tracker's `errored` state in `FirewallManager.tsx` |
| What cannot be saved: empty source or destination, a non-IPv4 address, TCP or UDP ports cleared or missing after a switch, ICMP with ports, a description over 250 characters | `problemsOf` in `lib/firewallDraft.ts`; the 250 limit is `maxLength` of `AdvancedFirewallRuleRequest.description` in `openapi.json` |
| Changes kept while BLDesk is open, per server; the server picker marks one; closing BLDesk drops them | the module-level `unsavedDrafts` and the picker option in `FirewallManager.tsx` |
| Add Rule, Import, Clone, Export and Disable Firewall are off while changes wait | `canEdit` includes `!pending`; Clone and Export have `|| pending` |
| “This server's rules changed after you started editing. Discard your changes to load the current rules, then make them again.” | the `stale` bar in `FirewallManager.tsx`, which shows no Save button |
| The list shows the saved rules straight away after Save changes | `useShowFirewallRules` in `api/queries.ts`; the action tracker's `invalidateQueries` for `change_advanced_firewall_rules` |
| Cells stay usable while the list is read again, but Add Rule, Import, Clone and Disable Firewall do not | `rowsEditable` has no `isFetching`; `canEdit` does |

How it was checked, in real Electron against the GUI harness mock (fictitious data), with every write read back from the mock's own request log (an earlier version of the check watched the page's request events, saw nothing, and passed its "nothing was sent" steps without observing anything; the log shows a save as exactly one write, which is the positive control):

- A long list: 100 rules, with the phone's geometry reproduced (a bottom bar taller than the page's padding, covering the lower part of the scrolling area), a finger resting 40 px above the bar, which the first version's edge zone (the last 64 px of the scrolling area's own box, mostly under the bar) did not reach and which never scrolled on the real phone. Now the first rule is carried to the bottom in about 9 seconds, the speed rising from about 600 to 6300 px a second, and the last rule back to the top in about 9; a mouse drag crosses a 100-rule desktop list in under 4 seconds with the same ramp. Letting go below the last rule moves the rule there. The simulated mouse had to keep moving a pixel, as a hand does, because Playwright's drag only reports the pointer's position when it moves.
- 59 steps on desktop: editing holds changes and sends nothing; a cleared address is refused and Save is off; arrows, native drag and drop, remove and undo change only the draft; a mouse press arms a drag on the rule's padding and number but not on a text field, a multi-line field, a dropdown or a button, and dragging across text in a field still selects it; during a drag the space above the first rule, or between two rules, is marked and opens to 28 px with no rule highlighted, and letting go there moves the rule; edits survive leaving the screen; Discard restores the saved rules; Save sends one write of the whole list with untouched rules byte for byte as returned and one History entry; a failed write keeps the edits and a retry goes through; a rule list changed underneath a pending edit withdraws Save; removing every rule needs the server's name typed; a server with no rules and the Add form behave as before; the server's own Firewall Rules tab is the same table.
- 30 steps with Chromium touch emulation at 411 px (real touch events, `pointerType` touch): hold then drag lifts, buzzes, marks the landing place and drops there without scrolling the page; a finger that moves first lifts nothing while the page scrolls (shown by the page having scrolled); a still hold lifts, on a label as well as the number but not in a text field; the place it will go is the space between rules, opened up; a tap, a hold released in place and a cancelled touch change nothing; a rule can be carried to the top of a long list by the edge scroll; swiping a cell still scrolls and cells still take edits. A rule held low on the screen did start scrolling by itself before the finger moved: that was found by this check and fixed. The drag test also found that the mouse drop was being refused, because a drop target must accept `dragenter` as well as `dragover` and the element under the pointer changes as the space opens; the list now accepts both.
- 1024×680 and 1280×840 windows at 80, 100, 125 and 150% by the app's own Ctrl+- and Ctrl+=, with the table in its busiest state (an edited row, a removed row, a validation message, the bar): no horizontal scroll, nothing cut off or overlapping, the last row reachable, and the confirmation's buttons on screen at every size. The table layout is used from 1280 CSS pixels; below that a rule is a card.
- 48 steps in `fw-review.mjs`, written from the review's three blocking findings and its non-blocking one. Typing: a space after the last word, a line break in the source cell and a second address on the next line are kept, and the write carries both addresses; a keystroke that changes only layout shows no bar and sends nothing. Save: the server's rules changed from another client after the edits began, with the table still showing the old source (the control), opens no confirmation, writes nothing, shows why and keeps the edits; a failed read does the same and a retry then goes through with exactly one write; rules changed while the confirmation is open are not written over after it is confirmed, and History records the entry as failed and not sent. Dragging: with four rules saved as three, the first rule carried below the last shows its landing place and moves there, with the mouse and with a held finger at 411 px, and the same after switching from a four-rule server to a cached two-rule one. Failure: a save the mock accepts and then fails puts the edits back, saves again, and a completed save does not bring its edits back. Run first against the commit under review (2507cdb), the script fails on each: the separator is deleted, Save reads nothing and opens its confirmation over the old list, a confirmed Save writes over a change made while it was open, a drag after the list shrank does nothing in all three variants, and the edits do not return; against this change all 48 pass. The first version of the shrink check also failed on the fixed code, because it dragged while the save's action was still being tracked, when the cells are off by design; it now waits for them to be usable and still fails on 2507cdb.
- `scripts/test-firewall-draft.mjs`: 24 tests. Twenty-nine deliberate faults put into `firewallDraft.ts` one at a time (an unedited row rebuilt, a cell left out of the edited test, ports never required, a removed row still written, a reorder not noticed, the drop position's clamp, the space a moved rule opens, the scroll zone, speed and its top speed, and others, and three in `draftApplies`: typed layout dropped, a draft kept after the rules moved, a draft with changes dropped when they moved) were each caught; one initially survived (the drop position's clamp, for a held row outside the list) and has a test now.

Real phone: the change ran as a throwaway debug build under its own app id on a Samsung phone (Android's WebView), by hand, with the installed app untouched: holding to lift a rule, the page scrolling when a finger moves first, the landing place, and the edge scroll, which first did not work there because the bottom navigation bar covers the lower part of the scrolling area (found on the phone, fixed, and reproduced in the long-list check above). That build is byte for byte the tree of this change as first opened; its history was squashed afterwards. The reviewer also ran it on Android's WebView, where holding a rule near the bottom navigation scrolled and dragged correctly, and typecheck and build on Windows. The vibration tick was not separately confirmed. The changes made after review (the reads in Save, separators, the drop position after a list shrinks, the edits coming back after a failed action) were checked in real Electron and with Chromium touch emulation, as above, not on the phone again. Windows and macOS were not run by me; the change is renderer code only. HTML5 drag and drop does not work from touch on Android, which is why touch has its own gesture.
## URL image import (27 October 2026, #308)

Checked `docs/help/backups.md` and `docs/help/server-backups.md` against `BackupManager.tsx`, `ImportImageModal.tsx`, `backupImport.ts`, `useImportImageMutation` in `queries.ts`, the shared confirmation/History/action tracker, and the public `UploadImageRequest`, `BackupSlot`, `BackupInfo.iso` and upload operation in `openapi.json`. The old statement that BLDesk cannot upload images is removed. Import downloads into backup storage; the returned image's type determines the later actions. ISO images are attach-only, not restorable or downloadable. No local-file upload or new ISO boot/install control is claimed.

- The form and confirmation use the shared Modal and `useConfirm`, with one confirmation and a History entry. The default is a free slot without replacement; oldest/newest/specified are explicit choices. A fresh backup list and server read check retention, ownership and known locked/attached state before confirmation. A failed read or invalid choice sends no import. Automatic replacement names only a current candidate, not a guarantee of which image the API will select later. The server and draft stay pinned while reads are pending.
- The HTTP/HTTPS URL and optional label (250 characters) follow `UploadImageRequest`. Specified sends `backup_id_to_replace` and the replaced image's matching `backup_type` from the fresh list; other strategies send the chosen `backup_type`. The separate live upload check in the [27 October peer review](https://github.com/termau/bldesk/pull/341#issuecomment-6433373885) reports rejection of missing or mismatched specified types and acceptance of the matching type; this is dated upload evidence, not an inference from the earlier Take Backup check. The label is a separate confirmation row, not a promised returned image name. The URL sent is the same parsed canonical URL used for display. Credentials, query strings and fragments are omitted from the source row and its History copy, not from the request. Server-supplied messages and access details inside a pathname are not redacted by this feature. Temporary import lifetime is the public BackupSlot description, including the ISO exception; Take Backup's frequency limit is not extrapolated to upload.
- `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` pass. The slot suite includes 22 import checks and 11 slot/ISO checks (33 total), including canonical URL handling, matching replacement type, bounded reads, action handoff, ISO flags and lost/unreadable/5xx-response handling without retry. The GUI mock suite passes 30 checks, including import completion, replacements, ownership, protected images, invalid sources, matching upload types and failed-action preservation. The upload mock uses the action type `upload_image`, a neutral returned image name and field-level validation envelopes, following the dated peer observations above; its error reasons remain synthetic. It never downloads a URL and does not establish extra service behaviour. Cancellation is guarded explicitly by the component before the submit helper and checked through the GUI fixture, not a dead helper branch.
- Real Electron with fictitious data: invalid source, cancel, free-slot success, full-slot refusal, oldest/newest/specified, a replacement locked elsewhere, failed fresh read, HTTP refusal, repeated submit, target change during a delayed read, source-row redaction, and success without an action. A separate check executes the mock POST then loses its response: one execution, unknown outcome, form closed and History Submitted. A server GET held beyond 20 seconds releases the form with no confirmation/POST, including after its late answer. Repeated submit produces one POST. Nine distinct request bodies pass the OpenAPI checker. React confirmation and busy/target wiring remain manual GUI checks, not a new CI component harness.
- Desktop form and confirmation checked at 1024x680 and 1280x840 with actual 80%, 125% and 150% Electron zoom: close and submit controls reachable, no page overflow. The last navigation item and last table action remain reachable in all six layouts. The change table names the server independently of its header.
- Android fixture coverage: URL import, cancel, occupied slot, specified replacement, tracked completion, redacted History and an errored action preserving the original image. Narrow layouts retain reachable controls and no page overflow. Three distinct native request bodies pass the OpenAPI checker; no renderer exceptions observed.
- Final focused Electron check: the mock executes one import before an injected HTTP 504. BLDesk reports unknown outcome with the status, closes the form, keeps History Submitted and sends no retry. Cancellation sends nothing; HTTP 400 retains the form and records Failed; a subsequent successful import completes in History. Three request bodies pass the OpenAPI checker. The 5xx change is a conservative client rule, not a claim about when BinaryLane can return a gateway error. The prior native/layout matrix was not repeated for this shared result-classification change.
- Peer-review correction: the initial form inherited near-white text in light theme. Explicit Take Backup-style field and label colours now apply to all five labels and both inputs/selects, including the specified-replacement form. Real Electron before/after measurements: the minimum text contrast was 1:1 in light theme; it is now above 8:1 in both themes. Twelve actual zoom/theme layouts (the six required sizes/zooms in light and dark) keep close, submit and ISO Attach reachable with no page overflow. The `isIsoBackup` helper uses only an explicit true flag; ISO rows show ISO and Attach only, hide Download/Restore, and guard both handlers. One Attach POST and no download/restore request are observed. Ordinary non-ISO rows retain Download and their strict Restore confirmation. The bundled help states the same restriction and does not add a boot/install workflow.
- Final corrected-mock Electron check: `upload_image` completes in History without the old capture-backup banner; the returned neutral name differs from the request label; the replacement confirmation keeps those concepts separate. A 504 injected after the fake import has completed triggers fresh backup/action reads and shows its new image without navigation, with one execution, no retry and History Submitted. A ProblemDetails refusal displays the field reason, retains the form and records Failed. Four distinct request bodies validate. The earlier freshness/deadline/target guards were not changed by these corrections; their earlier manual coverage remains separate from this focused rerun.
- Corrected Android fixture rerun: native WebView fields use the intended light/dark colours; both themes retain reachable form controls across three narrow-to-wide test viewports. An imported ISO is marked and has Attach only: one attach request, no download/restore request. Detach and ordinary backup Download/Restore remain available. A synthetic native HTTP 504 leaves History Submitted, closes the form, refreshes backups/actions and sends one import request without retry. Four distinct native bodies validate; no page exceptions. This native 504 fixture refuses before executing; the after-execution case is the separate Electron test above. No live import was performed by these correction checks.
- Not exercised against a live account: remote download, image-format acceptance, source authentication/redirects, expiry timing, and server-side import failures. No restart, automatic restore or automatic attachment behaviour is claimed. Other desktop platforms, system font scaling and accessibility tooling were not checked.

## Palette: another command's review no longer shows an earlier backup's replacement warning (27 October 2026, #330)

`CommandPalette.tsx` only. No help page or other user-facing text changed.

The amber note that names the backup a take may replace was cleared only when a backup check started. After a backup review that listed a backup, the review of another command, such as a reboot of a different server, showed the note with the first server's backup under it, though Run sends only the reboot and nothing is replaced. `replaceLines` and `replaceMap` are now also cleared when the palette returns to its input stage, which every way out of a review passes through: Back, Escape, closing the palette and opening it again. A backup review starts a new check, so it still shows what its own check found.

`docs/help/palette.md` is unchanged and still true: the amber note is the one a backup review shows for targets that hold a temporary backup, and “the amber note does not appear when no target has a line”. The lifetime and limit note is not affected, because it follows the command being reviewed and not this state.

- Real Electron 44.9.3 against the GUI harness mock, `main` and this change, the same six steps each: `backup edge-web-syd-01` (occupied slot); then, palette closed and reopened, `restart api-syd-01`; the same without closing the palette (Back, then another command); `backup stopped-batch-01` (no backups); `backup edge-web-syd-01,api-syd-01`; and a `backup` review after a `restart` review. On `main` the two `restart` reviews carry the note and `edge-web-syd-01`'s backup. With this change they show only the target. The other four reviews read the same on both: the note and the backup lines where a target holds a temporary backup, none for the empty slot, one line for each of two servers, and the note again for a backup review after the reboot one.
- There is no unit test: the repository has no component test tooling, and the issue allowed a GUI check.
- Not exercised: leaving a review while its backup check is still running. The existing run counter that ignores a late result is unchanged.
## Top bar at phone widths (22 October 2026, #293)

`TitleBar.tsx` only; `UpdateMenu.tsx` is not touched, because its width changes with its state. No user-facing text changed.

The bar's right-hand group (the version or update control, the account chip with its key button) is `flex-shrink-0`, so below about 400 px nothing could give and the controls ran past the right edge. Measured again on `main` (1.0.62-beta.31), because the bar has grown since the issue's 397 px: in the harness's browser mode (the built renderer with no preload, which takes the same mobile bridge path as the Android app) the bar needed 419 px with nothing to update and 449 px with "Update v…" showing. That was with the VM's fonts, which are wider than Android's. On the Samsung SM-S948B (411 CSS px, its own fonts), `main` fits at 411 and clips below it: the account chip is cut by 5 px at 390 (update available), 21 px at 375, 36 at 360, 56 at 340 and 76 at 320, with the key button the first thing to leave the screen.

Now, below the `sm` breakpoint (640 px) the bar lets its controls drop to a second row when they cannot fit on one (`flex-wrap`, the right-hand group pushed to the right), so none is cut off whatever the update control's width or the font. The wrapped bar keeps the safe-area inset at its top (`max(0.25rem, env(safe-area-inset-top))`), and a phone with a status bar gets 4 px more at the bottom, so a single row on the SM-S948B is 73 px tall, not 69. Below 420 px the side padding and gaps tighten; below 400 px the logo icon and the shield in the account chip are hidden (the menu button stays) and the account name gets 56 px, not 85. From 420 px up the classes are the original ones, apart from the wrap. A second, separate commit shows the Ctrl+K hint in the desktop search button only from `md` up: at 640 to 648 px it pushed the Settings button 1 to 9 px past the edge.

- **A regression caught on the phone.** The first version put `py-1` on the bar, which replaced the safe-area padding at the top: on the SM-S948B the padding above the controls went from 40 px to 4 px and the bar sat under the status bar. The VM has no status bar, so only the device showed it. The padding is back (40 px, measured; the band is visible in the screenshots) and the same layout was re-measured on the phone.
- **The phone** (real WebView over DevTools, the throwaway test build installed under its own id, against the mock API): at 411 px native and at 390, 375, 360, 340 and 320 emulated, in the idle state and with "Update v…" showing (a release list patched into `fetch`), nothing is cut off and the bar is one row at every width. At 300 and 280 px it is two rows, below the status-bar band, with nothing cut off. At 411 px the bar looks as `main`'s does, with the shield and the full name; at 360 px `main` clips the chip and the fixed bar does not.
- **The VM's fonts** (browser mode, the same two states, 320 to 639 px): nothing is cut off at any width. The bar is one row from 340 px (idle) and 360 px (update) up to 399 px, and from 430 px up (480 px and up with the update); between about 400 and 430 px, and at 320, it wraps to two rows instead of clipping, because those fonts are wider than the phone's.
- **Real Electron zoom**, 1024×680 and 1280×840 windows at 80, 100, 125 and 150% by the app's own Ctrl+-, Ctrl+= and Ctrl+0 (`webContents.sendInputEvent`): the CSS width equals the window divided by the zoom, the bar stays 44 px tall, nothing is cut off and the page has no sideways scroll. At 1024×680 and 150% (683 CSS px, below `md`) the window's minimise, maximise and close buttons are hidden; that is how it was and is not changed. Electron at 600 to 1600 px, including 640 to 648 px: nothing cut off.
- `npm run typecheck` (all guards and tests) and `npm run build` pass on a clean checkout in a VM. There is no unit test: this is layout, and the checks above are the evidence.
- Not run: a phone other than the SM-S948B, other fonts and the system's large-text setting (the wrap is what covers them), Safari, and Chrome on Android as a browser (the app's own WebView was used).

## Backup refusal close-out (28 October 2026, #313)

Checked `BackupManager.tsx` / `useTakeBackupMutation`, the command palette's backup runner / `useServerActionMutation`, and `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` / `ServerDetails.tsx`, through `api/errors.ts` and the History store and view. The merged field-reason formatter already reaches all three surfaces. A remaining Take Backup defect treated an empty HTTP error body labelled `application/json` as success: openapi-fetch returned a non-ok response with no truthy `error`, so the form closed and History remained Submitted. The mutation now checks the HTTP status as well as `error`, using the existing `apiFailure` fallback; its request, success path and retry policy are unchanged. Non-JSON error responses already get a readable body from `safeNormalizeResponse` in `client.ts`; the demonstrated failure is the empty JSON-labelled response, not a new live API observation.

The six Take Backup tests in `scripts/test-backup-mutations.mjs` execute the actual mutation function with openapi-fetch and synthetic responses. Empty HTTP 400/403/429 responses reject with their status; quota and generic-summary field reasons survive. Normal success preserves its body, returned action and success invalidations; specified replacement preserves its body and returned action. Empty HTTP 202 with Content-Length 0 and HTTP 204 remain successful without a returned action; this pins the hook's existing behaviour, not the form's handling of an absent action. The empty-error regression failed before the fix and passes after it. These run in the existing `test:backup-slots` command and typecheck guards, not a new test framework. The unit fixture uses plain openapi-fetch; the GUI checks below also exercise the application's client wrapper.

Real Electron 44.9.3, isolated API fixtures: nine cases cover Take Backup, the palette and Change Plan, each with a quota message, a generic summary whose reason is only in `errors`, and an empty JSON-labelled HTTP 400. Each sends one request, shows the reason or HTTP-status fallback, records Failed with matching detail for the correct server and no action ID, and leaves the fixture's server, backups and action list unchanged. Take Backup retains the form and optional label; the GUI uses its default Temporary slot, while slot preservation was checked in the catch path (only success resets it). History store entries were checked for all nine cases, and the displayed History detail was checked for the final Change Plan refusal. Cancel sends nothing; a successful Take Backup creates one image and one action, closes the form and reaches Completed in History.

For each surface's longer generic-summary reason, checked actual Electron windows 1024x680 and 1280x840 at 80%, 125% and 150% zoom, with both light and dark theme classes applied: 36 layout cases. Text wraps within the viewport; dismiss or Done remains reachable (Playwright's trial click checks actionability, not a delivered click). Representative captures were inspected. No renderer exception occurred. HTTP 400 console errors are the injected refusals; fixture runs also logged HTTP 404 responses. Nine intercepted requests (three distinct bodies) and the successful take's distinct body validate against `openapi.json`. All 30 mock tests, full typecheck/guards and build pass. The review follow-up adds only test/help/verification changes; the executable fix and its GUI evidence are unchanged.

`docs/help/backups.md` now explains refusal displays and retained form choices; `docs/help/server-change-plan.md` explains that a `pre_action_backup` field reason identifies “Backup first”. Both were checked against those callers and the actual failure/History displays. The 26 October #322 entry below records the observed nested-field key. No shared formatter, quota, lifetime, replacement or API-schema change. No new live API or Android device check; provider timing and counting are not re-established by these fixtures.

## Field-level reasons in a failed call (22 October 2026, #318)

`describeApiError` (`src/renderer/src/api/errors.ts`) is where a failed call's body becomes the sentence the customer reads: the typed client's `apiFailure`, the paged reads, and the places in `queries.ts`, `ServerDetails.tsx` and `powerState.ts` that call it directly. It returned `message` (or `detail`) as soon as there was one, so a generic validation summary hid the reason in `errors`. #318 came from the review of #315, where a take or a Change Plan backup-first step refused for want of a free temporary slot showed the summary and not the reason. On a disposable server on 20 October 2026 the live API answered that refusal with a generic validation summary in `message` and the useful sentence ("There are no free Temporary slots.") only in `errors`.

Now the summary is shown first and the field reasons after it, as “field: reason, reason; field: reason”, unless the summary already says every reason (a body whose `message` and `errors` repeat one sentence reads as that sentence once). `detail` is the summary when there is no `message`. What `errors` may hold follows the reference's `ValidationProblemDetails` (`openapi.json`: an object from field to a list of strings); anything else in it is left out and never printed: an `errors` that is not an object, a field whose value is not a string or a list of strings, a reason that is not a string, a field with nothing readable. Such a body falls back as before, to the message, then the title, then “BinaryLane returned an error without a reason.” Before, a value that was not a string was turned into text, so `{ name: { a: 1 } }` read “name: [object Object]”, and an array read “0: x”. Fields whose reasons are exactly the same are joined into one entry (“field_a, field_b: reason”), so the same text under several fields is said once, because repeating it reads as noise. At most five entries are shown (then “and N more”), each reason on one line, and what is added is cut at 300 characters. A body with only a message, only a detail or only a title reads exactly as it did.

Running the real app showed that the first version of this change did not reach the Take Backup form, which is the surface the issue is about. Three places turned an API error into text by hand and never called `describeApiError`: `useTakeBackupMutation` (the Take Backup form's “Backup failed” card), `useImageDownloadMutation` (the Download button's card) and the firewall step of a template job (`templateJobs.ts`). Each took `message`, then `title` (the template one `e.error?.message`, a shape the API does not send), the `errors` flattened, and, as a last resort, the whole body as JSON. They now use `apiFailure` or `describeApiError` and the condition that raises the error is unchanged. Rule 7 of `scripts/check-mutation-guards.mjs` fails on a hand-rolled `JSON.stringify(error)` or `Object.values(x.errors)` anywhere in the renderer outside `api/errors.ts`; putting the original Take Backup block back, a `JSON.stringify(error)` in a query and one in the template helper each made it fail.

No help page or other user-facing text changed: the words are the API's own, and `docs/HELP_VERIFICATION.md` lines that describe a message-only body (the firewall tab's line, a rejected SSH key) are still true.

- `scripts/test-api-errors.mjs` is now 15 tests: seven added for a generic message with a field reason, several fields and reasons in the API's order, a reason the message already says, the same reason under several fields, an `errors` of the wrong shape (fifteen of them), the bounds, and a failed call through `apiFailure`; the bodies are synthetic. Putting each fault back made tests fail: the message winning outright 7, no check for a reason already said 2, any value turned into text 1, no cap on fields 1, line breaks kept 1, an array read as fields 1, no bound on the joined text 1, no “and N more” 1, no full stop after a bare summary 1, `detail` not used as the summary 3, reasons not merged across fields 1, and fields merged by name and not by their reasons 1.
- Real app, current `main` against this branch, with a refusal body injected at the network layer (Playwright route; the mock can inject a status but not a body), the same eight bodies each time. The Take Backup form's “Backup failed” card, on `main`: “Validation of the request failed.” for a generic message with a field reason, and “[object Object], 7” for `{ errors: { name: { a: 1 }, size: 7 } }`. On this branch: “Validation of the request failed. backup_type: There are no free Temporary slots.” and “BinaryLane returned an error without a reason.” The Download card, on `main`: the headline alone, and the raw body `{"errors":{"name":{"a":1},"size":7}}`; on this branch the reason, and the neutral sentence. The palette's `backup` result line and the History entry's detail show the same text as each other for all eight bodies (message only, a summary that already says the reason, detail plus errors, errors only, the wrong shape, one reason under two fields, eight fields: the first five and “and 3 more”).
- Not run: a live refusal from BinaryLane, and the template job's firewall step in a window (it shares `describeApiError` with the rest and is covered by the guard).

Android fixture coverage: the same named-replacement sequence through the native HTTP bridge, with native taps on Change Plan, Resize and dismiss, produces one resize POST and one card with the complete reason. Server, backup and action-list readbacks remain unchanged. The reason wraps within the on-screen card and native dismissal works. Its screenshot was inspected; no renderer exception occurred, and all three distinct request bodies pass `check-requests.mjs`. No production UI, request, retry or error-formatting code, help prose, or API schema changes.
