## Which rules open a port to the world (22 October 2026, #326)

The fleet audit's “open to the world” flags and the Network Map's exposure label and SSH count now read a server's rules the way BinaryLane applies them: for each of the server's public IPv4 addresses (`publicAddressesOf` in `firewallMatrix.ts`: its public networks and failover IPs), rule by rule from the first, and the first rule that matches traffic from anywhere to that address decides; a port that no rule decides is open, because BinaryLane permits traffic that matches no rule. Before, any rule with a source of `0.0.0.0/0` counted, whatever its destination or position, and a port that no accept named was never counted. `exposes` and `exposureLabel` moved from `networkMap.ts` into `firewallMatrix.ts` (the map re-exports them) so the audit and the map share one reading, and so Node can test them. `explainUnreachablePort` in `firewallMatch.ts` skips a rule that does not apply to the address the reachability badge probed.

`docs/help/firewall.md` has two new paragraphs under Rules and access, and `docs/help/map.md` two new sentences about the exposure label. The help describes how BLDesk reads the rules, not provider behaviour: the one fact about BinaryLane that the reading rests on and the reference does not state is recorded below, with how it was checked and that BinaryLane confirmed it. Each clause was checked against the code:

| Sentence | Checked against |
| --- | --- |
| “for each of the server's public IPv4 addresses … from first to last … the first rule that matches decides” | `worldPorts`, `reachablePorts` (one pass per address, rules in order) |
| “a rule that lists named sources, such as an office address, is not traffic from anywhere, so it neither opens nor closes a port” | `reachablePorts` skips a rule unless `isWorld` holds for its sources, which needs `0.0.0.0/0` in the list (or an empty list) |
| “They read a rule that names only a private address or a VPC range, or another server's address, as not applying to that traffic” | `destinationApplies`: a destination applies only if it is empty, an any-address, or contains the address |
| “a server with no public IPv4 address … has nothing open to the world” | `worldPorts` returns no ports for an empty address list |
| “a port that no rule decides counts as open … a list with no final drop leaves everything it does not mention open” | `reachablePorts` returns the ports an accept decides plus every port left undecided at the end of the list, so an empty list is every port (which `auditServer` reports as no rules) |
| “The label lists the open ports or, when naming the closed ports makes a shorter list, says all but followed by the closed ones” | `exposureLabel`: it shows the closed ports instead of the open ones when they are fewer spans (an open `1:1024 49152:65535` is shown as `all but 1025:49151`, a range written as the API writes it) |
| “A port range in a rule is read as BinaryLane writes it, `start:end`, both ends included” | `portRange`, used by `reachablePorts`, `portsInclude` (the reachability verdict) and the matrix column sort; the hyphen is read too |
| “a list that only allows SSH from an office address … SSH is still open to them” | `reachablePorts` ignores the office rule for traffic from anywhere, so 22 is undecided and open; a test asserts it |
| “The audit's flags and the SSH count look at TCP; the map's port label counts a port as open if either TCP or UDP gets through” | `auditServer` and `exposes` call `worldPorts` with `['tcp']`, `exposureLabel` with `['tcp', 'udp']` |
| “IPv6 is not read” | no IPv6 address reaches `destinationApplies` |
| “allowing ping adds nothing because ICMP has no port” | `reachablePorts` reads only `tcp` and `udp` rules, plus `all` |

Service facts, from `openapi.json`: the rules response says the order is critical, the first matching rule is applied, and traffic that matches no rule is permitted; `destination_addresses` is the “destination addresses to match for this rule” and every rule must have one; a network's `nat_target` is the private address that receives packets for a public one. The reference does not say whether a rule's destination is compared with the address a packet is sent to or with the address behind a VPC server's public address. That was checked on 22 October 2026 on throwaway servers (a VPC with several members, a server outside a VPC, a server with two public addresses, and one with no public IPv4), probing six ports from outside and from a second member of the VPC. BinaryLane confirmed it on 23 October 2026: for a server in a VPC with NAT, a rule is applied to traffic from the internet before the public address is translated to the private one, so it is matched against the public address, and against the private address only for traffic inside the VPC. The same check was repeated that day, side by side, on one NATed server in each of four regions (Sydney, Adelaide, Singapore and Brisbane) with the same result. Results, each with a listener on every port tested (a new server blocks its own outbound port 22 by default, so SSH between VPC members could not be tested):

- Accept TCP to the VPC range (or to the server's private address), then drop everything: nothing answered from the internet and the other VPC member reached the other five ports. The same on a server outside a VPC with a rule for `172.16.0.0/12`.
- Accept TCP 22 and 80 to the server's public address, then drop everything: 22 and 80 answered from the internet, and the other VPC member reached nothing.
- Drop all TCP to the VPC range, then accept everything: the internet reached every port, including 22, and the other VPC member reached none. Drop TCP 22 to the public address, then accept everything: 22 was closed and the rest open.
- Drop TCP 22 for any destination, then accept TCP to the VPC range: 22 closed, every other port open from the internet (a port no rule matches is permitted).
- No final drop, each probed from outside: a list that is only an accept of TCP to the VPC range left every port open; the same accept followed by a drop of TCP 22 closed 22 and nothing else; a list that is only a drop of TCP 22 and a drop of TCP 3389 closed 22 and left the other ports open; a list that only accepts TCP 80 and 443 left 22 open too; on a server with two public addresses, a list that only accepts TCP 22 to the first left every port open on both.
- A bare `0.0.0.0` as the source or the destination of a drop of SSH, followed by an accept of everything, closed nothing (22 stayed open): it is the single address 0.0.0.0, not any address, so only `0.0.0.0/0` is read as any. The API refuses `*`, `any`, `::/0` and `::` as addresses (a 400), so a stored rule cannot hold them; earlier code treated all of these as any, which was harmless on an accept and hid a warning on a drop.
- A port range is written `start:end`: `8000:9000` was stored and returned as written, and dropping it closed 8000, 8080, 8081 and 9000 (both ends) and left 7999 and 9999 alone; `0:1023` (the “System ports” suggestion in mPanel) closed 22, 80, 443 and 1023 and left 1024 alone; accepting `20:30` and then dropping everything left 22 open. The API refused `8000-9000`, `*`, `all`, and a range whose end is not larger than its start with a 400, and accepted port `0`. Earlier code read only a hyphen, so no range in a real rule list was seen: an accept of `20:30` followed by a drop-all showed SSH closed.
- With two public addresses, accepting SSH on one and then dropping everything left the other address dropped, and dropping SSH on one and then accepting everything left SSH open on the other.
- A server whose only IPv4 is its VPC address has no public address to reach.
- With `drop all` as the only rule, IPv6 was still reachable on every port: the external firewall does not filter IPv6, as the help already says.

Not checked: failover IPs (they are included as public addresses, which can only add exposure, but no server here had one), and a source range wider than a /32 but narrower than `0.0.0.0/0`, which is still not counted as “the world”, as before. The first version of this change read a port that no rule decides as closed, as the audit always had, and a review (#327) showed that this removed warnings the old reading had given, for lists whose only accepts were for the VPC; the live results above for lists with no final drop are the check of that. One map label changes as a side effect of reading rules by protocol: a rule that allows ping from anywhere used to make the label `all`, and ICMP, which has no port, no longer adds one. Checked in the GUI harness on fixture servers, `main` (48ac71d) against this branch, with the same rules written to each through the mock's own API. On a server whose only accept is for the VPC range and whose list ends in a drop, `main` flagged eleven ports as open to the world and this branch flags none; on a server with no public IPv4, `main` flagged SSH and a database and this branch flags nothing. On the lists with no final drop that were tried this branch loses no warning that BinaryLane's behaviour supports (a drop of SSH ahead of an accept of SSH correctly closes it, where `main` flagged it): the accept for the VPC range alone still shows all eleven flags, the same accept followed by a drop of TCP 22 shows ten (SSH is closed, which `main` got wrong), and a list that only drops SSH and RDP shows nine flags and the label `all` where `main` showed none. A server that accepts SSH for any destination, or for the first of three public addresses, is still flagged. The SSH counts go from 7 to 3 in the matrix and from 8 to 4 on the map. A card whose label is `all but 22 3389` fits its footer beside the address, and the detail panel reads “all ports except 22 3389”. Not run: the packaged app and Android.

## Palette temporary-backup terms (23 October 2026, #313)

`CommandPalette.tsx` now renders the existing `TEMPORARY_BACKUP_TERMS` in its backup review, independently of the replacement list. The same constant is already used by Take Backup and Change Plan; its 48-hour lifetime and two-in-seven-days limit come from `BackupSlot` and Take Backup in `openapi.json` 0.45.0. `docs/help/palette.md` quotes the complete string and distinguishes it from the separate replacement warning.

| Claim | Decided by |
| --- | --- |
| The backup review shows the lifetime and limit even when no backup might be replaced | The `parsed?.kind === 'backup'` condition inside the review, outside `replaceLines.length > 0` |
| Other commands do not show the backup terms | The same condition; the review is shown only for a valid plan |
| Reviewing or returning to input takes no backup | The existing `primary`/Back paths; only `execute` calls `plan.run` |
| Run still takes a temporary backup with oldest replacement and the supplied label | The unchanged backup action body in the multi-server command handler |

Checked in the real Electron app against the GUI fixtures: an empty slot, an existing temporary image, and a multi-server command each show one copy of the terms; the existing replacement warning remains conditional. A power review shows no backup terms. No action POST occurs before Run or after Back; Run sends exactly one `take_backup` request with `backup_type: temporary`, `replacement_strategy: oldest` and the supplied label. At actual window sizes 1024x680 and 1280x840 and zoom 80%, 125% and 150%, the note wraps without horizontal overflow and Run/Back remain reachable; native window captures were inspected.

The [23 October live API check](https://github.com/termau/bldesk/issues/322#issuecomment-6353444081) establishes the ordinary-take/replacement path separately: with scheduled backups disabled, an initial temporary take and an oldest replacement completed (HTTP 200); a third oldest-replacement attempt returned HTTP 400, with `errors.backup_type` containing "Temporary backup limit of 2 per 7 day period has been reached." No action was returned or appeared in the subsequent action lists, and the second image remained unchanged. This is direct API evidence, not a check of BLDesk's failure card. Resize pre-action counting and elapsed expiry/reset timing were not tested. Shared validation-error presentation remains with #318/#325; this change does not claim those items complete or close #313/#322.

## Servers with no scheduled backups (21 October 2026, #314)

`BackupManager.tsx` shows `NO_SCHEDULED_BACKUPS_WARNING` under the banner when the server's options keep no daily, weekly or monthly backups (the banner's Disabled state), and `NO_SCHEDULED_BACKUPS_EMPTY` in the empty state of such a server. The banner's Disabled text now also says offsite backups are set in Change Plan (`offsite_backups` is a resize option in the reference, and Change Plan's Backups section sets it). `docs/help/backups.md` quotes all three.

The public reference does not say that BinaryLane keeps backups only on a backup schedule. BinaryLane confirmed it on 21 October 2026, and the warning is worded around the one exception, a backup taken by hand, whose 48 hours is the reference's (see the next entry). The warning keys on the server's options, so a server holding only a temporary backup still shows it, and a server with daily, weekly or monthly backups does not. #314 asked for the empty-state line to state no lifetime; the reference now gives one, so it does.

Checked in the GUI harness on a server with no backups (the warning and the empty-state line show) and one with daily, weekly and monthly backups (neither shows). Not checked against a live account.

Change Plan's hint on a server whose only free slot is temporary no longer says "replace a named backup instead" when there is no backup to replace.

## Temporary backup terms (21 October 2026, API reference 0.45.0)

`openapi.json` is now the live 0.45.0 reference (it was 0.43.3; descriptions only, see the commit). Two sentences in it are new: the `temporary` row of BackupSlot reads “A backup which is created on demand and only retained for a maximum of 48 hours (seven days for an uploaded .iso image)” (it said seven days), and Take Backup reads “At most two temporary backups may be taken of a server in any seven day period.” `BACKUP_SLOT_LABELS.temporary` now says “Temporary (kept up to 48 hours)”, and `TEMPORARY_BACKUP_TERMS` is shown under the slot when Temporary is chosen in `BackupManager.tsx` (Take Backup) and in `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` (Backup first). `docs/help/backups.md` and `docs/help/server-change-plan.md` quote them.

At the time of this 21 October update, the limit and refusal had not been checked against a live account, the reference did not say whether a pre-action backup or a replacement counts, and the command palette had no limit note. See the 23 October entry above for the subsequent ordinary-take check and palette note. Pre-action counting and failure presentation remain separate work (#322 and #318/#325). The GUI mock refuses a third temporary backup in seven days (`temporaryLimit`) and does not expire them.

## Temporary backup live checks (23 October 2026, #322)

The 21 October temporary-backup entry above records the status at that date. This entry updates the ordinary-take and pre-action checks; the palette note is separate in #329 and shared validation-error presentation remains with #318/#325.

Two direct API sequences used Ubuntu 26.04 with daily, weekly and monthly retention zero and offsite backups disabled. Neither sequence exercised a real BLDesk failure card:

| Sequence | Results |
| --- | --- |
| Ordinary take (`temporary`/`none`), ordinary replacement (`temporary`/`oldest`), another ordinary replacement | First two requests returned HTTP 200 and completed. The third returned HTTP 400 without a returned action or a new action in subsequent history. One temporary image remained unchanged. This was [reported on #322](https://github.com/termau/bldesk/issues/322#issuecomment-6353444081). |
| Ordinary take (`temporary`/`none`), RAM resize 1024 to 2048 MB with `pre_action_backup` (`temporary`/`oldest`), another ordinary take (`temporary`/`oldest`) | Take and resize returned HTTP 200 and completed. Resize replaced the first image with one available temporary image and changed only memory. The next take returned HTTP 400 without a returned action or a new action in immediate or later history; the pre-action image and memory remained unchanged. |

Both refusals contained `type: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7231#section-6.5.1`, `title: One or more validation errors occurred`, `status: 400`, `errors.backup_type: [Temporary backup limit of 2 per 7 day period has been reached.]`, and `message: backup_type: Temporary backup limit of 2 per 7 day period has been reached.` This is observed request validation, not an accepted action that later failed; no conclusion is drawn about internal queue implementation.

The new paragraphs in `backups.md` and `server-change-plan.md` describe the dated sequence rather than claim a new OpenAPI guarantee. The reference supplies the lifetime and allowance; `BackupManager.tsx` and `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` render `TEMPORARY_BACKUP_TERMS`, and the latter supplies the temporary pre-action backup. `lib/backupSlots.ts` supplies the shared text and slot/replacement choices. Their controls and quotes were not changed.

The mock already counted a successful pre-action temporary backup. Its counting is unchanged. A regression now covers the ordinary-take, resize/pre-action replacement, refused-next-take sequence, asserting that a refusal adds no action and changes neither the held image nor the server. At this stage, only the ordinary `take_backup` quota response reproduced the observed validation body. Resize still used the simplified mock response because its live error shape had not been checked here. See the 26 October entry above for the subsequent peer observation and mock update.

Real Electron, isolated GUI fixtures with that observed body: the palette's backup result line and the Take Backup form's "Backup failed" card both displayed `backup_type: Temporary backup limit of 2 per 7 day period has been reached.` Each request returned HTTP 400 and added no action. At an actual 1024x680 window and 150% Electron zoom, the form's reason wrapped and Cancel remained reachable; native captures were inspected. No renderer exception occurred (the expected HTTP 400 was logged to the console). All four distinct request bodies in the fixture log passed `check-requests.mjs`. This case already has the specific reason in `message`; it does not test a generic message with a useful reason only in `errors`, or replace #325's verification. The 25 mock tests, full typecheck/guards and build pass.

Elapsed 48-hour expiry and seven-day reset, failed-take counting, other replacement strategies, a live resize refusal after exhaustion, and Android were not tested in this change. See the 26 October entry above for the later resize-refusal observation and Android fixture check; expiry/reset timing and failed-take accounting remain unchecked. No provider behaviour is inferred for those cases. No production request, retry or error-formatting code changed here; #330's stale palette warning is unrelated.

## Notification settings (20 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.28)

Branch: `feat/notification-settings`. No new runtime dependencies. One help page added (`settings`), one changed (`tray`); `docs/DEEP_LINKS.md`, `docs/HELP_SPEC.md` and `FEATURES.md` each gained a line.

Before this change, desktop notifications could be muted only from the tray menu's Settings submenu, and only the kinds `server-state`, `action` and `balance`. Anything sent without a kind (the confirmations after a backup, firewall, DNS, VPC, load balancer, SSH-key or network change) was never gated, and the updater's “is ready” notification called Electron's `Notification` directly, so neither could be muted. Android and the browser build had no gate at all. All notifications now pass one gate (`notificationAllowed` in `src/shared/notifications.ts`), and the new Settings view edits the same switches the tray menu does.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `settings.md`, “Show notifications”, “Notify me about”, “Choose kinds”, “Done” | `SettingsView.tsx`: the master `Setting` label, the “Notify me about” row label and `Modal` title, the button, the dialog footer button | New. |
| `settings.md`, “Server state changes”, “Action results”, “Billing”, “Changes you make”, “BLDesk updates”, and the sentence after each | `CATEGORIES` in `SettingsView.tsx` (the labels and descriptions are the same strings the dialog shows) | New. |
| “a server appears, changes state or is no longer listed” | `fleetWatch.ts`: `kind: 'server-state'` for “appeared”, “is now …” and “is gone” | New. |
| “a server action is requested, finishes, fails, or pauses to ask you a question” | `kind: 'action'` in `ActionTrackerContext.tsx` (“completed”, “failed”, “needs an answer”, and the server-is-off or still-running verdict), and “Action requested successfully” / “Action initiated successfully” in `ServerList.tsx` and `ServerDetails.tsx` | Changed in `ServerDetails.tsx`: its notice had no kind, so it was a “Changes you make” notification while the same notice from the server list was an action one. It is now `action` in both. |
| “prepaid credit runs low, or an invoice payment fails” | `fleetWatch.ts`: `kind: 'balance'` for “Prepaid credit is running low” and “An invoice payment failed” | New. |
| “confirmations after you change something in BLDesk, such as starting a backup, editing firewall rules or adding an SSH key, and the one-time note that BLDesk is still running in the tray” | The `sendNotification` calls with no kind in `BackupManager`, `FirewallManager`, `SshKeysManager`, `LoadBalancerManager`, `DnsManager`, `VpcManager` and `ServerNetwork`; `onHiddenToTray` in `tray.ts` | New. |
| “a new version has finished downloading and is ready to install” | `UpdaterManager.notifyReady` in `updater.ts` (“BLDesk <version> is ready”), now sent as `kind: 'update'` | Changed: it bypassed the settings before. Clicking it now also brings the window back, as the other notifications do. |
| “Action progress, failures and History inside BLDesk are not affected” | The gate is `TrayManager.notify` and the bridge's `sendNotification`; in-app action cards, `notifyFailure` and History never call either | New. |
| “Nothing here is saved to your BinaryLane account” | `SettingsView.tsx` and `lib/traySettings.ts` import no API client and make no request; the values go to `tray.json` (desktop), or to app preferences and, when those cannot be written, localStorage (Android and the browser build) | New. |
| `tray.md`, the tray menu's Settings submenu has “Show notifications” and one switch per kind | `NOTIFY_MENU_LABEL` and the menu template in `tray.ts` (main-process text, so not curly-quoted) | Changed: the three existing labels are unchanged; two kinds and the master switch were added, and the kinds are disabled while the master switch is off (checked on the live menu, see below). |
| `tray.md`, “close-to-tray and launch at login only from the tray menu” | The `tray:setSetting` handler in `index.ts` accepts only `NOTIFICATION_SETTING_KEYS` | New. |
| Gear button in the title bar | `TitleBar.tsx`: `title="Settings"`, left of the window controls | New. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guard scripts and the new `test:notification-settings`, 8 tests) and `npm run build`. Removing the master-switch check from `notificationAllowed` made the master-switch test fail, and restoring it made it pass.
- Real Electron against the GUI mock with isolated user data (nothing sent to BinaryLane), run with `--no-sandbox` because the unpacked Electron had no SUID helper, driven over the debugging port, with the session's `org.freedesktop.Notifications` `Notify` calls watched on DBus:
  - Master switch off: five notifications sent from the page (one of each kind, one with no kind) reached the OS zero times, `tray.json` showed `notificationsEnabled: false` with the five kind switches unchanged, and the dialog's switches were disabled with the explanatory line.
  - Master on, “Changes you make” off: two notifications of that kind (explicit and no kind) reached the OS zero times; one each of `action`, `server-state`, `balance` and `update` all reached it. The summary read “4 of 5 kinds are on.”, survived a reload, and read “All 5 kinds are on.” after switching it back.
  - The `tray:setSetting` handler refused `launchAtLogin`, `closeToTray`, a non-boolean value and `__proto__`; a change made with it reached `onTraySettingsChanged` listeners as `[false, true]`.
  - The command palette's `go settings` opened the page; Escape, the close button, Done and a backdrop click each closed the dialog; none left a dialog in the DOM.
  - Tray menu, read and clicked over DBus (the app's StatusNotifierItem `com.canonical.dbusmenu`, same build, GNOME): Settings lists “Keep running in tray when window is closed”, then Show notifications, the three original labels unchanged, “Notify about changes you make in BLDesk”, “Notify when a BLDesk update is ready”, and “All settings…”. With Show notifications unchecked, the five kinds read disabled and kept their checks. A click on Show notifications wrote `notificationsEnabled: false` to `tray.json`; “All settings…” from the Servers tab opened Settings; with Settings open, a tray click moved its master switch and summary (“Muted: Show notifications is off.”, “All 5 kinds are on.”, “4 of 5 kinds are on.”) and, with the dialog open, the dialog's switch, none of it needing a reload. The menu was rendered by a shell extension in a screenshot of the installed 1.0.62-beta.25, which shows the old three-checkbox menu; the new one was not screenshotted, only read over DBus.
  - Gear button visible and no layout issues reported at 1024×680 and 1280×840 at 80%, 125% and 150% (emulated viewports; see the harness README), in the dark theme. The page and the dialog were also looked at in the light theme, at one size (1920×1200).

Not tested: the tray menu on Windows and macOS (it was checked over DBus on Linux only), and Windows and macOS generally; the Android and browser bridge on a device after the fix below (the reviewer ran the first version on a tablet; the fixed one was run only as a bundle against fakes); a real update download for the updater's notification; whether another desktop environment shows the notification at all.

### After review (20 October 2026)

A maintainer reviewed the first commit (`02a9389`) and found two defects in the Android and browser adapter, `mobile-bridge.ts`. Both were reproduced before anything was changed.

1. **Overlapping saves lost a change.** Each `setTraySetting` read the stored settings, changed one switch and wrote them all back, so two calls made together could read the same starting value and overwrite each other. The reviewer saw Action results stay on in all 10 runs on a tablet.
2. **A failed write could report master-muted while notifications still went out.** The write fell back to localStorage when the app preferences refused it, but the read used localStorage only when the preferences read *threw*, not when it found nothing. The answer said muted, the next read said on, and nothing told the user the choice was not kept.

The settings are now kept by `createNotificationSettingsStore` in `src/shared/notifications.ts`: the settings in force are held in memory and are what the gate and the screen read; changes apply in call order and saves run one at a time, each writing everything changed so far; every save carries a version one higher than any copy seen, and a load takes the newest copy from either store, so an older copy left in the localStorage fallback can never outrank a newer one (see the second review, below); a change that could not be saved anywhere still applies and the call rejects with “BLDesk could not save this setting on the device. It applies until BLDesk is closed, then goes back to what was saved.”, which `useNotificationSettings` shows as a failure card before it reads back the setting in force. That sentence is shown by `notifyFailure` in `lib/traySettings.ts` and is the only new user-facing text; no help page quotes it. The desktop is unchanged: `TrayManager` already keeps its settings in memory.

- `npm run typecheck` (guards and `test:notification-settings`, 18 tests at this point; 21 after the second review below) and `npm run build`. Putting each original flaw back into the store made the matching tests fail: reading storage again on every change failed 5 tests, reading the fallback only when the primary read throws failed 3, and reporting a failed save as success failed 2.
- The real `mobile-bridge.ts`, bundled with esbuild against stand-ins for the Capacitor modules (a Preferences stand-in that answers after a pause and can be made to refuse writes, a localStorage stand-in, and a captured `Notification` constructor), driven through `window.bldeskApi`, with a new bundle for each simulated launch. The original bridge (`02a9389`) lost a switch in 10 of 10 overlapping-save runs, and after a refused write it still let notifications through, before and after a simulated restart. The fixed bridge kept both switches in memory, in storage and after a simulated restart in 10 of 10 runs; showed nothing after a refused write, before and after a restart, and nothing when both stores fail; and reported that nothing was saved when both failed. The checks that did not depend on the fix (a kind switched off mutes only that kind, no kind is “general”, `launchAtLogin`, `closeToTray` and `__proto__` are refused, launch at login and close to tray read as off) passed on both.
- This is not a device-storage failure or an OS-delivery test, and the overlapping-save case was not re-run on a tablet.

### Phone widths (20 October 2026)

Found while preparing a device test, not in review: the gear in the title bar did not fit on a phone. `TitleBar.tsx` lays the bar out in one row with no wrapping, and the page container hides overflow, so anything past the right edge cannot be reached. Measured in real Electron with emulated viewports, scroll position reset, with the gear hidden by a style rule against shown:

| width | bar overflow without the gear (the layout before this feature) | with the gear |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 360 px | 44 px | 78 px, gear at 410–438 (off screen) |
| 375 px | 29 px | 63 px, gear off screen |
| 412 px | 0 px | 26 px, gear at 410–438 (off screen) |
| 450 px and wider | 0 px | 0 px, gear on screen |

So on a phone at 412 px the Settings page could not be opened from the title bar at all, and at 360 and 375 px the bar was already 44 and 29 px too wide (the end of the profile pill is clipped; unchanged by this feature, and not fixed here).

The gear is now hidden below 640 px (`sm`), so a phone's title bar is exactly what it was, and the mobile drawer (the “More” menu) has a “Settings” entry at the end of its list (`mobileMenuItems` in `Sidebar.tsx`; `settings.md` says so). Checked at 360, 375, 412, 450, 480, 639, 640, 700, 767, 768 and 1024 px: the bar's overflow is the same as without the gear at every width below 640 px (44, 29, 0, 0, 0, 0), the gear is on screen from 640 px up, the drawer lists Settings after Embedded SSH, tapping it opens the page and closes the drawer, and `layoutReport` is clean at 412 px with the page and with the dialog open. Looked at in screenshots at 412 px.

### On a real phone (20 October 2026)

A Samsung Galaxy (SM-S948B, Android 16, SDK 36). A debug build of `0abbc5f` made in a throwaway worktree with four test-only edits (the API address pointed at the fake API through `adb reverse`, the CSP and a cleartext rule for `127.0.0.1`, and the application id `com.termau.bldesk.notiftest`, so it installed beside the real app, which was not touched). The WebView reported 411×891 CSS px at 3.5× density, platform `android`. Taps were real touch events (`adb shell input tap` at coordinates worked out from the page), the WebView was read over DevTools, and what the native Preferences held was read from the test app's own `shared_prefs/CapacitorStorage.xml` with `run-as`. Nothing was sent to BinaryLane.

- **Layout.** The title bar fits at 411 px with no gear, the “More” drawer lists “Settings” after Embedded SSH, touching it opens the page and closes the drawer, and “Choose kinds” opens the dialog. The unfixed bar (gear shown) would have put the gear at x 410–438 here.
- **Saved across a restart.** Each switch touched on the phone showed up in the native file (`notifyGeneral: false`, then `notificationsEnabled: false`). After a force-stop and relaunch the page read “Muted: Show notifications is off.” with the master switch off and “Changes you make” still off. The master switch, set off and then back on, survived a force-stop and relaunch both times.
- **Overlapping saves on native Preferences** (the review's case, Action results and Billing together): 10 of 10 runs left both muted in memory and, read 1.5 s later, in the native file; 5 rounds of three overlapping changes, each followed by a pause and a force-stop and relaunch (10 restarts), all read back correctly.
- **A write is not on disk when the call returns.** `Preferences.set` resolves before Android writes the file (it uses `SharedPreferences.apply()`). Read at once after the save, the file was stale in 20 of 20 runs and correct 1.5 s later in 20 of 20; a force-stop straight after a save lost it in 6 of 6 rounds, and a force-stop 2.5 s after lost it in 0 of 6. An earlier run of the persistence checks that read the file or killed the app straight away failed for this reason, not because of the store. A hard kill within about a second of a tap can therefore lose that tap. This applies to every setting kept in Preferences (the update channel and the active profile too), and is not changed here.
- **The gate on the device.** The Android WebView has no `Notification` object (`typeof Notification` is `undefined`), so BLDesk shows no notification on Android whatever the switches say, and sending one does not throw. With a stand-in constructor that counts calls (as in the review): all on, 6 of 6 sent; master off, 0 of 6; “Changes you make” off, 0 of 2 of that kind and 4 of 4 of the others.

Not tested on the phone: a real storage failure and the “could not save” message (covered with fake storage above), delivery of an Android notification (there is none to deliver), other Android versions, tablets, landscape, and the browser build in a real browser.

### Saving on desktop, and the Android line (20 October 2026)

Two follow-ups agreed after the review. On desktop, `writeSettings` in `tray.ts` used to log a failed write to `tray.json` and say nothing, so a change could look saved and be gone after a restart. `setSetting` now returns whether it saved. A change made on the Settings page that could not be saved rejects with the same sentence the Android bridge uses (“BLDesk could not save this setting on the device. It applies until BLDesk is closed, then goes back to what was saved.”, `NOTIFICATION_SETTING_UNSAVED` in `shared/notifications.ts`), and the page shows it as a failure card titled “Couldn't save the notification setting” (`useNotificationSettings`). A change made from the tray menu has no caller to reject to, so the main process marks the `tray:settingsChanged` event `unsaved` and `useTraySaveFailures`, mounted in `MainDashboard`, shows the card “Couldn't save the setting” wherever the user is. Either way the change applies in memory until BLDesk is closed. No help page quotes the long sentence; `settings.md` says in plain words that a failure card appears and the change applies until BLDesk is closed.

`settings.md` also says “On Android BLDesk does not show notifications at the moment, so these switches have no effect there yet.” Checked on the phone: `typeof Notification` is `undefined` in the Android WebView, and `sendNotification` in `mobile-bridge.ts` builds one only when `'Notification' in window && Notification.permission === 'granted'`.

- `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build`.
- Real Electron with a folder where `tray.json` should be, so every save failed (the app started normally, with one read warning). From the Settings page: the call rejected with the sentence above; master off was in force (`getTraySettings`); three notifications of different kinds sent while it was unsaved reached the OS zero times (DBus); a click on the switch showed the card, and the switch showed the setting in force. From the tray menu (clicked over DBus): the card “Couldn't save the setting” appeared and the change was in force. After the folder was removed, the next change wrote `tray.json` with the earlier unsaved change (server state off) as well as the new one (billing off) and raised no new card. The failure card was looked at in a screenshot.
- Not tested: a real full disk or read-only file system, and Windows and macOS.

### After the second review (20 October 2026)

The maintainer rechecked `fee0593`, confirmed both original findings fixed, and reproduced one narrower case. `save` wrote the primary and then tried to clear the localStorage fallback copy, rewriting it if clearing failed; if both failed it swallowed the error and reported success. A load preferred a readable fallback, so with the primary write succeeding and the older fallback copy neither removable nor writable, the next launch restored the older value without any warning. The reviewer's order: master on is saved to the fallback while the primary refuses; the primary recovers but every write to the fallback fails; master off is saved; the call reports success; after a restart master reads on. This was my own rule, “a fallback copy exists only while the primary was failing, so it is the newer one”, and it only holds if clearing it always works. I had noted that edge when writing it and judged it unlikely, which was wrong to leave.

Reproduced first, through the real `mobile-bridge.ts` bundled against fake Capacitor modules (the same harness as above): on `fee0593` the call reports success and a restart reads master on. The rule is replaced rather than patched. Every saved value is now `{ v, settings }` with a version one higher than any copy seen at load; a load reads both stores and takes the highest version (a tie goes to the app preferences copy); `save` writes the primary and, only if that fails, the fallback. Nothing is removed or rewritten afterwards, so an older copy can sit untouchable in either store and never wins. A bare settings object saved before versions existed counts as version 0. `SettingsStorage.remove` is no longer needed and is gone from the interface and the bridge. No user-facing text changed.

- `scripts/test-notification-settings.mjs` is now 21 tests: the review's exact case, versions rising across restarts, a newer fallback copy beating an older primary one and a later primary save beating the fallback, the unversioned-copy rule, and the tie rule replace the two tests that described clearing the fallback. Putting each failure back made the matching tests fail: the old "fallback wins whenever it exists" rule failed 3, no version increase 2, no version restored on load 2, an unversioned copy counted as newest 1, a failed primary write reported as saved 2, and a tie going to the fallback 1 (after the tie test was added).
- The real bridge, rebuilt from the fixed source: the review's case now reads master off after the restart, and the 13 earlier bridge checks all pass (overlapping saves, refused writes, both stores failing, and the gate).
- Not re-run on the phone or on Windows after this change. The stored text on the native side gains a version number; the storage calls are unchanged.

### After the third review: a version that cannot tie with a copy that could not be read (22 October 2026)

The third review of #320 (`c4222f9`) found, and called non-blocking, one more recovery case in the Android and browser settings store. `stored` treats a store that cannot be read as holding nothing, so the next version was counted from the readable store alone. If the unreadable store held a newer copy, a save could tie with it or fall below it, and once that store recovered its older copy won, with no warning. The reviewer's order: master on is saved to the app preferences (version 1); the preferences refuse writes and master on goes to localStorage (2); the preferences work again and master on is saved to them (3); a restart during an outage of the preferences, which can then be neither read nor written, saves master off to localStorage as 3 and reports success; after the preferences recover both copies are version 3, the tie goes to the preferences, and master reads on. A tie rule alone would not cover an unread copy with a higher version.

Reproduced first, through the real `mobile-bridge.ts` bundled with esbuild against fake Capacitor modules (a Preferences stand-in that answers after a pause and can be made to refuse reads or writes, a localStorage stand-in with the same switches, and a `Notification` stand-in that counts what reaches it; a restart is a fresh import of the bundle): on `main` the review's order ends with master on and a notification reaching the constructor, and in the mirror image, where the unreadable store is localStorage and holds the newer copy, the later change is lost. Those are 3 of 10 checks; the other seven (the outage session itself, overlapping saves, a refused write read back from localStorage, both stores failing, and the gate) pass on `main`, and all ten pass on the fix.

A save's version is now the larger of one more than any copy seen and the time in milliseconds. While the clock moves on between saves, a save made while a store could not be read is later than anything that store holds, and the time says so, where a counter could not know. The store takes the clock as an optional fourth argument (`Date.now` by default) so the tests can move it. A copy saved by the earlier counter (version 1, 2, 3) is outranked by any new save and still loads on its own; a version is still a whole number, so the stored text keeps its shape. No user-facing text changed, and the storage calls are the same.

- `scripts/test-notification-settings.mjs` is now 27 tests: the review's case step by step, the same on a clock that moves on by only a millisecond between saves, the mirror case, a version above every copy seen and no lower than the clock (two saves in one millisecond differ; a readable copy from the future is outranked), the real clock as the default, and the limit below on a clock that does not move (that test pins an outcome, so it guards nothing). The test that expected exact counters now checks that each save outranks the one before. Putting each fault back made tests fail: counting versions as before 5, the time alone without the copies seen 1, no increase within a millisecond 1, ignoring the clock handed in 4, and a load that does not take the highest version 1.
- Limits. A copy that could not be read is outranked only while the clock moves on between saves. If the device clock is set back by more than the time between two saves, a save can still fall below it. If saves come faster than the clock ticks (the same millisecond, or a clock that does not move), the counter runs ahead of the time and a save can tie with it: on a fixed clock the review's order saves the primary at t, the fallback at t+1 and the primary at t+2, and the save made during the outage is t+2 as well, so after recovery the tie goes to the primary and its older copy wins. The review of this change (27 October 2026) found that case. `main` fails it too, and with the clock moving on by one millisecond between saves the review's order comes out right; there is a test for each. Nothing can bring back a choice that is held only in the store that could not be read: that session starts from the other store's copy, and what it saves is written on top of it.
- Not run on a phone or on Windows: the change is inside the store, the storage calls are unchanged, and the outage needs a store that fails, which the stand-ins make controllable and a device does not.

## Temporary backup availability and Change Plan (19-20 October 2026, #312)

Checked `docs/help/server-change-plan.md` against `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`, `freeBackupSlots` and `replaceableBackups`; checked `docs/help/backups.md` against `BackupManager.tsx` and `replacedByOldest`. Current retention permits a backup type but does not prove free capacity. Named replacements exclude locked, attached and unknown-type images, and an invalidated selection cannot submit.

Live CLI checks on a disposable Ubuntu server on 19 October observed one temporary slot with no scheduled retention: an empty-slot take succeeded, another `none` was refused, and explicit replacement succeeded. A memory increase and return using temporary/specified pre-action backups succeeded; occupied temporary/none was refused without a resize. These are bounded observations, not a temporary-capacity guarantee in the public reference. The independent review on 20 October reports the same occupancy/refusal behaviour.

Earlier Electron checks covered empty/full slots, scheduled fallback, loading/failed reads and Retry, Cancel versus confirmed payloads, and both required window sizes at 80%, 125% and 150%. Physical Android landscape checks on 19 October used an isolated app and fictitious data; portrait was not checked. Those checks predate the final named-replacement filtering change and are not represented as fresh coverage of it.

Retention warnings/disabled-type handling and the mock-completion timing fix have been removed from this PR for separate review. No assertion that the reported rejection fix is deployed remains here.

Fresh 20 October checks of the narrowed code passed: 10 slot-helper and 19 mock cases, typecheck/all guards and build. Native Electron checks at 1024x680 and 1280x840, each at 80%, 125% and 150%, verified visible Cancel/Resize controls and six cancellations without a resize. A confirmed temporary/specified resize, locked/attached picker exclusions, failed-read Retry and scheduled fallback passed; actual fixture request bodies passed schema validation. Native screenshots were inspected. No renderer exceptions occurred. Test processes were stopped; no new Android or live-account check is claimed.

## Change Plan backup retention (20 October 2026)

Checked `docs/help/server-change-plan.md` against `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`, `retainedBackupTypes`, `reducedBackupSlots`, the picker/selection guards, typed resize body and confirmation. Scheduled types being disabled cannot be selected as free or named pre-action backups; positive retained counts remain usable when there is current capacity. Any retention reduction adds the quoted deletion warning and destructive severity, preserving stronger reinstall/address-release confirmation.

The vendored Resize reference describes a pre-action backup before other changes and weekly-zero deletion. BinaryLane confirmed on 19 October that positive reductions trim oldest-first and retention trimming can delete locked or attached images; replacement skips those images. The independent PR #315 review reports a 20 October live daily 2-to-1 reduction deleting the locked oldest image. These confirmations are presented for maintainer review, not as extra tests by this author or explicit statements newly found in OpenAPI.

The reported rejection fix does not establish its deployment: the same independent review reports live daily/none plus daily-zero acceptance followed by deletion. Guidance therefore requires a type that remains enabled so the backup can be kept, without predicting rejection versus acceptance. The mock is unchanged and is not evidence of deployed retention behaviour. Temporary-slot occupancy and protected named-replacement filtering remain in separate PR #315; the mock-timing fix is separate too.

Fresh verification: all 9 retention-helper and 17 unchanged mock tests passed, as did typecheck/all guards and build. Native Electron checks at 1024x680 and 1280x840, each at 80%, 125% and 150%, verified the warning fitted and confirmation controls stayed visible. Daily/weekly/monthly named choices were cleared and blocked when disabled, a positive daily reduction retained the free choice and showed the deletion warning, and seven cancellations sent no resize. One confirmed temporary/specified request with scheduled counts zero passed schema validation. Screenshots were inspected and no renderer exceptions occurred. Test processes were stopped. No new Android or live-account test is claimed.

## README install instructions (13 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.25)

Branch: `docs/readme-install-instructions`. Only `README.md` changed (a new Install section, and the Node prerequisite under Getting Started); no in-app text and no help page changed.

Tested on Ubuntu 26.04 (amd64):

- **Release `.deb`.** `sudo apt install ./BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-linux-amd64.deb` installed it. `linux/after-install.sh` and `linux/after-remove.sh` were read: they add and remove the `/usr/bin/bldesk` alternatives link and `/etc/apparmor.d/bldesk`, and neither touches the home folder. apt printed `Download is performed unsandboxed as root as file ... couldn't be accessed by user '_apt'` when the file was in a folder `_apt` cannot read (twice, the second time under the home folder); the install completed both times.
- **From source.** With Node v22.23.3 from nodejs.org (checksum verified): `npm ci` (528 packages, no engine warnings), `npm run typecheck` (exit 0) and `npm run build:linux` (writes `release/BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-linux-amd64.deb` and `release/BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-linux-x86_64.AppImage`; `build.directories.output` in `package.json` is `release`). The built `.deb`'s `postinst` and `postrm` are identical to the published release's. After `sudo apt remove bldesk`, `sudo apt install ./release/BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-linux-amd64.deb` installed it; `/opt/BLDesk/bldesk.bin` is byte-identical to `release/linux-unpacked/bldesk.bin`. Launched headless with the Chromium sandbox on and a throwaway data directory, it started its zygote and network service processes with no sandbox error. No window was looked at.
- **Ubuntu's apt Node.** `nodejs` 22.22.1 from apt reports `process.config.variables.node_use_amaro` as `false`. `node --experimental-strip-types` on a one-line `.ts` file, `scripts/test-api-errors.mjs` (inside `npm run typecheck`) and `scripts/write-notices.mjs` (the last step of `npm run build`) all stop with `ERR_NO_TYPESCRIPT`. Node 22.23.3 from nodejs.org runs all three.
- **Compiler.** `node_modules/node-pty/prebuilds` holds darwin-arm64, darwin-x64, win32-arm64 and win32-x64 only; on Linux `npm ci` compiled `build/Release/pty.node`. `g++` and `make` came from `build-essential`; Python 3 was already installed, and the build was not tried without it, so `python3` is listed in the README's `apt` command rather than assumed.
- **Node 22.** `node-version: 22` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `release.yml` and `android.yml`. The old README said "v20+ recommended"; Node 20 was not tested.
- **Profiles after removal.** `vaultPath` in `src/main/safeStorage.ts` is `vault.enc` under `app.getPath('userData')`, which is in the home folder.
- **Troubleshooting.** `docs/help/troubleshooting.md` exists and `HelpView.tsx` lists it under Reference.

Tested on Windows 11 (24H2, x64) on 13 October 2026 against release 1.0.62-beta.25, in a throwaway virtual machine with default settings and Microsoft Edge 155, with no BinaryLane account and no token:

- **Download.** The release's Assets list shows `BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-win-x64.exe` (117,727,535 bytes), `BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-win-x64-portable.exe` and `BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-win-x64.exe.blockmap`.
- **Browser warning.** When the download finished, Edge's Downloads popup said the file "isn't commonly downloaded" and to make sure you trust it. Its **…** menu offered Delete, Keep, Report this file as safe, Learn more and Copy download link. **Keep** opened a box titled "Make sure you trust BLDesk-1.0.62-beta.25-win-x64.exe before you open it", saying Microsoft Defender SmartScreen couldn't verify the file because it isn't commonly downloaded (Publisher: Unknown), with **Report this app as safe**, **Learn more**, **Cancel** and **Delete** with an arrow; **Keep anyway** is in that arrow's menu. It was seen and clicked in the first run; in a second run the menu closed before it could be clicked. After **Keep anyway**, opening the installer showed no "Windows protected your PC" window and no administrator prompt. The saved file had no `Zone.Identifier` stream (the Mark of the Web) and a `SmartScreen` stream instead.
- **Install.** A "BLDesk Setup" window showed for about four seconds, then BLDesk started by itself at the connect screen. It installed to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\bldesk`, added a Start menu and a desktop shortcut, and registered as "BLDesk 1.0.62-beta.25" under Settings → Apps. That matches `nsis.oneClick: true`, `perMachine: false` and `allowToChangeInstallationDirectory: false` in `package.json`; `nsis/installer.nsh` shows no prompt.
- **Uninstall.** Settings → Apps → BLDesk → Uninstall worked. Run again with the uninstaller's silent switch it exited 0, and the program folder, both shortcuts and the Apps entry were gone. `%APPDATA%\bldesk` stayed (19 entries, no `vault.enc` because no profile was saved), which matches `deleteAppDataOnUninstall: false` and `vaultPath` in `src/main/safeStorage.ts`.

Not tested on Windows: any browser other than Edge, and what a Windows-level SmartScreen window says; a user without administrator rights or with UAC on (the test user was an administrator with UAC off, so the README says only that the installer has no pages to click through); the portable `.exe`, whose sentence repeats the Windows (NSIS) row of `docs/AUTO_UPDATE.md`; Windows 10, Windows on ARM, other Windows 11 builds and languages other than English; the download's checksum against the one on the release page; updating from an older installed version.

Not tested on macOS and Android: the sentence repeats the macOS signing paragraph in `docs/AUTO_UPDATE.md` and the Installation section of `docs/help/troubleshooting.md`.
