## DNS record TTL (29 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.7)

Branch: `fix/dns-record-ttl`. No new runtime dependencies. Reference wording for `DomainRecordRequest.ttl` in `openapi.json`: "The default and only supported value is 3600. Leave null to accept this default."

Checked against the live API on 2026-09-29 with a disposable zone: adding a record without `ttl` succeeds and is stored with TTL 3600; `"ttl":3600` and `"ttl":null` also succeed with 3600; `"ttl":300`, the value the form used to send, is refused with 400 "The default TTL of 3600 may not be changed." So before this change the Add Record form could never create a record: every submit failed. The sentence in `dns.md` that said the form sends 300 seconds described a form that did not work.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `dns.md`, "The form sends no TTL, so BinaryLane's default applies: its API reference gives 3600 seconds as the default and only supported value." | `handleCreateRecord` in `DnsManager.tsx`: the request body is `type`, `name` and `data` only | Changed from "This form sends TTL 300 seconds". The palette's `dns add` also sends no `ttl` (`CommandPalette.tsx`). |
| `dns.md`, "There is no TTL input, existing-record editor or priority/weight/port input." | The Add Record form in `DnsManager.tsx` has type, host and target fields only; nothing in `src/` calls the record update endpoint | Unchanged, rechecked. |
| `dns.md`, "previous answers may remain until their TTL expires" | `handleDeleteRecord` summary in `DnsManager.tsx` | Unchanged, still true. |
| TTL column in the records table; "TTL 3600s" beside each zone | `{r.ttl}` and `{domain.ttl}` in `DnsManager.tsx` | Unchanged: shows what the API reports, so a record created earlier with 300 still reads 300. No help page describes either column. A zone whose `ttl` is null or missing used to read "TTL s"; it now shows no TTL text (#171). A real 0 still reads "TTL 0s". |
| `FEATURES.md`, DNS boundary | Same form | Changed from "fixes TTL at 300". |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guard scripts) and `npm run build`.
- Real Electron builds with isolated user data, name resolution blocked and every API request answered by a local fixture (nothing sent to BinaryLane), driven through the page over the debugging port. The fixture follows the live results above: `ttl` absent, null or 3600 is accepted and stored as 3600; any other `ttl` is a 400 with the message above. The zone had an existing record at TTL 300.
  - Base build (before this change), Add Record with type A, host `www`, target `192.0.2.99`: sent `POST /v2/domains/example.com/records` with `{"type":"A","name":"www","data":"192.0.2.99","ttl":300}` and got the 400. The UI reported it only as an alert, "Failed to add record: The default TTL of 3600 may not be changed."; the Add Record dialog stayed open with the fields filled, no row was added, and History showed "Add DNS record" as Failed.
  - This branch, same input: body `{"type":"A","name":"www","data":"192.0.2.99"}`, no `ttl` key, accepted; the dialog closed and a new row appeared with TTL 3600. The existing 300 row still read 300. The form has no TTL text or input.
  - This branch, command palette `dns add A palette.example.com 192.0.2.50`, Run: body `{"type":"A","name":"palette","data":"192.0.2.50","priority":null}`, no `ttl` key, "1 of 1 submitted", and the new row appeared with TTL 3600.
  - The 400 message text and the stored 3600 come from the live check; the fixture's error envelope (`title`, `status`, `detail`) is an approximation of the reference's problem-details shape.

## Disable automated backups confirmation (29 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.7)

Branch: `fix/disable-backups-confirm-text`. No new runtime dependencies. One help page changed (`backups`).

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only (`DisableBackups`, `EnableBackups`): disabling “will disable backups for a server only if it has the backup configuration that is created by the EnabledBackups action, i.e. two daily backups”, it “is not a 'pause' for backups, this will change the server's options to remove the backups”, and “This is a destructive operation (previous backups will no longer be available) and no further confirmation will be requested.” `EnableBackups` “will enable two daily backups if the server currently has no backups.”

The reference's “previous backups will no longer be available” is broader than what a live check showed (see Checks performed): the daily backups were removed, but a temporary backup was kept. The dialog and the help page claim only what was observed, and the reference's wording is quoted only as the reference's. The difference is to be raised with BinaryLane.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Changes the server's options to remove its daily backups. This is not a pause: BinaryLane removes them, including any you took with Take Backup into a daily slot, and does not ask again.” | `handleToggleAuto` in `BackupManager.tsx`, `summary` when disabling | Changed. It replaces “Nightly backups stop. Existing backups are kept until they age out.”, which the reference and the live check both contradict. Options changed to remove the backups, not a pause and no further confirmation are the reference's; that a backup taken into a daily slot is removed is from the live check. |
| “BinaryLane does this only when the server has the two daily backups that enabling automated backups creates.” | `handleToggleAuto`, first `notes` entry when disabling | New. The reference limits the action to the configuration Enable creates, and the live check refused it on an already-disabled server and on a weekly setup, so an unconditional claim would not be true for them. |
| “Temporary backups you took with Take Backup are not removed.” | `handleToggleAuto`, second `notes` entry when disabling | New, from the live check only; the reference does not say it. “Temporary” is the slot Take Backup offers by default (`BACKUP_SLOT_LABELS.temporary`). |
| Severity of the disable dialog | `handleToggleAuto`, `severity` | Stays `destructive`: a red button and no typed name, by the maintainer's decision (an earlier revision of this change made it `irreversible`). The footer reads “Recorded in History.” and the History tab lists the entry as DESTRUCTIVE with its outcome. |
| The Enable dialog | `handleToggleAuto` | Unchanged: `normal`, no notes, no typed name. |
| “Disabling automatic backups” section of `docs/help/backups.md` | Same handler; “Disable Schedule” label from the banner button in `BackupManager.tsx` | Rewritten: its quotes are the summary, the two notes and the title above, the typed name is the `irreversible` default, and “use Download on any image you need to keep” refers to the Download button on every row of the image table. Its old claim that images age out is gone; it says the reference's wording is broader than what was observed. |

### Checks performed

- Repo-wide search for the old claim and for other descriptions of disabling backups (`age out`, `kept until`, `disable_backups`, `automated`/`automatic backup`, `nightly`, `Disable Schedule`) across every tracked file. Only `BackupManager.tsx` and `docs/help/backups.md` repeated the claim. `README.md` and `FEATURES.md` say only that there is a nightly schedule toggle, `REQUIREMENTS.md` lists the two actions, `docs/help/server-backups.md` says retention is controlled by BinaryLane, and `docs/help/palette.md` covers the `backup` verb, which takes a temporary backup; the palette has no disable-backups verb. `CHANGELOG.md` is release-time history and was not changed. None of these describe what disabling does to existing backups.
- Clean `npm run typecheck` (including the help guard: 49 quotes resolved) and `npm run build`.
- Built app run in Electron with isolated user data and a fixture account, cloud writes rejected. On a server with backups, “Disable Schedule” opened the dialog as quoted above with a red button, both notes, an `on → off` row and “Recorded in History.”, and no typed-name field. Cancel sent nothing. On a server without backups, “Enable Nightly Backups” opened the unchanged normal dialog. The rewritten help section rendered as written. After the severity was set to destructive, the dialog was run again against a fixture that accepts the request: exactly one `disable_backups` request went out, and the History tab listed “Disable automated backups”, the server, DESTRUCTIVE and Completed.
- Real Electron zoom in 1024×680 and 1280×840 windows: 80, 100, 125 and 150% by Ctrl+-, Ctrl+= and Ctrl+0 sent with `webContents.sendInputEvent`, and by the View menu's Zoom In, Zoom Out and Actual Size. The page's CSS width matched the window divided by the zoom each time. The irreversible dialog's title, summary, notes, name field, Cancel and confirm all sat inside the window with no horizontal overflow or clipped text; only at 1024×680 and 150% did the dialog body scroll, by about 18 pixels, with the header, name field and both buttons still in view. Zoom stopped at 150% and 80%, and Actual Size returned to 100%. The rewritten help section could be scrolled to its last paragraph at every zoom, with no horizontal overflow. Screenshots were taken with `webContents.capturePage()`.
- Failure path: a refused disable (a 400 problem response, and separately a dropped connection) was recorded in History as failed, with the response text as its detail, and shown in the alert “Schedule update failed: ” followed by that text.
- Checked against the live API on 2026-09-29 with a disposable server, by calling the API directly: `enable_backups` gave the two-daily setup; a backup taken into a daily slot with `take_backup` was removed by `disable_backups` at once (200, about two seconds, no confirmation step); a temporary backup taken with `take_backup` survived it and stayed available; `disable_backups` on an already-disabled server, and on a weekly setup, was refused with 400 and removed nothing, the message saying the backup setup is not the default and pointing to `resize`. The server was disposable and nothing here identifies an account.
- Not verified: `disable_backups` sent from BLDesk's own dialog against a live account; the Electron checks above used fixtures and made no cloud writes.

## Take Backup replaces a backup without saying so (1 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.9)

Branch: `fix/take-backup-warn-oldest-replaced`. No new runtime dependencies. Three help pages changed (`backups`, `palette`, `server-backups`) and one line of `FEATURES.md`.

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only (`TakeBackup`, `BackupReplacementStrategy`, `BackupInfo`, `ImageRequest`, `SelectedSizeOptions`, `BackupSlot`). `oldest`: “Use any free slots of the provided backup type, and if there are no free slots replace the oldest unlocked and un-attached backup of the provided backup type.” `none`: “Do not replace any existing backup: use a free slot of the provided backup type. If there are no free slots an error will occur.” `specified`: “Replace the specific backup id provided.” A locked backup: “If this is true the backup is locked and cannot be replaced.” On locking: “You may not lock or unlock a temporary backup or a backup that is attached to a server.” Retention: “The number of retained daily backups.” (likewise weekly and monthly). `temporary`: “A backup which is created on demand and only retained for a maximum of seven days.” The reference gives no number for how many temporary backups a server can hold, and does not say what BinaryLane answers when every backup of a type is locked or attached. It says a backup is replaced or overwritten, not how soon the old one disappears, so “The replaced backup will no longer be available.” is the plain consequence of replacing and was not checked against a live account. Nothing in the reference says what a failed read of the backups list means for a take, so a take whose list could not be read is treated as one that might replace a backup: BLDesk asks, names no backup, and says it could not read the list. The list is the first page only (the default `per_page` is 20), as it was before this change: a server holding more is not read in full, and this change does not alter that.

The Backups form and the palette both send `replacement_strategy: oldest` (the form sends `specified` when you pick a backup under “Replace Existing Image”). The request sent is unchanged by this fix; only what you are told before it is sent has changed. `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` already avoids `oldest` and was not touched. The default in `useTakeBackupMutation` (`queries.ts`) is still `oldest`, but its only caller always passes a strategy, so nothing reaches it.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “No daily slot is free, so this backup replaces the oldest daily backup that is not locked or attached. The replaced backup will no longer be available.” | `takeBackup` in `BackupManager.tsx`, `summary` when `replacedByOldest` (`lib/backupSlots.ts`) says `certain` | New. Only for daily, weekly and monthly, whose slot count is `selected_size_options`; shown only when the backups list already holds that many of the type and at least one is neither locked nor attached. |
| “If this server has no free temporary slot, this backup replaces its oldest temporary backup that is not locked or attached. The replaced backup will no longer be available.” | Same handler, `summary` when not `certain` | New. Worded as a possibility because the reference gives no temporary slot count; shown whenever the server holds a temporary backup that is not locked or attached. A server with no temporary backup, or only an attached one, gets no dialog. |
| “This backup replaces the backup you chose. The replaced backup will no longer be available.” | Same handler, `summary` for `specified` | New. Not shown when the chosen backup is locked or attached, because the reference says a locked backup cannot be replaced and an untrue sentence is worse than none; the request is sent as before and BinaryLane answers. |
| “Replaced backup”, “Replaced if no slot is free”, “New daily backup”, “Label” | Same handler, `changes` table | New. The old value is the backup's name, ID, slot and date, from the backups list the tab already loaded. |
| Severity of the dialog | Same handler, `severity` | `destructive`: red button, no typed name. A replaced backup cannot be recreated, which by the letter of the severity rules is `irreversible`, but the maintainer put the larger loss (disabling automated backups removes every daily backup) at `destructive`, and a typed server name on every on-demand backup into a full slot is out of proportion to a single rotated backup. `normal` was rejected: a blue button on a step that discards a backup understates it. |
| “Couldn't read this server's backups, so BLDesk can't say which one would be replaced if no slot is free. A backup that is replaced will no longer be available.” | `takeBackup` in `BackupManager.tsx`, `summary` when the read made at submit failed (an error status, with or without a body, or a read that did not finish after the submit) and the strategy is `oldest` | New. Replaces the stale-list fallback: only the read made at submit counts. No backup is named, the dialog is `destructive`, and History records it. |
| “Couldn't read this server's backups, so BLDesk can't check whether the backup you chose can still be replaced. If it can, this backup replaces it and the replaced backup will no longer be available.” and the row “Backup you chose” | Same handler, `summary` and `changes` when the read failed and a backup was chosen under “Replace Existing Image” | New. The row names the backup from the list on screen, where it was picked; the request's `backup_type` is the type of that backup, which does not change. |
| “Couldn't read this server's backups.”, “Couldn't refresh this server's backups.”, “The list below is from the last successful load.” and a Retry button | `BackupManager.tsx`, the alert above the list when `useServerBackups` is in error | New. `useServerBackups` (`queries.ts`) now throws on a failed read instead of returning an empty list, so “No Backups Found” is shown only when the read succeeded. `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`, the only other caller, reads the same data and shows nothing new. |
| “Checking which backups would be replaced…” | `CommandPalette.tsx`, confirm stage while `checking` | New. Shown while Run is disabled. |
| “If a server has no free temporary slot, BinaryLane replaces its oldest temporary backup that is not locked or attached, and the replaced backup will no longer be available. The backup that would be replaced on each server is listed below.” | `CommandPalette.tsx`, amber note in the confirm stage | New. Followed by one line per server: name, backup name, ID, slot and date. |
| “example-server: could not list its backups, so BLDesk cannot say which would be replaced” | `checkReplacements` in `CommandPalette.tsx` | New. Shown for a target whose backups list could not be read. |
| History row “Replaced if no slot is free” and DESTRUCTIVE for a palette take | `run` in `CommandPalette.tsx` | New, for a target with a line above; other backup targets are recorded as before. |
| `backups.md`, section “Take Backup”; the Take Backup dialog's help link opens it | Dialog: `helpSlug: 'backups#take-backup'`; text: the strings above | New. Its quotes are the three summaries and the two failed-read summaries, “Take Backup”, “Replaced backup”, “Replaced if no slot is free”, “New daily backup”, “Replace Existing Image” (the group label of the slot list), “Couldn't read this server's backups.” and “No Backups Found”. “its button is red but asks for no typed name” is `severity: 'destructive'`. |
| `palette.md`, Backups | Same palette code | Rewritten: quotes the checking text, the amber note, the unreadable-list line and the History row, and says the reads are made four at a time (`mapLimitNullable` in `queries.ts`, the helper the fleet queries use). The `FEATURES.md` line on backup safety says the review names the backup. The sentence “This can destroy an existing image.” is gone, replaced by the note it described. |
| `server-backups.md` | Link only | New “Taking a backup” section pointing at `backups.md`. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guard scripts; the help guard resolved 78 quotes, 18 more than on `origin/main`), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- The real app in Electron through `scripts/gui-test` (isolated user data, name resolution mapped to a local mock, nothing sent to BinaryLane), the base build (`origin/main`) against this branch. Before #204 the repository mock listed every backup as daily and never removed one, so these runs used a copy of it outside the repository that modelled the reference: per-server backups with `backup_info.type` and `locked`, `attached_backup`, retention counts, and the three strategies (`oldest` replaces the oldest backup of the type that is neither locked nor attached; a full slot with none replaceable, and a locked `specified`, are answered with a 400; a temporary slot count of one is an assumption of the fixture). Its log recorded every request body and which backup a request replaced.
  - Free slot (a daily slot at 1 of 3, no temporary backup, a server with dailies only, a server with no backups): no dialog on either build, one identical request, nothing replaced.
  - Full daily and weekly slots, and a server holding a temporary backup: the base build sent the request on submit and replaced the oldest backup with no mention; this branch showed the dialogs above naming the backup, and Cancel sent nothing (the three cancelled takes are the only difference in request count between the builds). Confirming sent the same request as the base build and replaced the same backup. With two temporary backups it named the older; with the oldest daily attached it named the next oldest.
  - Only locked or attached candidates: no dialog, the same request on both builds, the fixture's 400 shown by the existing alert.
  - Palette `backup` on one server and on seven at once (some with a replaceable temporary backup, one with only an attached one, two with no backups): the review listed the two servers that would replace a backup and named their backups, and Run waited for the reads (with 2 s of added latency: Run disabled, a second Enter ignored, no request before Run). Servers whose list showed no backups were not read. Esc during the reads returned to the input and sent nothing. Confirming sent the same seven requests as the base build (six accepted, one refused by the fixture).
  - History showed the replaced backup in both paths, marked DESTRUCTIVE. The new help section rendered, and the dialog's help link cancelled the dialog and opened the page at its heading.
  - Re-run after #204 against the repository's own unmodified mock, which now holds per-server backups with `backup_info.type`, retention counts, locked and attached images and the `oldest` strategy. The base build and this branch sent identical request bodies on 23 matching steps (the two cancelled steps sent nothing on the branch). For full daily, weekly and monthly slots the dialog named one backup and the mock then replaced exactly that one; with the oldest daily locked, or attached through Mount, both the dialog and the mock skipped it and used the next oldest; a `specified` take agreed. No dialog appeared for a server with no backups, one whose only temporary backup is attached, a free slot, or a take whose only candidate is locked (the request went out and the mock's 400 appeared through the existing alert, as on base). The mock sets no limit on temporary backups and never replaces one, so the conditional temporary lines named backups the mock kept.
  - Live API (1 October 2026): this branch's build, signed in to a real account, on one disposable server with one daily and one weekly slot, nothing else touched. A weekly take into a free slot showed no dialog and was taken. A second weekly take showed the dialog naming the first weekly backup; confirming it left only the new backup, so the named backup was the one replaced and was gone afterwards. A first temporary take showed no dialog; a second showed the conditional temporary dialog naming the first, and confirming it left only the new one, so on that plan a server holds one temporary backup and the conditional wording is accurate. With the only weekly backup locked from outside the app, the dialog first still named it (the list had been loaded earlier and is not polled); the form now reads the list again before it asks, and then showed no dialog, sent the request, and BinaryLane answered "Validation of backup_type, replacement_strategy failed." through the existing alert, replacing nothing. A `specified` take of that locked backup likewise showed no dialog and was refused. A `specified` take of an unlocked temporary backup showed the dialog and was then refused by BinaryLane with "Validation of backup_type, backup_id_to_replace failed." because the request carries no `backup_type`; a request with `backup_type` set to the replaced backup's type was accepted. That was before #236, which now sends `backup_type` for a `specified` take; the branch has since been rebased on it.
- Real Electron zoom, 1280×840 and 1024×680 windows at 80, 100, 125 and 150%, by `webContents.sendInputEvent` Ctrl+minus, Ctrl+equals and Ctrl+0; the CSS width matched the window divided by the zoom each time. The Take Backup dialog and the palette review (seven targets, two warning lines) had no horizontal overflow and every button was inside the window; screenshots came from `webContents.capturePage()`. With the two warning lines the panel's bottom edge ran past a 1024×680 window at 150% (500 against 453 CSS px) though Run stayed in view. Worst case, `backup *` with every server holding a temporary backup (28 targets): the content is 588 CSS px tall against 325 available at 150%, which without a scroll would leave Run out of the window, so the review body now scrolls (`max-h-[calc(100vh-8rem)]`); at every size the panel then stayed inside the window and Run was reachable by scrolling. Zoom stopped at 150% and 80% and Ctrl+0 returned to 100%.
- Not verified: a daily or monthly slot against the live API (only weekly and temporary were taken); a backup locked or attached between the moment the form reads the backups list and the moment Confirm is pressed; attached state, which comes from the servers list (refreshed about every 15 seconds), so a backup attached from outside the app in that window can still be named (attaching through Mount in the app was right at once); an image attached to a different server, which the mock protects and the dialog does not check; the palette against the live API; the Android layout.

### Checks after the review

Run on the final commit, with the repository's unmodified mock, on the same steps against three builds: `origin/main` (BASE), this branch before the review changes (BEFORE) and the final commit (AFTER). A failing read was injected with `/__mock/fail` (500 with a JSON body, and 429) and, for a 500 with an empty body, by a local Playwright route.

- `npm run typecheck` (78 quotes), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Reads that succeed (monthly full, daily full after two takes, daily free, a temporary with one held, a server with no backups, only a locked or attached candidate, `specified` with an unlocked and with a locked backup): AFTER behaves as recorded above, and its requests are the ones BASE sends, including `backup_type` on a `specified` take (from #236; the request now takes the type the form already worked out).
- A read that fails at submit, after the list loaded (500 JSON, 500 empty, 429; `oldest` into a full monthly slot, a temporary slot and a free daily slot; `specified`): BASE sent the request at once with no dialog, and the full monthly slot lost its backup. AFTER keeps the submit disabled for about three seconds (the query retries twice), then shows the failed-read dialog, red, with no backup named for a slot and the “Backup you chose” row for `specified`. Cancel sent nothing. Confirming sent the request BASE sent, and the mock replaced the same backup. BEFORE showed its stale-list dialog here.
- The list's first read fails (500 JSON, 500 empty): BASE and BEFORE showed “No Backups Found” and a take went out with no question (a full monthly slot lost its backup). AFTER shows “Couldn't read this server's backups.” with Retry and no “No Backups Found”, and asks on submit with the failed-read dialog. Retry with the failure still on keeps the error; with the failure cleared it shows the list.
- The list loaded, then a read fails: BEFORE emptied the list on screen; AFTER keeps its four rows under “Couldn't refresh this server's backups.” and “The list below is from the last successful load.”.
- The other caller of `useServerBackups`, `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`, with the read failing: no error, the same options as before this change (“Not be taken before the change”, “Use a free slot” with the slots the server's retention allows, no “Replace an existing backup”). With a successful read it offers “Replace an existing backup” and leaves out a full slot. Not changed here. With the read failing it can offer a full slot, which its `replacement_strategy: none` request would have refused rather than replaced.
- Palette `backup *` (27 targets that hold backups, mock latency 1.2 s): BEFORE had all 27 reads in flight at once (the mock saw bursts of six, the browser's per-host limit); AFTER never had more than four, counted in the app and in the mock log, and took about 11 s to settle against 6 s. Three failing targets (500, 429, 500) and one empty 500 each showed the “could not list its backups” line. Escape 1.5 s in left 19 of the 27 reads unstarted, where BEFORE had sent all. Run on seven targets sent the same seven requests on BASE, BEFORE and AFTER.
- Double submit of the form into a free daily slot and into a full monthly slot, by two `requestSubmit()` calls in one task and by Enter twice in the name field: BASE and BEFORE: the second request was refused by the client's duplicate guard, with a failed History entry and the alert “Backup failed: Action was already submitted. Please wait a moment before trying again.”; BEFORE with a dialog also queued a second dialog whose buttons stayed disabled. AFTER: one request, one dialog, no failed entry and no alert. With the mock answering after 1.5 s the submit button is disabled with a spinner during the read in AFTER, and was enabled in BEFORE. Two clicks 30 ms apart sent one request on every build, because the mock answers faster than that.
- The failed-read dialog's help link cancelled the dialog (no request) and opened the Take Backup section, which rendered the new paragraphs.
- Real Electron zoom (`webContents.sendInputEvent`): 1280×840 and 1024×680 at 80, 100, 125 and 150%. For both failed-read dialogs (with a label typed) and the list's “Couldn't read this server's backups.” box: no horizontal page scroll, no overflowing text, Cancel, the red button and Retry inside the window every time (at 1024×680 and 150% the navigation is the compact drawer, opened from the title bar, and the box's bottom edge is two pixels below the window while Retry is in view and the page scrolls). Zoom stopped at 150% and 80% and Ctrl+0 returned to 100%.
- Live API, this final build, a real account, one disposable server with one daily and one weekly slot, two backups in total: a weekly take into the free slot showed no dialog and was taken (the read at submit succeeded and changed nothing); a second weekly take showed the dialog naming the first, and confirming it left only the new backup, so the named backup was the one replaced. The failed-read path cannot be produced on the real API, so it was checked only against the mock.
- Not verified here: a failed read against the live API; the first page only of the backups list (the default `per_page` is 20): a server holding more is not read in full, as before this change; the palette against a fleet of hundreds of servers (27 were tried, four reads at a time); a double click on the form against the live API.

## Plan availability per operating system (24 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.6)

Branch: `fix/size-availability-per-image`. No new runtime dependencies. No help page describes plan availability, so no help text changes.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “We currently do not have resources available to provision a server on these plans.” | `PlanBlockNotes` in `PlanBlocks.tsx`, under the plan table on the create form and Change Plan | Changed: shown whenever any listed plan is blocked by stock, region or retirement, beside any other note. Before, a Windows image's memory note made it fall back to "Out of stock in this region." |
| Crossed-out circle on a blocked plan | `BlockedMark`, in place of the radio on both forms | New, matching the web panel. |
| Storage choices | `diskChoices`, `diskFloor`, `defaultDisk` in `serverPricing.ts` | Changed: from the larger of the plan's `disk_min` (20 GB on every Standard plan) and the image's `min_disk_size`, to `disk_max`, on the reference's steps (multiples of 5 GB, of 10 above 60, of 100 above 200); `restricted_disk_values` used as given. Before, the list started at the plan's included amount (100 GB on 4 vCPU). The plan's included amount is the untouched choice even when it is off the steps: std-8vcpu starts at its included 340 GB at no extra cost. On a server's current plan the untouched choice is the server's own storage instead, and the included amount is offered only if it is a step. |
| `memory` and `disk` in the create and resize requests | `CreateServerModal.tsx`; `startingFigures` in `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` | Changed: each is sent only when the customer moves it off where the form started (the plan's defaults, or the server's own figures on its current plan), as the reference's "leave null" wording describes. Before, both were always sent, so an untouched std-8vcpu went out as `disk: 400`. |
| “If the disk can't be resized, for example because the data doesn't fit in the new size or the disk has been changed inside the server, the change fails after the server has shut down, and the server restarts with its storage unchanged.” | `STORAGE_CHANGE_NOTE` in `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`, above the options and in the confirmation | New: shown whenever storage changes, up or down, except on a reinstall. It replaces “Reducing memory or storage shrinks the disk. The guest has to fit inside the smaller volume, and resizing back up afterwards does not restore anything lost.”, which was shown on any memory or storage reduction. |
| Retired current plan row | `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`, `isRetiredCurrent` | New: listed in price order, ticked and greyed with its memory and storage fixed; crossed out once another plan is picked, and cannot be picked again. The notice above the table is unchanged. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Public API: `/v2/sizes` without `image` reports a stock figure that matches no OS. With `image=windows-2022`, std-6vcpu and std-8vcpu are out of stock in Brisbane; with `image=ubuntu-24.04`, std-4vcpu to std-8vcpu are out of stock in Melbourne; the unfiltered list showed both in stock. With `server_id`, the list is the sizes that server can be resized to, with that server's stock (lb1 could still move to cpu-2thr and cpu-4thr, which are out of stock in Brisbane in general), and it includes the server's own retired plan. `image` accepts legacy slugs (`ubuntu-24.04.0`, `cpanel-whm-rocky-8`).
- `serverPricing.ts` bundled and run over every size and distribution image the API lists (21 x 27, 45 hidden as below the image minimum): no storage or memory value offered that the reference's rules reject, none below the plan's or image's floor, none above the maximum; CPU Optimised plans offer exactly their `restricted_disk_values`. Memory stays the doubling list from the included amount to `memory_max`, all valid values. Rerun after the change to `defaultDisk`: the only off-step value offered anywhere is std-8vcpu's own 340 GB as its untouched default, and with a current-plan `keep` of 400 it offers 300, 400, 500 without 340. In the app: a 4 vCPU server's Change Plan lists 20 to 2000 GB with its 100 GB selected, and std-8vcpu shows 340 GB at $156.80.
- Dev build against a live account, compared with the web panel by Blake: Brisbane Windows Server 2022 server on a retired plan (Change Plan: 1 GB hidden, current plan first and ticked at $17.50, 6 and 8 vCPU and 6 and 8 threads crossed out, then crossed out itself after picking 1 VCPU / 2 GB); create form Brisbane + Windows Server 2022 (6 and 8 vCPU crossed out, 1 GB hidden), Melbourne + Ubuntu 24.04 (4 to 8 vCPU crossed out, 1 GB listed), Sydney + Windows Server 2022 (all listed from 2 GB). A server on a current plan (std-4vcpu) behaves as before, apart from the "(current)" label, which is gone as in the web panel.
- Requests read from the dev build with the create and resize POSTs intercepted in the page (nothing sent to the API), on a live std-1vcpu server with 3 GB memory and 40 GB storage:
  - Change Plan, current plan, storage 40 → 20 GB: `options` has `disk: 20` and no `memory`. The storage note shows above the options and in the confirmation.
  - Change Plan, std-8vcpu untouched: no `disk` or `memory`. The row reads 340 GB, $156.80; the storage note shows (40 → 340 GB).
  - Change Plan, current plan, memory 3 → 4 GB: `memory: 4096` and no `disk`. No storage note.
  - Create form, std-8vcpu untouched: no `disk` or `memory`. Storage changed to 400 GB: `disk: 400`, row priced $163.40.
- Public API, for the storage note. The same server's disk was split by hand into `/` (10 GB) and a second ext4 partition (30 GB), with a marker file on each, the second placed past 20 GB. Resizes to 20 GB and to 60 GB were each accepted (HTTP 200) and then errored at "Configuring virtual disk" after "Shutting down server"; the server restarted by itself at 40 GB with both markers intact. A memory-only resize completed. A same-plan resize without `memory`, on the server at 3 GB, was refused with "This would result in no effective change.", so leaving `memory` out keeps the current value.
- Same-plan resize with `memory` and `disk` left out, the case the reference contradicts itself on (`Resize.options` says fields left out are removed; `memory` and `disk` say null keeps the current value on the same plan). A std-1vcpu created with 4 GB memory and 60 GB storage, above the plan's 2 GB and 40 GB. From Change Plan, staying on the plan and changing only daily backups (0 → 1): the request's `options` held no `memory` or `disk`, and the confirmation showed only the backup change. The resize completed. Afterwards the server's `memory` was 4096 and `disk` 60, and `selected_size_options` read `daily_backups: 1`, `memory: 4096`, `disk: 60`. Memory and storage left out are kept, not reset to the plan's defaults.

## Requests accepted with no action to follow (1 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.9)

Branch: `fix/action-202-no-body-accepted`. No new runtime dependencies. One help page changed (`history`).

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only: `POST /v2/servers/{server_id}/actions` documents two successes, `200` with an `ActionResponse` and `202` with the description “Accepted” and no content. The reference does not say which action types answer which, and says nothing about when a change takes effect after a `202`. BLDesk therefore claims only that the request was accepted.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “"Rename Server" was accepted by BinaryLane. It returned no action to follow, so check the server details for the result.” (the quoted part is the action's label, for example "Change Partner Server") | `executeAction` in `ServerSettings.tsx`, `case 'accepted'`, the green notice | New. Before, a `202` with no action produced a red banner, for example “"rename" returned HTTP 202 with no action body, so there is nothing to track.”, though the request had been applied. |
| “Accepted by BinaryLane (HTTP 202) with no action to follow, so there is no later outcome to record. Check the server for the result.” | `ACCEPTED_WITHOUT_ACTION` in `queries.ts`, written as the History `detail` by `ServerSettings.tsx` and `ServerNetwork.tsx` | New. The History entry stays Submitted (accepted, not finished); before it was recorded as Failed with the red banner's text. |
| “<label> — accepted by BinaryLane, which returned no action to follow. Check this tab for the result.” and the desktop notification “<label> accepted by BinaryLane.” | `run` in `ServerNetwork.tsx` | New. An action that returns an `action` still says “<label> — done.” and “<label> completed.” |
| Paragraph in `docs/help/history.md` beginning “A change BinaryLane accepts without returning an action to follow” | The History entry above | New. The label list is unchanged (still five), so the earlier note that History has five outcome labels stays true. Submitted is documented as “accepted, not finished”, which is all that is known here. |
| A `200` with no action, a `204`, or any other reply without an `action` | `describeMissingAction` in `queries.ts` | Unchanged: still a red failure with the status and Content-Length. Only a `202` is treated as accepted, because only a `202` is documented without content. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guard scripts), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Live API, disposable servers named DELETEME in the account, called directly with raw requests (status, headers and body kept). The disposable servers were deleted afterwards.
  - `202`, `Content-Length: 0`, no `Content-Type`, empty body: `rename`, `change_partner` (set and remove), `change_backup_schedule`, `change_threshold_alerts` (unchanged values), and `change_ipv6_reverse_nameservers` with an empty list on a server with no IPv6. For `rename`, `change_partner` and `change_backup_schedule` a GET straight after already showed the new value.
  - `200`, `application/json`, chunked, with an `action` still `in-progress`: `change_advanced_features`, `change_port_blocking`, `change_advanced_firewall_rules`, `change_reverse_name`, `change_ipv6`, `change_ipv6_reverse_nameservers` with entries or emptying them, `is_running`, `ping`, `uptime`, `reboot`.
  - `400` `application/problem+json`: `change_network` to the VPC the server is already in, `change_separate_private_network_interface` without a private network, `change_source_and_destination_check`.
  - So the same action type can answer either way (`change_ipv6_reverse_nameservers`), and a `202` is not rare: it is the answer for ordinary Settings edits such as renaming.
- Real Electron signed in to a real account (isolated copy, network limited to the API host), driven through the UI on two disposable servers, writes only after the confirm dialog named a DELETEME server:
  - Base build, Settings: renaming (twice) and Change Partner (set, then remove) each showed the red banner “"rename" returned HTTP 202 with no action body, so there is nothing to track.” (or the `change_partner` version) although the next API read showed the change had been applied; History recorded all four as Failed with that text. Update Advanced Features and the Network port-blocking switch, which return an action, were Completed.
  - This branch, same steps: no red banner; the green notice above; History showed the four entries as Submitted with the detail above; the next dialog on both builds started from the refreshed server (the second rename named the new name as its target and in its Name row; the partner removal showed the partner as its From value), and an API read after each step agreed. Advanced Features and port blocking behaved as on base (notice “completed successfully” / “done”, History Completed with the action number).
  - The Settings screen has no control for `change_backup_schedule`, so it was checked only by the direct API call above.
- GUI harness mock with a scratch copy of the mock outside the repository that answers `rename`, `change_partner`, `change_threshold_alerts` and `change_port_blocking` with a bodyless reply in each header shape, driven through Settings and the Network switch on base and on this branch:
  - `202`, no `Content-Type`, `Content-Length: 0` (the live API's shape) and `202` with neither header: base showed “HTTP 202 with no action body”; this branch showed the accepted notice.
  - `202`, `application/json`, `Content-Length: 0`: base showed “HTTP 202, Content-Length: 0 with no action body”; this branch showed the accepted notice.
  - `202`, `application/json`, no `Content-Length`: base showed only “Unexpected end of JSON input” (this Chromium's wording of the error) and History Failed with that text; this branch showed the accepted notice, no parse error anywhere.
  - `200`, `application/json`, `Content-Length: 0`: still a red failure on both, with the status and Content-Length in the text.
  - An ordinary `200` with an action: identical on both, notice “completed successfully”, History Completed with the action number.
  - In every accepted case the mock log showed exactly one POST per confirm (five per run).
- Real Electron zoom at 80, 100, 125 and 150% in 1280×840 and 1024×680 windows, by Ctrl+- and Ctrl+= sent with `webContents.sendInputEvent`, with the accepted notice showing: the notice and its Dismiss button stayed inside the page at every setting, no horizontal page scroll, no clipped text; the notice wrapped to two lines at 150% (1280×840) and from 125% (1024×680). The layout code is unchanged; only the notice's text is longer than “completed successfully”.
- Not verified: the Network tab's accepted path against the live API (the only live `202` for a network action was an empty `change_ipv6_reverse_nameservers` list on a server with no IPv6, which the tab cannot send; that path ran only against the mock); the Android build (the native HTTP bridge is a different response path, left unchanged apart from the shared `parseAs`); and the other places that send server actions with their own handling of an empty reply (firewall copy and clone, VPC moves, templates, the power-state probe, the list and palette actions), which this change does not touch.

## Generate SSH key pairs (24 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.5)

Branch: `feat/ssh-keygen` (#100). No new runtime dependencies: generation uses the system's `ssh-keygen`, found on PATH like `ssh`.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `keys.md` - "Generate a key pair" section | `GenerateKeyPairDialog.tsx`, opened from the SSH Keys header and beside Add SSH Key on the create form; `src/main/sshKeygen.ts` | New. Buttons render only when `window.bldeskApi.generateSshKeyPair` exists, so not on Android. |
| "The name you give is used for both the file in ~/.ssh and the key on your account ... the date is added to it" | `resolveKeyName` in `sshKeygen.ts`, fed the live account key names | Checked: a clash on disk or on the account gets `-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`, then `-2` and up. An empty name becomes `bldesk-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`. |
| "Nothing in ~/.ssh is ever overwritten, and ~/.ssh is created if it does not exist." | `generateKeyPair`, `ensureSshDir`; `ssh-keygen` runs with no stdin | Checked: an existing file is skipped by name; if one appears mid-call, `ssh-keygen`'s overwrite prompt reads end-of-file and exits 1, file unchanged. |
| "ssh-keygen writes it, and BLDesk reads only the .pub file" | `generateKeyPair` reads `${privateKeyPath}.pub` only | The private key is never opened. |
| "becomes its Key for this server once the create is accepted" | `CreateServerModal.tsx` calls `setKeyAssociation` with the generated key's path after `POST /v2/servers` returns an id | Checked live, below. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- `sshKeygen.ts` bundled and run against a temporary home, with both Microsoft's `C:\Windows\System32\OpenSSH\ssh-keygen.exe` (9.5p2) and Git for Windows' `ssh-keygen`: missing `.ssh` created; disk clash, account clash and empty name resolved as above with the first file's hash unchanged; `../evil`, `config`, `x.pub`, `.hidden`, `has space` rejected; missing `ssh-keygen` reported; `ssh-keygen -y` on each private key reproduces its `.pub`. Microsoft's `ssh-keygen` sets the private key's own ACL (the user, SYSTEM, Administrators); Git's inherits the same from the profile. Both are accepted by Microsoft's OpenSSH.
- Dev build against a live account: generated from the SSH Keys page (the key appears on the account with the same public key as the local `.pub`, and under the local ~/.ssh card as Linked), and again with the same name (dated name). "config" disables Generate with the reason shown. From the create form, the dialog prefilled the hostname and the new key was ticked beside MASTER.
- End to end: created a std-min Ubuntu 26.04 server in Sydney from the create form with a generated key. BLDesk stored the key's path as that server's key, and Remote Access showed it as Key for this server. `ssh -i <key> -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o BatchMode=yes -o PasswordAuthentication=no root@<ip>` logged in, and the key was in `authorized_keys`. The server, the test keys and their files were deleted afterwards.
- Linux: `sshKeygen.ts` bundled and run with Node 18 on Ubuntu 24.04 as a new user with no `~/.ssh` (OpenSSH 9.6, `/usr/bin/ssh-keygen` from PATH). `~/.ssh` was created 700, the private key 600 and the `.pub` 644; the clash, no-overwrite and `ssh-keygen -y` checks passed as on Windows; with the `.pub` in that user's `authorized_keys`, sshd (StrictModes on, the default) accepted a key-only login. This exercises the main-process generator, not the Linux app window.
- Not exercised: macOS. The build is not sandboxed (DMG and zip, no Mac App Store target), `ssh-keygen` is `/usr/bin/ssh-keygen`, which is on the PATH a Finder-launched app gets, and a new `~/.ssh` is chmodded 700 as on Linux. It needs a check on a Mac before this ships.

## Android request that gets no answer (29 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.9)

Branch: `fix/android-no-replay-after-transport-error`. No help page changed, no new runtime dependencies. One new error string; the desktop app never reaches it.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “No response from BinaryLane. If this request changed anything, it may or may not have gone through; check before trying again.” | Thrown as a `TypeError` by `executeFetch` in `client.ts` when the native `CapacitorHttp` call fails for any reason other than the plugin being unimplemented (a lost response, a timeout, a response that cannot be read). Callers show `err.message`: the create-server dialog's red line (`setErrorMsg(err.message)`) and History's failed detail, or a failure toast (`notifyFailure`) | New. Before, the request was sent a second time through `fetch` and the second answer was shown as if it were the first. A GET or HEAD, which cannot have changed anything, throws the shorter “No response from BinaryLane.” without the second sentence (a failed read, such as the firewall tab's error card, shows this). |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guard scripts), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Android 15 emulator, debug build with the API address pointed at a local fixture through a test proxy that lets the fixture run a request and then closes the connection without returning its answer (nothing left the machine). BASE against FIXED: a lost answer to `POST /v2/servers`, `POST` actions, `DELETE`, `PUT`, `PATCH` and `GET` ran twice on BASE and once on FIXED, which showed the message above (on a fresh connection: on a reused keep-alive connection Android's own HTTP stack sends the request once more before the app sees any error, on BASE and FIXED alike, and this change does not touch that). The create-server dialog on FIXED showed it as the red line with the fixture's `mock.log` holding one `POST /v2/servers`. The plugin being unimplemented still falls back to `fetch`, and 200, 204, 400 and 404 answers are unchanged.

## Firewall rules that could not be read (29 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.7)

Branch: `fix/firewall-failed-read-not-empty`. No new runtime dependencies. The Firewall tab and a server's Firewall sub-tab are the same component, `FirewallManager.tsx`; `useFirewallRules` in `queries.ts` is also read by the reachability chip in `ReachabilityBadge.tsx`.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `firewall.md`, section "When the rules cannot be read"; one sentence and link in `server-firewall.md` | The alert card and disabled controls below | New. The link target is checked by `check-help-guards.mjs`. |
| “Couldn't read this server's firewall rules.” | `FirewallManager.tsx`, the `role="alert"` card above the rule list when `firewallQuery.isError` and no list has loaded | New. Before, `useFirewallRules` answered a failed read with `[]`, so the tab showed “Firewall Inactive / Open” and offered every edit. |
| “Couldn't refresh this server's firewall rules.” | Same card, when `firewallQuery.data` holds a list from an earlier read | New. Before, a failed refetch replaced the loaded list with `[]`. |
| The line under the heading | `firewallQuery.error?.message`, thrown by `useFirewallRules`: `describeApiError` of the response body, or `HTTP <status>` when a JSON error has no body. A dropped connection reads “Failed to fetch”; an empty non-JSON error reads “HTTP 500 Internal Server Error” (from `safeNormalizeResponse`). Clamped to three lines. | New. |
| “The list below is from the last successful load.” | Same card, only when `firewallQuery.data` holds a list | New. |
| “Editing is switched off until they load, because every save writes the whole list back.” | Same card | New. True of Add Rule (unless the form is already open, when it reads Close Form), Import, Save Rule, the import dialog's Apply Rules, Disable Firewall and each rule's move and delete buttons: all `disabled` unless `firewallQuery.isSuccess`, and the add, move, delete, disable and import handlers return early on the same test. Clone and Export are not covered: with no list read they are already off, and with a list from an earlier read they still work from it, as the page says. |
| Retry | `firewallQuery.refetch()`; the icon spins while `isFetching` | New. Leaving the tab and coming back also refetches, because a failed query is retried on mount. |
| “Firewall Inactive / Open” | Now rendered only when `firewallQuery.isSuccess` and the list is empty | Changed. Before, also shown when the read failed and while a first read had not started (offline). |
| “(N rules)” beside the heading | Rendered once a list has loaded | Changed. Before, “(0 rules)” while loading and after a failed read. |
| “Fetching firewall rules from edge hypervisors...” | `isPending && fetchStatus !== 'idle'` | Changed. Also shown while the first read is paused because the machine is offline, where the card was empty. |
| Reachability chip, “Port 22 did not answer from this network. Nothing was refused, which is what a drop looks like.” | `explainTimeout` in `ReachabilityBadge.tsx`, `default` branch, now reached when the rule read fails | Changed. Before, a failed read gave `[]` and the “No BinaryLane firewall rules are set for this server…” sentence, which is false for a read that failed. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (including the help guard: 50 quotes, five more than before, all found in the source), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Real Electron, isolated user data, a fabricated profile, every request to the API answered from fixtures (no request left the process; name resolution was also disabled), writes captured. The build at `f06a6fc` (BASE) and this branch's build (FIXED) were driven with real mouse and key events through the same scenarios.
- Read fails (500, 502, 503, 401, 403, 404, an HTML 502 page, a long HTML 502 page, an empty-body 500 with and without a JSON content type, a dropped connection), on a server whose fixture list has 12 rules. BASE: the tab reads “Firewall Inactive / Open”, Add Rule and Import are enabled, Add's confirmation shows a lone `+accept tcp 22 from 0.0.0.0/0 — Allow SSH`, and its POST carries one rule; Import says “Replaces the current 0 rules with the 1 imported.” and also POSTs one rule. FIXED: the card above, Add Rule, Import, Clone and Export disabled, no POST possible, Retry enabled. The error appears about 2.7 s after the tab opens, because the app's default retry runs the read three times.
- Recovery: first read cycle fails, then succeeds. FIXED: Retry shows the real 12 rules, every control is enabled, and Add's diff is the 12 rules plus one, POSTing 13. A single blip (one 500, then 200) recovers by itself in about 1 s with no error shown. BASE: no control; the empty state stays.
- Genuinely empty list (200 with `firewall_rules: []`, and 200 with `{}`): BASE and FIXED alike show “Firewall Inactive / Open”, Add Rule, Import and “Add First Filter Rule” enabled, and Add POSTs one rule.
- A list that loaded, then a refresh fails (right after the customer's own write; on returning to the tab after 20 s): BASE wipes the tab to “Firewall Inactive / Open”, and a further Add POSTs one rule over the 13 the server then held. FIXED keeps the 12-rule list on screen under “Couldn't refresh this server's firewall rules.”, with editing off until Retry, after which the 13 rules show.
- Loading (a read that never answers, which the hook has no timeout for): BASE enables Add Rule and Import and POSTs one rule; FIXED has every write control disabled, and still does after 22 s.
- Other readers of the same query, BASE against FIXED. Reachability chip with a timed-out port and a failing read: BASE says no rules are set; FIXED says the port did not answer. With a genuinely empty list, and with a 12-rule list, the chip text is unchanged. The fleet matrix (“1 could not be read”) and the clone dialog's own read of its target (“Replaces the 0 rules on … with the 12 from …”) do not call the hook and are identical in both.
- Zoom, driven with ctrl+plus, ctrl+minus and ctrl+0 through `sendInputEvent`: 80%, 100%, 125% and 150% at 1280 x 840 and 1024 x 680, for the never-read and the stale-list card. Retry, Add Rule and Import reach the viewport, the page never scrolls sideways, the card text is intact. Zoom stops at 150% and 80% by key and by the View menu; Actual Size returns to 100%. The card was also checked in the light theme.
- The two help pages, opened in the app: the new section renders as written, and the link on the server firewall page opens it.

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Dev build against a live account, with disposable keys (since deleted):
  - Create form, "+ Add SSH Key": before, every add failed with a 400 (`public_key` was never sent; the caller passed `public_key` to a hook that reads `publicKey`). After, the key is created, with default set when ticked. History records it as Completed, and a rejected add (duplicate key) as Failed. Before, this path wrote no History entry.
  - With two default keys, the create form pre-ticks both (before: only the first).
  - Opening the create form fresh (not from a template) resets the ticks to the account's current defaults: MASTER unticked and the form closed, then reopened, came back ticked; a key made default through the API while the app was open was ticked on the next open (before, the previous ticks were kept). Template opens are unchanged, since the reset is skipped when the form has a prefill; not exercised live, as no template on the account sets keys.
  - A key added from the create form is ticked for that server straight away, as in the web panel, alongside the keys already ticked. A rejected key shows the API message (e.g. "The provided SSH key was not in a recognised format"), not the raw JSON body.
  - Edit: rename plus default off, then default on alone, each confirmed with a before → after table and read back from the API. An edit with nothing changed closes without a confirm or History entry.
  - Add SSH Key with the checkbox ticked creates a default key.
  - Freshness, with keys created and deleted through the API while the app was open: the create form dropped a deleted key the next time it opened (before, it kept offering it until Servers was reopened); the SSH Keys page showed an added key and dropped a deleted one on Refresh, and on leaving and returning within 20s (before, the cached list was reused for 20s). `keys.md` - "The list reloads from BinaryLane each time you open the page, and Refresh reloads it on demand" is `refetchOnMount: always` on `useSshKeys` plus the Refresh button.
- Table width, emulated CSS widths: at 1280 and 1600 the Actions column fits. At 1024 and below the table scrolls sideways, as it already did (59px before, 159px now, from the Default column). The column heading is "Default", with the full mPanel wording as its tooltip, so it fits at 1280.

## Firewall servers that could not be read: Clone and Copy ruleset (30 September 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.9)

Branch: `fix/firewall-unreadable-target-not-empty`. No new runtime dependencies. The same failure as the entry for the Firewall tab above (a read that failed shown as “no rules”), in the two places that read another server's rules before replacing them: the Clone dialog in `FirewallManager.tsx`, and Copy ruleset in `FirewallMatrix.tsx`, fed by `useFleetFirewalls` in `queries.ts`.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `firewall.md`: one paragraph after “Add Rule, Import, Clone and Export stay off.” and the section "Servers that cannot be read" | The Clone dialog and the Copy ruleset panel, below | New. |
| “Couldn't read the firewall rules on web-base. Nothing was changed: a clone replaces the target's whole list, so it needs the current one first.” | `FirewallManager.tsx`, `handleCloneSubmit`: `cloneError`, in the `role="alert"` box of the Clone dialog when the destination's rules could not be read (an error status, an error with an empty body, or a dropped connection). `web-base` stands for the destination's name. | New. Before, the dialog read the destination with its own request and turned any failure into an empty list, so it asked “Replaces the 0 rules on … with the 4 from …” and the write replaced the destination's real rules. |
| `firewall.md`, prose around that message: Clone reads the destination's rules when you press Apply Rules; no confirmation opens and nothing is sent; pressing Apply Rules again tries again | `handleCloneSubmit` reads the destination first, returns before `confirmAction` and before the POST, and the Apply Rules button stays enabled | New. |
| “unreadable” beside a target's name | `FirewallMatrix.tsx`, the target's label in the Copy ruleset panel, when `rulesByServer.get(s.id) === null`; its checkbox is disabled and unticked, and the everyone link skips it | New. Before, the box could be ticked and the copy diffed the server against no rules. |
| “(unreadable)” after a name in the source list | Same panel; the option is disabled | Changed. Before, “(0 rules)”, selectable. |
| “Review diff for N servers…” | Counts only the targets that can be written to | Changed. Before, it counted every ticked box. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (including the help guard: 56 quotes, two more than before, all found in the source), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Real Electron through the GUI test harness, a fictitious token, every request answered by the fixture API. The base build (`ae12d03`, BASE) and this branch's build (FIXED) were driven with real clicks through the same scenarios. The target was `postgres-syd-01` (#8102, four rules, one of them a PostgreSQL rule the source lacks); the source was `edge-web-syd-01` (#8100, four rules). Full request bodies were recorded in the page, and for the successful copy the writes were also read back from the fixture API.
- Target read fails, Copy ruleset, with 500, 403, 404 (JSON error bodies), a dropped connection, an empty 500 with a JSON content type and an empty 502 with none. BASE: the row says “unreadable” (or “0 rules” for the empty JSON-typed body, see below), the box can be ticked, the diff is four additions and no removals (a healthy read of the same server shows the PostgreSQL rule being removed), and Write to 1 server POSTs four rules that replace the server's real list. FIXED: the label reads “postgres-syd-01 unreadable” with a disabled, unticked box, “Review diff for 0 servers…” is disabled, nothing can be sent.
- Empty-bodied failures in `useFleetFirewalls`: an empty 500 with a JSON content type left `error` unset, so the hook returned `[]`, the matrix said “0 rules” and counted the server under “2 with no rules at all”, and the copy offered it as a target. That is fixed by testing `response.ok`, as `useFirewallRules` does. With the same failure on the source, BASE read “edge-web-syd-01 (0 rules)”, offered “Replaces the rule list on each selected server with the 0 rules from edge-web-syd-01.” and POSTed an empty list to the target. FIXED: the source reads “(unreadable)” and cannot be chosen.
- Source read fails (500): BASE listed “(0 rules)” and left Review disabled with no reason; FIXED lists “(unreadable)”, disabled.
- Mixed targets (one unreadable, two readable): BASE wrote to all three. FIXED wrote to the two readable ones, with the third shown unticked and “unreadable”. The everyone link selected 27 servers on BASE (unreadable one included) and 26 on FIXED. A target ticked while readable and made unreadable by Refresh: BASE still wrote to it (diff of four additions); FIXED unticked it, showed “unreadable” and wrote to the other one only.
- Clone, destination read fails with 500, 403, 404, a dropped connection and an empty 500 with a JSON content type: BASE: “Replaces the 0 rules on postgres-syd-01 with the 4 from edge-web-syd-01.”, a diff of four additions, and Clone rules POSTed four rules. FIXED: the dialog stays open with the message above, one read of the destination, no confirmation, no POST. The message clears when the destination is changed and when the dialog is reopened.
- Clone, source read fails: Clone stays disabled and the card says “Couldn't read this server's firewall rules.”, on BASE and FIXED alike. A destination that is genuinely empty (200 with `[]`): BASE and FIXED both say “Replaces the 0 rules on stopped-batch-01 with the 4 from edge-web-syd-01.” and POST the four rules. Both readable: identical dialog, diff and POST.
- Recovery: a read that fails once and then succeeds. Copy ruleset: after Refresh the label loses “unreadable”, the box can be ticked, and the diff shows the real rules (one removal, one addition) and writes four. Clone: the first Apply Rules shows the message and sends nothing; the second shows “Replaces the 4 rules on postgres-syd-01 with the 4 from edge-web-syd-01.” with the same diff.
- Cancel sends nothing (Copy ruleset and Clone). A successful Copy ruleset to three servers (one of them genuinely empty), a successful Clone and a healthy single copy show the same dialog text and buttons and POST the same bodies on BASE and FIXED.
- Zoom, driven with ctrl+0, ctrl+minus and ctrl+plus through `sendInputEvent`: 80%, 100%, 125% and 150% at 1280 x 840 and 1024 x 680, for the Copy ruleset panel with an unreadable server and the Clone dialog showing the message. The label, Review diff, Close, Apply Rules and Cancel are inside the viewport every time and the page never scrolls sideways. 1024 x 680 at 150% is 683 CSS px, so the mobile navigation drawer applies there, as AGENTS.md notes. Zoom was returned to 100%.
- The Firewall help page, opened from the tab's help button: both new passages render as written.
- Test-only: the fixture API's failure switch has no dropped-connection or empty-body mode, so those three cases used a temporary local edit to the fixture server that was not committed.
- Not exercised: the real BinaryLane API, a packaged build, the View menu zoom limits, the light theme, Android, macOS and Linux.

## Server-state notifications on first load (1 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.9)

Branch: `fix/no-power-notifications-on-first-load`. No new runtime dependencies. No help text changes: the one sentence that describes these notifications is still true, and is recorded here because what triggers them changed.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tray.md`: “Notifications can report server state, action results and balance conditions.” | `fleetWatch.ts`: `kind: 'server-state'` (a server appearing, changing state, or no longer listed) and `'balance'` (a failed invoice payment, prepaid credit below a floor); action results come from the components that call `sendNotification` after an action | Unchanged, still true. Not edited. |
| “<name> is now off” with “Was running.” (and the other words from `describeStatus`: running, off, being provisioned, archived) | `fleetWatch.ts`, the server-state effect | Strings unchanged. Changed when it is raised: not for the first power reading of a server whose API status has not changed, because the API keeps reporting a powered-off server as `active` and `usePowerState` only learns the real state from the server's latest sample a moment after the list. Before, that first reading read as a server switching off (or on) in front of the user. Still raised for every later change, for a change of API status (for example `new` to `active`), and for appearing and disappearing servers. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- Real Electron against the GUI mock, BASE (`origin/main`) against this branch, with the main process's notification calls logged and the OS toast suppressed. API-active servers given a stale latest sample and the rest a fresh one (answered synthetically in the page, nothing forwarded), first load with no cached list. BASE: “is now off / Was running.” for each stale server and “is now running / Was off.” for a server the API lists as off whose latest sample was fresh. This branch: no notification. The same run with the account empty at first and then filled: both builds raise “appeared” once per server (unchanged), and BASE adds the two power notifications above; this branch adds none.
- Real transitions after the first reading, BASE and this branch alike: a server whose latest sample goes stale raises one “is now off / Was running.”, and one “is now running / Was off.” when the sample is fresh again, each once.
- Live account (read-only for existing servers, one disposable server powered off through the API and deleted afterwards), first load with no cached list: BASE raised “is now off / Was running.” for the powered-off disposable server within seconds of launch; this branch raised nothing and showed it as off. With this branch open, a second disposable server powered off through the API raised one “is now off / Was running.” once the sweep saw its latest sample go stale, and one “is now running / Was off.” after it was powered on again.
- Not reproduced on BASE: relaunching with a cached server list present (the cached list counts as fresh for 10 s, so the first real fetch comes with the 15 s refresh, by when the power readings are in; seen on the mock and on the live account). The problem needs the first fetched list to arrive before the readings: a first launch, an account not loaded before, or after the crash screen's Reset Cache & Reload.
- Not exercised: a change of API status such as `new` to `active` (the code keeps raising it, since the new condition requires the API status to be unchanged), and switching profiles in the app (from the code: `baselineProfile` resets the list baseline, and `usePowerState` empties its readings on the same `profileId` change, so the new account's first readings are again the baseline). Android, which uses the same hook.

## Billing data transfer card wording (2 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.17)

Branch: `fix/billing-transfer-card-wording`. No new runtime dependencies. One help page changed (`billing`). The card is `BillingOverview.tsx`; the read is `useDataUsage` in `queries.ts`, which is unchanged: it already reads the pages of `/v2/data_usages/current` (200 per page, up to ten pages) and fails as a whole if any page fails.

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only: `DataUsage` has one entry per server. `transfer_gigabytes` is “The included data transfer for this server in this period in GB.” `current_transfer_usage_gigabytes` is “The used data transfer for this server in this period in GB.”, and adds: “If you have more than one server, please see our data pooling policy: this value may include excess data transfer used by other servers or may have 'offloaded' excess data transfer to other servers with spare capacity.” Each entry has its own `expires` and `transfer_period_end`. The reference does not say when pooling applies, how a server's figure is adjusted, or how to tell from the list which servers are over; it defines no “unlimited” value for `transfer_gigabytes` (a required integer). So the card can add the per-server figures up and say so, and cannot say pooling is, or is not, happening. The issue's proposed “Pooling / Not pooling” indicator is not built for that reason, and the allowance still comes from this list rather than from the server list: the list gives it per server directly.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Data Transfer” | `BillingOverview.tsx`, title of the third card | Changed from “Pooled Bandwidth”. |
| “Real-time account balance, data transfer, and tax invoice history.” | Same file, the line under the page heading | Changed from “…pooled bandwidth…”. |
| “Used / included across 3 servers, each for its own current period” | Same card, caption under the bar once the list has loaded and has at least one server (“1 server” for one) | New. |
| “BinaryLane's transfer list has no servers.” | Same caption when the loaded list is empty | New. |
| `0.0 / 0 GB` and an empty bar | The figure and bar for a loaded total allowance of 0 (an empty list, or servers whose allowance is 0) | Changed from `0.0 / Unlimited` with the bar at 5%. If something was used against an allowance of 0, the bar is full. |
| `billing.md`, new section “The Data Transfer card”, and the sentence in “When a figure cannot be loaded” that named “Unlimited” | The card above | New and changed. Its three quotes are strings above, checked by `check-help-guards.mjs` (97 quotes). |
| “Couldn't load the data transfer usage.”, the dash while loading, and the used / included figure for a list with a real allowance | Same card | Unchanged. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (including the help guard), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`. The help guard was also run against the changed text with the card strings altered, and failed on the new quotes, so it does check them.
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, isolated user data, the fictitious token. BASE (`cecbece`) and this branch were driven through the same data-usage answers, supplied by Playwright request interception on the mock's host (the mock's own fixture is one server): the mock's single server; an empty list; two servers with an allowance of 0 and nothing used; one with an allowance of 0 and 3.25 GB used; three servers with different period ends and allowances; two servers, one over its own allowance; 250 servers (two pages of 200 and 50, both requested); one server using all of its allowance. In every case the figure on both builds equalled the sum of the rows served, so the sum over pages was already right on BASE. BASE read “Pooled Bandwidth” in all of them, and `0.0 / Unlimited` with the bar at 5% for the empty list, for both allowance-of-0 cases (`3.3 / Unlimited`). This branch reads “Data Transfer”, the caption, `0.0 / 0 GB` with an empty bar for the empty list and the allowance-of-0 case with nothing used, and `3.3 / 0 GB` with a full bar when 3.25 GB was used against 0. Every other case differs only in the title and the caption.
- A failing usage read (500 on every try): a dash with no new caption while retrying, then “Couldn't load the data transfer usage.”; after the mock was reset and the page reloaded, the normal figure and caption. Same as BASE apart from the title.
- On my own account, read-only (the real app against the real API with no writes): BASE and this branch were each opened on Billing and the card compared with `GET /v2/data_usages/current` read separately. The figure, the bar width and, on this branch, the server count in the caption equalled the sum and the number of rows returned. The account's servers have period ends on several different days of the month, none is over its own allowance and none has an allowance of 0, so the card changed only in its title and the added caption there. The figures are not recorded here.
- Zoom: real Electron zoom at 80, 100, 125 and 150% by Ctrl+-, Ctrl+= and Ctrl+0 sent with `webContents.sendInputEvent`, content sizes 1280×840 and 1024×680, for the one-server list, the 250-server list and the empty list; the page's CSS width was the window divided by the zoom each time. The cards stay three across except at 1024×680 and 150%, where the existing compact layout stacks them. No horizontal page scroll, the card inside the viewport, no element past the card's edge and no clipped text (measured from the DOM). The caption is one line at 1600 CSS px, wraps to two lines at 100% in both window sizes (1280 and 1024 CSS px), and to three at the narrowest three-column widths (a card 176–187 px wide, 853 and 819 CSS px), so the cards are taller than before: at 1280×840 and 100%, 145 px against 115 px, and 193 px against 162 px at 150%. The View menu's zoom limits are not touched by this change and were not re-checked.
- The Billing help page, opened from the “?” beside the heading: the new section renders between “When a figure cannot be loaded” and “Follow a blocked action”.
- Not verified: Android; the page's own dark-mode toggle (the caption uses the grey of the other cards' captions); a list that the real API returns with an allowance of 0 or a server over its own allowance (the real account has neither, so those cases are fixtures); more than 200 servers on a real account. `REQUIREMENTS.md` still describes this as a “pooled data transfer dashboard” in its planning notes; it is not changed here.

| `palette.md`: `@word` with no verb first lists the tags in use that start with word as rows (coloured chip, “N servers”, category Tags; Enter or a click makes the box `@name ` and runs nothing; a tag typed in full has no row), then the servers whose tags start with word (several words must all match), each opens like any server; plain text lists servers by name or address only; plain text that is exactly a tag's name adds one separate Tags row that rewrites the query to the tag | the `!parsed` branch of `CommandPalette.tsx`; `liveTagCounts` and `tagSuggestions` (`lib/tags.ts`), the same list and order as the Servers search's @ suggestions | Changed. Removing a profile clears none of tags, groups or colours. The reset entry earlier in this file records the earlier wording. |

A desktop window at 1024x680 and 150% zoom is 693 css px wide, so it is the phone layout and its list chips are read-only too.

In the palette `@wordpress` listed the five servers under “Servers tagged @wordpress (5)”, `@word @prod` three, `@zzz` none; Enter on the first opened its server page. Typing `@` in the palette lists the tags in use as rows above the servers (a coloured chip, “N servers”, category Tags, the first row highlighted, none for a tag with no server in the account, alphabetical as in the Servers search); Down and Enter, or a click, made the box read `@name ` and list that tag's servers without sending a request; `@wo` and `@WO` narrowed the rows; a tag typed in full has no row and its first server is highlighted, and Enter on it opened the server; with no server matching two `@` words only the tag rows remain and the first is highlighted; `restart @wordpress`, the Groups row and the plain-text tag row were unchanged; Escape closed it.

Filter open: one Back closed it and focus returned to the filter button. A tag editor open (the Servers list's popover, and the Fleet matrix's): one Back closed it. With nothing open the handler found no dialog, so it would go on to the existing navigation. A real Escape key press gave the same result in each state. With the viewport resized to 411x420 (as a soft keyboard would shrink it) and the hex field focused, the panel was 8 to 412 px, Save and Cancel were inside the viewport and not covered, the form scrolled inside the panel, the hex field was in view, and the page had not scrolled. A real soft keyboard was not used.
