With nothing saved, FIXED shows “Couldn't load the server list.” and not “No servers found”. With the token rejected (401) the sidebar reads “API refused the token”.
- Palette DNS words: `dns add CAA example.com.au 0 issue letsencrypt.org` answers “Priority must be a whole number, got "issue"” on BASE and “CAA records need flags and a tag: add it from Add Record on the DNS tab” on FIXED; `dns add A qa.example.com.au 203.0.113.9 10` previews “(priority 10)” on BASE and answers “A records take no priority” on FIXED; MX with 70000 is accepted on BASE and refused on FIXED.
- SSH keys with the key list failing: BASE shows “Account SSH Keys (0)” and an Import button on every key found in ~/.ssh. FIXED shows “(…)”, “Keys not read”, and no Import button.
- Advanced settings, Save with nothing changed: BASE enables Save and sends the action; FIXED disables it, and a feature ticked off and on again is no change. One feature added shows one Features row on both.
- The record count in the DNS zone header and in “Remove DNS hosting” (“and all N records in it”) is the number read; with the list unread the header says “Records not read” and the dialog says “and every record in it”.
- Zone list: the read now fails if any page fails, like the other lists (it was built on a first page check that let a failure with no error body through, and kept a partial list when a later page failed).
- A save that fails without a body: on desktop the client already turns it into “HTTP 500 Internal Server Error” (BASE and FIXED show it for a DNS record and a VPC delete); the checks now also test the response status, as `errors.ts` documents, for the paths that reach the same code with no such rewrite. That path was not exercised on the Android build.
- Create Server opened from a template that names no region, image or plan, after Melbourne and Debian were chosen by hand: FIXED shows Sydney, Ubuntu 24.04 LTS and the first plan, via the palette and via Templates. BASE (beta.12) answers the palette command differently, so no BASE comparison is recorded.
- Palette tag: `tag add "my tag" #9001` previews “Tag @my tag” on BASE and “Tag @mytag” on FIXED, and the notice says “Use @mytag as a target anywhere.”; `tag add !!! #9001` is refused.
- Template export: BASE writes YAML anchors (`&a1`, `*a1`) where a rule's address list repeats. FIXED writes the lists out; the file imports back as a template with the same 3 rules, and a second import of the same name becomes “… (imported)”.
- Android, on the emulator (412 px wide) with a debug build of this branch and the mock API: the failed VPC read shows the notice and a tap on Retry, after the failure is cleared, brings the list; the Add DNS Record form shows Priority, Weight and Port for SRV without overflow and sends `{"type":"SRV","name":"_sip._tcp","data":"sip.example.com","priority":5,"weight":10,"port":5060}`; the record list's data column was about ten characters wide and broke values letter by letter, and now has room (a scrolling table) and shows `priority 10` above the MX data; Settings, Advanced lists the four video devices.
- `check-requests.mjs`, new in `scripts/gui-test`: every distinct request body in the mock log is validated against `openapi.json` (an action against the schema its `type` names). It reported 0 invalid for the runs above. The mock now logs whole bodies (it cut them at 300 characters), the checker counts and reports a line it cannot read instead of skipping it, and a synthetic log with a long create body, a `constructor` action type and a cut line gave 1 invalid and 1 unreadable. The mock's `/__mock/fail` takes `method` and `empty`.
- Not checked: a physical phone and the live API. `enable_rescue_mode` (#129) is not in the public reference and is left as it is.

## Ten issues from the review and testing queue (2 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.14)

Branch: `fix/batch-15`. Help pages changed: `backups`, `server-cancel`, `history`, `troubleshooting`, `keys`, `map`. The sources are `BackupManager.tsx` (`isAutoBackupEnabled`, the toggle); `ServerDetails.tsx` and `App.tsx` (`onCancelled`, the Cancel Server note); `serverPricing.ts` (`currentMonthlyCost`) with `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`; `updater.ts` (`held`); `ActionTrackerContext.tsx` (`followed`) and `templateJobs.ts`; `FirewallMatrix.tsx`; `mapFooter.ts` with `NetworkMap.tsx`; `sshKeyNames.ts` (`keyNameFromPublicFile`) with `src/main/index.ts`.

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only: `Server.selected_size_options.daily_backups` is the server's daily retention, which `enable_backups` ("Two Daily Backups") sets and `disable_backups` removes ("not a pause"); `Server.backup_ids` lists the backups that exist, however they were taken, and `next_backup_window` is "the next scheduled backup, if any". A server's bill is its plan's `price_monthly` plus the options it has selected, which are priced from the plan's `options`; `Account.tax_code` gives the tax.

| Claim | Decided by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `backups.md`: the banner reads Enabled when the server has daily backups in its options; backups taken by hand do not count; the button is “Remove Daily Backups” and the dialog is “Remove daily backups” | `isAutoBackupEnabled`, the title and button in `BackupManager.tsx` | Changed. Before, any backup made the banner read Enabled and the button “Disable Schedule”, which reads like a pause. Checked by `check-help-guards.mjs`. |
| `server-cancel.md`: the Cancel Server dialog gives what the server currently bills (plan, extra memory and storage, image surcharge, extra addresses, backups, licences), with the account's tax added and named when it has one, and “at least” until the licences have been read; the figure is the one Change Plan compares against | `currentMonthlyCost`, `billingTotal` | Changed. Before, it was the plan's list price alone. |
| `backups.md`: a server with weekly or monthly backups and no daily ones reads No nightly, with no enable button | `weeklyOrMonthlyOnly` in `BackupManager.tsx` | New. It read Enabled before (any backup counted), and Disabled with “Enable Nightly Backups” in the first version of this change; the reference enables two daily backups for a server that has none. |
| `history.md`: an action still being followed when the account is switched is closed as Lost track in the account that made it; a template's firewall rules change is recorded in the account the server was built in | `followed` in `ActionTrackerContext.tsx`, `changeProfile` in `changelog.ts` (an outcome goes to the account its change was recorded in), `input.profileId` in `templateJobs.ts` | Changed. Before, the first stayed “Submitted” for ever, and the second went to whichever account was active when the build finished. |
| `troubleshooting.md`: once an update has downloaded, Restart to update stays until it is installed, the update channel is changed, or a newer one starts to download; a later check that finds nothing newer or fails does not take it away | `held` in `updater.ts` | Changed. Before, any later check cleared it. On Windows and Linux the waiting update is dropped when a different version is announced, because electron-updater deletes the downloaded installer when that download starts (`validateDownloadedPath`, `removeFileIfAny` in `AppUpdater.js`). |
| `keys.md`: a key found in ~/.ssh is named after its file without the .pub ending, so my.pubkey.pub is my.pubkey | `keyNameFromPublicFile` | Changed. Before, the first “.pub” in the name was removed. |
| `map.md`: a server card shows the open ports at the left and the addresses at the right; the private address only when both fit; a long port list is cut with an ellipsis, with the full label on hover | `cardFooter` in `mapFooter.ts` | Changed. Before, the two could be drawn over each other. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guards and the 8 + 9 + 4 + 5 + 2 unit tests) and `node --test scripts/gui-test/mock.test.mjs` (13).
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, isolated user data, the fictitious token, the mock API. The installed 1.0.62-beta.14 (`--bin /opt/BLDesk/bldesk`, its own data directory and mock) was run as BASE next to this branch as FIXED. The mock gained `extraToken` (a second profile needs a different token to be a different client) and `feedStatus` (the update feed answers with a status and no body).
- Backups banner (#120, #191): a server with daily backups reads Enabled with “Disable Schedule” on BASE and “Remove Daily Backups” on FIXED. A server with no backups reads Disabled on both; after one on-demand backup BASE reads Enabled with “Disable Schedule” and FIXED stays Disabled with “Enable Nightly Backups”. The confirmation is titled “Disable automated backups” on BASE and “Remove daily backups” on FIXED.
- History after a failed attach (#165): the request answers 500. Both show “Attach failed: Injected 500”; BASE leaves the History entry Submitted, FIXED Failed. “Flush DNS Cache” answering 500: BASE says nothing, FIXED shows “Flush failed: Injected 500”.
- Cancel Server (#221): confirmation open for stopped-batch-01, another server opened behind it, then confirmed. Both send one DELETE for stopped-batch-01. BASE then shows the server list (27 servers); FIXED stays on the other server.
- Cancel Server note (#146), on a 10% GST account: win-app-01 BASE $39.20/month, FIXED $84.92/month (incl. $7.72 GST); multi-ip-v6-01 $39.20, $88.22 (incl. $8.02 GST); edge-web-syd-01 $9.80, $27.28 (incl. $2.48 GST). Change Plan's New monthly total for the same three servers reads $84.92, $88.22 and $27.28.
- Firewall matrix (#253), with the mock answering after 1.5 s: BASE reads “28 servers · 0 distinct rules”, “28 could not be read” and “Reading rules…” while loading; FIXED has no “could not be read”. Both finish at “28 servers · 5 distinct rules” and “1 with no rules”.
- Network Map (#239), three servers given long port lists: BASE draws the ports over the addresses on 7 of 28 cards (measured on the rendered text); FIXED on none. FIXED keeps the private address on 15 cards, cuts “1024-65535 2000-3000 4000-5000” to “1024-65535 2000-…”, and keeps the full label in the card's tooltip.
- Local keys (#220), in a throwaway home with `my.pubkey` and `id_ed25519`: BASE lists “mykey.pub” with no private key path; FIXED lists “my.pubkey” with its private key path, and `id_ed25519` is the same on both.
- Profile switch (#142), a reboot started with a 2-minute action and a second profile (a different token) switched to: BASE leaves the History entry Submitted; FIXED shows Lost track. A template with firewall rules applied to a server built in the first profile, then the second profile selected before the build finished: BASE records “Apply template firewall rules” in the second profile's History, FIXED in the first's.
- Updater (#136), the packaged build of this branch (`electron-builder --dir`, run with `APPIMAGE=1` so the launcher adds `--no-sandbox` here) against BASE, each with an update downloaded: a check with the same version offered keeps it on both; a feed answering 500 turns BASE to “error” and a feed answering 404 to “check-failed”, and the Restart to update button goes; a feed with nothing newer turns BASE to “up-to-date”. FIXED keeps “ready 1.0.62-beta.99” and the button through all three, and a newer version replaces it.
- After an independent review of this change, fixed and re-run the same way: a server with weekly backups only (created through the mock with `options.weekly_backups` 1) reads “No nightly”, “This server keeps weekly or monthly backups and no daily ones. Daily backups are set in Change Plan.” and has no button, where the first version read Disabled with “Enable Nightly Backups”; “Only flagged” ticked while rules load reads “Reading rules…”; the Network Map footers are measured once per layout; the repeated “is ready” notification and the macOS quit-while-downloading case are handled; an outcome is written to the account its change was recorded in, which is not the active one when the account was switched between the confirmation and the answer. Not exercised: a failed download of a newer version on Windows or Linux (read from electron-updater's source), and the switch between a confirmation and its answer.
- The Network Map footer is measured with the browser's own font, with an estimate for where nothing can be measured.
- Android, on the emulator (412 px wide) with a debug build of this commit and the mock API: the backup banner showed “Remove Daily Backups” on three lines beside the description, which ran into the button; the banner now wraps and the button stays on one line (checked again on a rebuilt app and at 1280 px on desktop). Cancel Server for win-app-01 reads “This server currently bills at $84.92/month (incl. $7.72 GST).”, and Back closes the dialog. The Network Map has 28 cards with none overlapping, the private address on 15, using the WebView's own font measure.
- Not checked: the updater on macOS and Windows, a physical phone and the live API.

## Ten more issues from the review queue, and a uuid alert (2 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.15)

Branch: `fix/batch-16`. Help pages changed: `server-settings`, `shortcuts`, `terminal`, `server-remote-access`, `deep-links`, `troubleshooting`. The sources are `deeplinks.ts` (`awaitingProfile`); `ServerSettings.tsx` (`passwordResetWording`); `deeplink.ts` (`restartArguments`) with `updater.ts`; `mobile-bridge.ts` (`openedAnotherApp`, `readSavedChannel`); `src/main/index.ts` (`console:openRescue`); `TerminalTab.tsx`; `ssh.ts` (`iterm2Script`).

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only: `Server.password_change_supported` is "if this is true the password_reset server action can be called to change a user's password. If this is false the password_reset server action will merely clear the root/administrator password allowing the password to be changed via the web console", and `PasswordReset` says a random password "will be generated and emailed to the account email address" where it is supported.

| Claim | Decided by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `server-settings.md`: password reset generates a new password and emails the account address where BinaryLane supports changing it, and only clears the root or administrator password where it does not, with nothing emailed | `passwordResetWording` from `password_change_supported` | Changed. Before, the card and the confirmation always said a new password is emailed. |
| `deep-links.md`: with `?account=` BLDesk switches first and then opens the link, so the server is looked up on that account; if the switch does not land it says so and does not open the link | `awaitingProfile` in `deeplinks.ts` | Changed. Before, the link was resolved against the account still in use. |
| `server-remote-access.md`: on Android, if BLDesk cannot tell within a few seconds that an app opened the ssh:// link, it says so and copies the command; if the rescue console page cannot be loaded, its window closes and BLDesk says why | `openedAnotherApp`; `console:openRescue` | Changed. Before, Android said it had opened a terminal whatever happened, and a console that could not load left a blank window. |
| `troubleshooting.md`: BLDesk remembers the update channel, on Android as well | `saveChannel` | Changed. Android forgot it on every launch. |
| `shortcuts.md`, `terminal.md`: Cmd+F or Ctrl+Shift+F opens scrollback search; plain Ctrl+F goes to the session | the key handler in `TerminalTab.tsx` | Changed. Before, Ctrl+F was taken for search and a shell's forward-char, or vim's page down, never arrived. |
| “The rescue console page could not be loaded (${…}). Check your connection and try again.”, “BLDesk couldn't tell that an app opened the ssh:// link. If nothing opened, install an SSH app that handles ssh:// links, or run the command below in one.” | `src/main/index.ts`, `mobile-bridge.ts` | New, not quoted in help. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guards and the 8 + 9 + 4 + 5 + 2 + 3 + 3 unit tests) and `node --test scripts/gui-test/mock.test.mjs` (15).
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, isolated user data, the fictitious token, the mock API. The installed 1.0.62-beta.15 (`--bin /opt/BLDesk/bldesk`, its own data directory and mock) was run as BASE next to this branch as FIXED. The mock gained a second account's own server (`extraToken` sees only #9101), `consoleUrl` and `links.pages`.
- Password reset (#141): win-app-01 (the mock gives it `password_change_supported` false) reads “Generates a new root password and sends it to your account email.” on BASE, and on FIXED “Clears the root/administrator password so a new one can be set at the server's web console. No password is emailed.” The confirmation says the same on each. edge-web-syd-01 (true) is unchanged on FIXED.
- Deep link with an account (#138), `bldesk://server/9101?account=Second`, with two profiles and the first active: BASE switches to Second, says “Server #9101 was not found on Atlas Demo (fictitious).” and ends on the server list. FIXED switches to Second and opens second-account-01's own view, with no message.
- After an independent review of this change, fixed and re-run the same way: the wait for a profile switch ends when its link is acted on or a newer link replaces it (a tab link naming another account switched, opened DNS and showed no “Couldn't switch” message 17 seconds later; a newer link is no longer held back by an older one's wait); a link naming an account waits until the saved profiles have been read, and a link that arrives while another is being opened is kept; the Android message says BLDesk could not tell that an app opened the link, after 3 seconds, and the copied command stays; the mock's second account no longer reads the first account's server sub-resources (`extraToken` has a test); the docs no longer claim the portable `.exe` does not update, that `installing` comes first on macOS, or that a Windows server is the case where a password cannot be changed.
- Rescue console (#153), the console address made one that cannot be resolved: BASE leaves a second window showing Chromium's error page and says nothing. FIXED closes it and shows “Couldn't open the rescue console for edge-web-syd-01: The rescue console page could not be loaded (ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED). Check your connection and try again.” A reachable console opens on both.
- Terminal (#152), an embedded session opened to 127.0.0.1: plain Ctrl+F opens the find bar on BASE and not on FIXED; Ctrl+Shift+F opens it on both.
- iTerm2 (#156): the AppleScript string decoded the way AppleScript decodes it, then run through `sh -c` with the session shell replaced by `true`. FIXED prints every argument intact (`a b`, `it's`, `q"uote`, `$HOME`, a backslash, a semicolon, backticks). The old construction fails with “Syntax error: Unterminated quoted string”, because `keepOpenScript` has double quotes of its own, so every iTerm2 launch was broken and not only awkward arguments. Not run on a Mac.
- Linux restart (#157): the arguments are tested (`restartArguments` drops every `bldesk:` link and keeps flags such as `--user-data-dir`). The restart itself needs a real update and was not run.
- Mock links (#237): `links.pages` is present on every list route and validates against the reference; other differences in the mock's answers (an image's `type`, a domain's `id`, a VPC member's `resource_id`, a sample set) are not in this change.
- Android, on the emulator (debug builds of main as BASE and this branch as FIXED, mock API): the update channel set to Beta reads Stable again after the app is closed and opened on BASE and Beta on FIXED. `launchNativeTerminal` to a host with no SSH app answers `{success: true}` on BASE and, on FIXED, `{success: false}` with “BLDesk couldn't tell that an app opened the ssh:// link…”; with a scheme an app handles (https, test build only) FIXED answers `{success: true}`.
- Dependabot alert 3 (uuid < 11.1.1, via `@capacitor/cli` → `xcode@3.0.1`, development only): `overrides` for `xcode`'s `uuid` and the lock entry edited by hand, because regenerating the lock with this npm rewrote 238 unrelated lines. `npm ls` shows `uuid@11.1.1` under xcode, `npm audit --package-lock-only` reports 0 vulnerabilities, `npm ci --dry-run --legacy-peer-deps` (the way CI installs) succeeds, and xcode's `require('uuid').v4()` works with 11.1.1.
- `docs/AUTO_UPDATE.md` (#163) was rewritten from the code: the workflow (read-only build jobs, one publish job with `contents: write`, a draft release then published), the `.deb` and AppImage updaters, the macOS zip path (not Squirrel), Android's own check, and that betas are full releases with `latest*.yml` so Stable is offered them (#166, still a decision). The same claim is corrected in `AGENTS.md`, the `release.yml` header and `updater.ts`.
- Not checked: macOS (the iTerm2 launcher, the updater), a physical phone, the live API and a real update restart on Linux.

## Boot into Rescue Mode stays, as a recorded exception (2 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.17, #129)

Branch: `fix/rescue-mode-exception`. One help page changed (`server-recovery`). The sources are `UNPUBLISHED_ACTIONS` and `UNPUBLISHED_ACTION_NOTE` in `actionLabels.ts`, `handleAction` in `ServerDetails.tsx`, `AGENTS.md` ("Accepted exceptions") and `scripts/gui-test/check-requests.mjs`.

Service fact, from `openapi.json` only: the `enable_rescue_mode` action is not in the public reference, so nothing there says what it does or promises it will stay. The maintainer decided to keep it.

| Claim | Decided by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `server-recovery.md`: the confirmation also says “This action is not in BinaryLane's public API reference, so its behaviour is not documented there and it could change or stop working.”; BLDesk keeps Boot into Rescue Mode, it is the one action the reference does not list, and what it does to the server is not described there | `unpublishedActionNotes` in `handleAction` | New. Checked by `check-help-guards.mjs`. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (with the guards and the unit tests).
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, the installed 1.0.62-beta.17 as BASE next to this branch as FIXED. The Recovery tab's “Boot into Rescue Mode”: BASE's confirmation reads “Submits "Enable Rescue Mode" to BinaryLane.” and FIXED adds the note above, and the button has a tooltip saying the action is not in the reference. Both send the request once on confirm.
- `check-requests.mjs`, on a synthetic log with `enable_rescue_mode`, an invented action and `reboot`: the invented one is reported invalid, `reboot` is checked, and `enable_rescue_mode` is counted as an accepted exception instead of being reported.
- Not checked: what the action does on a real server (it is not documented in the reference) and the live API.

## The skip reason for Archived servers (6 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.23)

Branch: `fix/palette-archived-skip-reason`. No help page changed. The `reason` for an archived server is set in `partitionByStatus` (`lib/commands.ts`) and shown by `CommandPalette.tsx` and, through `terminalSessions.ts`, by `BroadcastPanel.tsx`. It was missed when the Archived wording followed the 0.43.3 reference (the Archived rows in the 23 September section above).

Service fact, from `openapi.json` only: `ServerStatus` describes `archive` as "The server is powered off due to non payment."

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “skipped — archived (non-payment)”, on the palette's Skip row when a power command matches an archived server | `CommandPalette.tsx`: `skipped — ${m.reason}`, with the reason from `partitionByStatus` for `status === 'archive'` | Changed from “skipped — archived (cancelled or unpaid)”, to follow the reference's definition. The other reason, “still being built”, is unchanged. |
| “Skipped (1): archived-old-01 (archived (non-payment))”, in Broadcast SSH's target preview, and the same text in the broadcast confirmation's note | `BroadcastPanel.tsx`: `${s.server.name} (${s.reason})`, the same reason | Changed with the row above (one string). The confirmation's note was not opened: reaching it needs a command and Run broadcast, which starts SSH. |

### Checks performed

- All of CI's steps pass: `npm run typecheck` (with every guard and test), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- The compiled app against a build of `main` (`3d8e239`): main and preload are byte-identical, and in the renderer one file differs, the main chunk, in this one string.
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, with a temporary archived server added to a local copy of the mock (never committed). It checks the reason against ServerStatus's own description of `archive` in `openapi.json`: the palette with `shutdown` and with `start` on the archived server, and with `poweroff` on it and another server (that one stays eligible), and the Broadcast SSH preview with those two names typed. Before the change, 0 of 4 pass and each shows “cancelled or unpaid”. After, 4 of 4. No write requests were sent, no command was typed in Broadcast and no SSH tab opened.
- The same harness suites as for the Archived rows above, on this branch: the 19 UI steps (the same 23 action requests, the same SHA-256 as the earlier run), the 12 further action cases (the same 30 write requests) and the 13 firewall and template cases (the same 11), and the 4 Archived wording checks (4 of 4). `node --test scripts/gui-test/mock.test.mjs` passes 16 of 16.
- Android, on a physical tablet (shown wide enough for the desktop layout): a throwaway debug build of this commit with the harness README's test-only edits, driven over the DevTools protocol, with the same temporary archived server. The palette, opened with its on-screen Search button: `shutdown`, `start` and `poweroff` with another server show “skipped — archived (non-payment)”, 3 of 3. The 4 Archived wording checks: 4 of 4. The 15 firewall, template and action cases send the same requests as on desktop (the template apply's two requests are byte-identical, 1,503 and 621 characters), except that Power On went to the temporary archived server with it in the mock; against the mock without it, it sent the same request as on desktop. Broadcast SSH is not on Android: the Embedded SSH tab, which holds it, only exists where the desktop's pty bridge does.
- Not checked: the broadcast confirmation's note (see above), a phone, and the live API.

## Where weekly and monthly backups are set, on the Backups page (7 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.25, #301)

Branch: `fix/backups-page-weekly-monthly-pointer`. One help page changed (`backups`). The sources are the banner in `BackupManager.tsx` (`isAutoBackupEnabled`, `weeklyOrMonthlyOnly`), `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` (the “Backups” label and its Daily, Weekly and Monthly rows) and `Sidebar.tsx` (the Change Plan item among a server's pages).

Service fact, from `openapi.json` only (0.43.3): `enable_backups` is “Enable Two Daily Backups for an Existing Server” and takes no options. The retention counts (`daily_backups`, `weekly_backups`, `monthly_backups`) are options of the `resize` action, which is what Change Plan sends, so the Backups page has no way to set the weekly or monthly ones.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “BinaryLane takes an automated nightly backup during your scheduled maintenance window. Weekly and monthly backups are set in the server's Change Plan, under Backups.” | `BackupManager.tsx`, the banner's text when `isAutoBackupEnabled` | Changed: the second sentence is new. |
| “This server keeps weekly or monthly backups and no daily ones. Daily, weekly and monthly backups are set in the server's Change Plan, under Backups.” | the same text when `weeklyOrMonthlyOnly` | Changed from “… Daily backups are set in Change Plan.”, which named only the daily ones. |
| “Automated backups are currently turned off for this server. The button enables two daily backups; weekly and monthly backups are set in the server's Change Plan, under Backups.” | the same text otherwise | Changed: it said only that automated backups are turned off. “The button enables two daily backups” is `enable_backups`, which the reference describes as above. |
| “the server's Change Plan, under Backups” | `Sidebar.tsx`: the Change Plan item in a server's pages; `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`: the “Backups” label over the Daily, Weekly and Monthly rows | Checked in Electron (below). |
| `backups.md`, section “Disabling automatic backups”: a new paragraph and the three quotes above | the strings above | New. The quotes are whole strings the app shows, checked by `check-help-guards.mjs`. “One of the server's own pages in the sidebar” is the sidebar's list of a server's pages. |

### Checks performed

- All of CI's steps pass (23): `npm run typecheck` (with every guard and test), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- The compiled app against a build of `main` (`d1d58ac`): main and preload are byte-identical. In the renderer one file differs, the main chunk, in the one line that holds the banner's text and in the bundled `backups` help page.
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, with a temporary weekly-only server added to a local copy of the mock (never committed): the Backups page for a server with daily backups, one with none and the weekly-only one, on the account-wide page and on the server's own Backups tab. Each banner reads exactly its quote in `backups.md`, with the same state and buttons as before (“Remove Daily Backups”, none, “Enable Nightly Backups”). The sidebar has Change Plan, and the Change Plan panel has the “Backups” label over Daily, Weekly and Monthly. Against a build of `main`, the three banners fail (their old text) and the Change Plan check passes. Against this branch, 4 of 4. No write requests were sent.
- Layout of the banner, for those three servers at the widths of a 1280 and a 1024 window at 80, 125 and 150% zoom (1600, 1024, 853 and 683 css px, by viewport emulation) and at a phone's 390: the text wraps and nothing passes the edge of the viewport, with no sideways scroll. The banner is not a dialog, navigation or a dense table.
- The same harness suites as for #297, on this branch: the 19 UI steps (the same 23 action requests, the same SHA-256), the 12 further action cases (the same 30 write requests) and the 13 firewall and template cases (the same 11), and the 4 Archived wording checks (4 of 4). `node --test scripts/gui-test/mock.test.mjs` passes 16 of 16.
- Android, on a physical tablet (shown wide enough for the desktop layout): a throwaway debug build of this commit (Android SDK platform 36, JDK 21, the Gradle wrapper) with a different application id, using the harness README's test-only edits and `adb reverse` to reach the mock, with the fictitious token, driven over the DevTools protocol. The same four checks, with the same temporary weekly-only server: 4 of 4, with no write requests, and the banner stays inside the screen with no sideways scroll. The 4 Archived wording checks: 4 of 4. The 15 firewall, template and action cases send the same requests as on desktop (the template apply's two requests are byte-identical, 1,503 and 621 characters). The app was uninstalled from the tablet afterwards.
- Not checked: the live API, and a phone.

## Showing and changing when backups run (7 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.25, #301)

Branch: `feat/backup-schedule`. Two help pages changed: `backups` (a new “Backup schedule” section, the three banner quotes reworded, and “nightly” becoming “daily”) and `server-backups` (one pointer to the new section). The sources are `BackupManager.tsx` (the banner, the Backup Schedule card, the Change Backup Schedule form, `handleChangeSchedule` and `handleToggleAuto`), `lib/backupSchedule.ts` (which values apply to a server, how they read, and the request body), `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` (the “Backups” label and its Daily, Weekly and Monthly rows) and `api/queries.ts` (`useServerActionWithHandoff`, `readQueuedAction`, `ACCEPTED_WITHOUT_ACTION`).

Service facts, from `openapi.json` only (0.43.3):

- `Server.backup_settings` (`BackupSettings`, required on a server) holds `backup_hour_of_day`: “The hour of the day, in Australia/Sydney time for all server regions, that backups will be scheduled. This is an approximate value.” `backup_day_of_week`: “If weekly backups are enabled the day of the week, as an Australia/Sydney calendar day for all server regions, that the weekly backup will occur. Sunday is day 0.” `backup_day_of_month`: “If monthly backups are enabled the day of the month, as an Australia/Sydney calendar day for all server regions, that the monthly backup will occur.”
- `change_backup_schedule` (`ChangeBackupSchedule`, “Change the Backup Schedule of a Server”) takes `backup_hour_of_day` (0 to 23), `backup_day_of_week` (0 to 6, “Sunday is 0, Monday is 1 etc.”) and `backup_day_of_month` (1 to 28). Each is optional and nullable: “Do not provide a value to keep the current setting.” The actions endpoint documents 200 with an action and 202 “Accepted” with no content.
- `selected_size_options.daily_backups`: “The number of retained daily backups. e.g. if this is '2' we will store two daily backups, so each daily backup will be retained for two days before being overwritten.” `enable_backups` is “Enable Two Daily Backups for an Existing Server” and takes no options.
- The reference never uses “maintenance window” for backups. Its only uses of “maintenance” are a host's status page and a server's `is_under_maintenance`.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “BinaryLane takes automated daily backups. The Backup Schedule below shows when they run. Weekly and monthly backups are set in the Backups section of the server's Change Plan.” | `BackupManager.tsx`, the banner's text when `isAutoBackupEnabled` | Changed from “BinaryLane takes an automated nightly backup during your scheduled maintenance window. Weekly and monthly backups are set in the server's Change Plan, under Backups.” “Maintenance window” is not in the reference (above), so it is gone. “Daily” replaces “nightly”: the hour can be set now, and the reference calls them daily backups. “The Backup Schedule below” is the card, which is rendered whenever this banner reads Enabled, and says when BinaryLane did not report the schedule. |
| “This server keeps weekly or monthly backups and no daily ones. Daily, weekly and monthly backups are set in the Backups section of the server's Change Plan.” | the same text when `weeklyOrMonthlyOnly` | Changed from “… in the server's Change Plan, under Backups.” |
| “Automated backups are currently turned off for this server. The button enables daily backups and keeps two of them; weekly and monthly backups are set in the Backups section of the server's Change Plan.” | the same text otherwise | Changed from “The button enables two daily backups; … under Backups.” “Two daily backups” read as two a day; the reference's `daily_backups` is the number retained, two being what `enable_backups` sets (above). |
| “the Backups section of the server's Change Plan” | `Sidebar.tsx`: the Change Plan item among a server's pages; `ChangePlanPanel.tsx`: the “Backups” label over the Daily, Weekly and Monthly selects and the offsite checkbox | The panel has no separate heading element: the section is that label and the rows under it. |
| “Automated Daily Backups”, “No daily”, “Enable Daily Backups” | the banner's title, its chip and its button | Changed from “Automated Nightly Backups”, “No nightly” and “Enable Nightly Backups”. The enable dialog's summary is changed the same way, from “BinaryLane takes a nightly backup on the server's schedule.” to “BinaryLane takes daily backups on the server's schedule.”; no help page quotes it. `README.md` and `FEATURES.md` said “nightly schedule”; they now say “backup schedule” (and FEATURES.md “daily-backups toggle”). |
| “Backups run at about {hour}, Australia/Sydney time.”, “Weekly backups run on {weekday}.”, “Monthly backups run on the {ordinal}.” | `BackupManager.tsx`, the Backup Schedule card, from `backup_settings`; `lib/backupSchedule.ts` for the wording | New. “About” is the reference's “approximate”. The hour reads “midnight”, “noon”, “2 am”, “2 pm”; the weekday is 0 for Sunday, as the reference says; the day of the month is “1st” to “28th”. The weekday line is shown only while the server keeps weekly backups and the day line only while it keeps monthly ones, as the reference's “If weekly backups are enabled” and “If monthly backups are enabled” say. A server that keeps no daily, weekly or monthly backups has no schedule, so no card. When `selected_size_options` is missing only the hour line is shown, since the hour applies to every kind. |
| “BinaryLane did not report when this server's backups run.” | the card, when `backup_settings` is missing or one of its three values is not a whole number | New. The reference requires `backup_settings`, so this is for an answer that does not follow it, and the card then has no button. |
| “Change Backup Schedule”, “Hour of the day” (“Australia/Sydney time, approximate”), “Day of the week” (“for weekly backups”), “Day of the month” (“for monthly backups”) | the form (`Modal`) in `BackupManager.tsx` | New. The ranges are the reference's: the selectors offer 0 to 23, Sunday to Saturday and 1 to 28, and the server's current value as well when the API reports one outside its range, so the form never shows a value that is not set. The button is off until a value differs. |
| “Changes when BinaryLane runs this server's scheduled backups. The hour is approximate, and the days are Australia/Sydney calendar days.” | `handleChangeSchedule`, `summary`; the title is “Change backup schedule”; severity `normal` | New. Both claims are the reference's. `normal` because the change is undone by changing it back and nothing is removed. The table (`changes`) lists only the values that differ, and only those are sent. |
| `backups.md`, new section “Backup schedule”, and its quotes | the strings above | New. Its quotes are whole strings the app shows, checked by `check-help-guards.mjs`. “Sunday is the first weekday” is the reference's day 0. “The reference does not say how a change interacts with a backup that is already due” is true of `ChangeBackupSchedule` and `BackupSettings`, which say nothing about it. |
| `backups.md`: “If BinaryLane accepts it without giving an action to follow, the entry stays “Submitted” … BLDesk then reads the server list again, and the card shows the schedule that list reports. If BinaryLane applies the change a moment after accepting it, the card can show the old schedule until BLDesk next reads the list, which it does regularly.” | `handleChangeSchedule` (`state: 'accepted'`, `ACCEPTED_WITHOUT_ACTION`); `useServerActionWithHandoff`'s `onSettled`, which invalidates the server list and re-reads it straight after the 202, without waiting; `useServers`, which reads the list again every 15 seconds (`refetchInterval`) | The History entry's detail is `ACCEPTED_WITHOUT_ACTION`. The added sentence is what the code does: the re-read after the 202 does not wait for BinaryLane to apply the change, so a late change shows at the next regular read. Observed in Electron against a local copy of the mock that applies the change 4 seconds after its 202 (see Checks performed). That the live API answers `change_backup_schedule` this way is the claim in `readQueuedAction`'s comment; it was not checked against a live account (see Not checked). |
| `backups.md`: “The form shows the schedule BinaryLane reports now: a value you have not changed follows it if the schedule is changed elsewhere while the form is open, and only the values you changed are sent.” | `BackupManager.tsx`: `edits` holds only what the user chose and `draft` is `scheduleDraft(schedule, edits)`; `lib/backupSchedule.ts`: `setScheduleEdit` (choosing what the server reports clears the edit), `scheduleDraft`; `useServers`, which reads the list every 15 seconds | New, after a review of this change. The form used to hold a copy of the schedule taken when it opened and compare it with the live one, so a value changed elsewhere meanwhile (mPanel, say) counted as an edit and was sent back. Both halves are the code: untouched values come from the live schedule, and the request body is built from the fields that differ between the two. A value the user chose stays theirs if the server moves under it; the confirmation shows it going from the server's current value to theirs. |
| `backups.md`, section “Disabling automatic backups”: “reads No daily”, “enabling daily backups is for a server that has none”, “in its Backups section” | the banner chip and button; `ChangePlanPanel.tsx` | Changed from “No nightly” and “nightly”, and from “under Backups”. |
| `server-backups.md`, new section “Backup schedule” | the same Backups view pinned to a server (`BackupManager.tsx`) | New. A one-line pointer, with a link to the `backups` section. |
| `REQUIREMENTS.md`, the Backup Schedule row | `queries.ts` and `openapi.json` | Fixed: it named `backup_hour?` and `schedule?`, which are not fields of `ChangeBackupSchedule`; it now names its three. |

### Checks performed

- All of CI's steps pass (24): `npm run typecheck` (with every guard and test, including the new `test:backup-schedule`), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build`.
- `backups.md`, the paragraph under “Choose the right operation”: the sentence “Retention and scheduling belong to BinaryLane” is replaced by one that says the hour and days are shown and changed on this page, and the link text now reads “its automated backups article” (the same BinaryLane support article as before).
- Repo-wide search of the tracked files for `nightly`, `maintenance window`, `under Backups`, `two daily backup` and `Retention and scheduling`. What is left is not about this page or is deliberately kept: dated records in this file and `CHANGELOG.md` (release history, not changed); a fixture backup's name in `mock.mjs` and the showcase launcher; `starterTemplates.ts` (a PostgreSQL template's own `pg_dump`); the worked example in `backups.md` that says a server keeps two daily backups (retention, as above); and the disable dialog's note “BinaryLane does this only when the server has the two daily backups that enabling automated backups creates.”, which uses the reference's own words for `disable_backups` (“i.e. two daily backups”) and was not part of what #303 raised.
- The compiled app against a build of `main` (`29f456f`, which has #303): main and preload are byte-identical. In the renderer the main chunk is the only file whose content differs (14.4 kB larger: the schedule code, the changed strings and the bundled help pages); the other files differ only in the hashed chunk names they import. Capacitor's `webDir` is `out/renderer`, so that is the Android app's web assets too.
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test`, the harness mock with fictitious token, and three temporary servers (weekly only, daily only, monthly only) added to a local copy of the mock and never committed. Ten cases: the card for a server with daily, weekly and monthly backups, weekly only, daily only, monthly only and none (no card), each read against the mock's own `backup_settings` and formatted by an independent implementation; the form's selectors (24 hours, Sunday first, 28 days, the button off until a value differs); a change of the hour (the confirmation reads “Hour of the day 2 am→3 am”, one request with only `backup_hour_of_day`, History has “Change backup schedule” as Submitted); the card following the change; all three values at once; only the weekday sent for a weekly-only server; a change from a server's own Backups tab (no picker, the confirmation names that server, and only that server changes); no change sends no request, and cancelling the confirmation keeps the form open; a refused request keeps the form open and History records Failed; and the case in the next bullet. Against this branch 10 of 10; against a build of `main` 0 of 10, because the card is not there.
- The banner case: the three banners and the Change Plan check, on the account-wide page and on a server's own Backups tab: each banner reads exactly its quote in `backups.md`, with the state and button above. Against this branch 4 of 4; against a build of `main` 1 of 4 (its banner has the old title and text; the Change Plan check passes).
- Layout, with the real window and the real zoom, not viewport emulation: the main process resizes the window to 1024 × 680 and to 1280 × 840 and the app's own zoom shortcuts (`webContents.sendInputEvent`) take it to 80, 125 and 150%. At each of the six, the Backup Schedule card, the three banners, the form and the confirmation stay inside the window with no sideways scroll, and the buttons of the form (Cancel, Change Schedule) and of the confirmation (Cancel, Confirm) are reachable (the element at each button's centre is the button). The zoom stops at 80% and 150% on further presses and resets to 100%. The navigation columns are not changed by this PR, so the last-navigation-item check was not repeated. The banners at the phone width of 390 px (viewport emulation) wrap and nothing passes the edge.
- `node --test scripts/gui-test/mock.test.mjs` passes 17 of 17 (the new test: `backup_settings` is reported; `change_backup_schedule` changes only what is sent, validates the ranges and answers 202 with no body). `check-requests.mjs` finds every request body the app sent in the desktop and the tablet runs valid against `openapi.json`, `change_backup_schedule` included.
- Edit only the hour while the weekday is changed elsewhere (a call straight at the mock's API, as mPanel would): in Electron, the form's weekday selector followed the change at the next regular read of the server list (about 10 s), the hour stayed as edited, and the app sent one request holding only `backup_hour_of_day`, so the weekday stayed changed. On a build of the commit before the fix the selector did not follow, the app sent `backup_hour_of_day` and `backup_day_of_week: 0`, and the weekday went back. `test:backup-schedule` has the same case on the helpers (the edited hour, a refresh with a changed weekday, the body holds only `backup_hour_of_day`), and fails if the form goes back to keeping a copy of the schedule; it also covers putting a value back and a value the user chose staying theirs when the server moves.
- The card's Change Schedule button is the solid primary button (white on blue with a shadow, as Take Backup and the form's own button are), changed after a review from a tinted one (blue text on a pale blue ground with a blue border), which read poorly in the dark theme. Looked at in Electron in the dark and the light theme, and the computed colours of the two buttons (Change Schedule and Take Backup) are the same in both. Nothing in the help pages quotes it. The banner's own buttons (Enable Daily Backups, Remove Daily Backups) are not changed.
- A schedule change that BinaryLane applies late, in Electron against a local copy of the mock (never committed) that applies `change_backup_schedule` 4 seconds after answering 202: the form closed 39 ms after Confirm with the card still reading the old hour (and the mock still holding it), and the card read the new hour 15.2 seconds after Confirm with no action from the user, at the next regular read of the server list. One request was sent. This is the mock being late on purpose; how late the live API is, if at all, is not known.
- The same harness suites as for #297, compared with that run by the content of their requests: the 19 UI steps (the same 23 action requests, the same SHA-256), the 12 further action cases (the same 30 write requests, the same SHA-256) and the 13 firewall and template cases (the same 11, the same SHA-256), and the 4 Archived wording checks (4 of 4).
- Android, on a physical tablet (shown wide enough for the desktop layout): a throwaway debug build of this branch's app and help files (Android SDK platform 36, JDK 21, the Gradle wrapper) with a different application id, using the harness README's test-only edits and `adb reverse` to reach the mock, with the fictitious token, driven over the DevTools protocol. The same ten schedule cases (10 of 10, the own-tab change and the case above included: the weekday followed after 6.1 s and the request held only the hour), the four banner cases (4 of 4), the form and the confirmation inside the screen with their buttons reachable, and the three banners inside the screen, the weekly-only one (the longest text) included. Real touch (`adb shell input tap`, not scripted clicks): Change Schedule opens the form; Cancel closes it with nothing sent; the form's button opens the confirmation; Cancel there keeps the form and sends nothing; Confirm sends one request holding only the hour, closes the form and the card reads the new hour. The 4 Archived wording checks: 4 of 4. The same 15 firewall, template and action cases send the same write requests as on desktop (13 of 15 the same in the plain comparison; the template apply's two requests are byte-identical in full, 1,503 and 621 characters; Power On went to the temporary archived server with it in the mock, and against the mock without it sent the same request as on desktop). The app was uninstalled from the tablet afterwards.

### Not checked

- The live API. Whether a server's list returns `backup_settings` as the reference says; that `change_backup_schedule` is answered with 202 and no action (the claim in `readQueuedAction`'s comment); whether the list shows the new schedule straight after a 202 (if it does not, the card lags until the next regular read, as above); what BinaryLane sets for the weekday and day of the month of a server that starts keeping weekly or monthly backups; and how a change interacts with a backup that is already due, which the reference does not say.
- A phone, and macOS and Linux fonts.

## Servers tile view lists every address (7 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.25, #296)

Branch: `fix/tile-view-show-all-ips`. No new runtime dependencies. No help page changed: `servers.md` says only that there are grid and list layouts, and `server-overview.md` describes the Overview's Network & Addressing pane, not a tile. The words below are on screen, in the tile (grid) card of `ServerList.tsx`, and come from the app's own labels, from what mPanel's server list shows (its private address column carries a link to the VPC beneath the address) and from the API's `type` and `vpc_id` fields.

Service fact, from `openapi.json` only (version 0.43.3, `NetworkType`): `public` is "A public (internet accessible) network." and `private` is "A private (non internet accessible) network." The reference does not say which `public` entry is first or that the first is a primary one; "Secondary" is BLDesk's word for the public entries after the first, as in the Overview.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Public IP:` | the first row of the tile's address block: the first `type: 'public'` entry of `networks.v4` | New label on the address the tile already showed without one. It is the Servers table's column heading “Public IP”, so the two layouts of one list use the same words; the Overview calls the same address “Public IPv4”. The row is always there, with “None” when the server has no public IPv4. |
| `Secondary IP:` / `Secondary IPs:` | the second row: every `type: 'public'` entry after the first, singular or plural by count | New. Follows the Overview's “Secondary IPv4” / “Secondary IPv4s” (`ServerDetails.tsx`, Network & Addressing), with “IP” as in the table; Change Plan says “secondary address” for the same entries. Left out when there are none. |
| `Private IP:` / `Private IPs:` | the last row of a server that has no `vpc_id`: every `type: 'private'` entry, singular or plural by count | New. The table's column is “Private IP / VPC”, and mPanel's list says “Private IP”; the Overview says “Private IPv4” / “Private IPv4s”. Left out when the server has no private address. |
| `VPC IP:` / `VPC IPs:` | the same row for a server that has a `vpc_id` | New wording for the private address of a server in a VPC (there is one private row, and its name follows the server's `vpc_id`). Nothing in the app is called “VPC IP”: the nearest are the Network tab's history label “Change VPC IPv4 <address> → <address>” for changing that address (`ServerNetwork.tsx`) and “VPC address” in the VPCs page's Detach confirmation (`VpcManager.tsx`, quoted in `vpcs.md`), both for the same address; the Overview and the Network Map say “Private IPv4” for it. The row is always there for a server in a VPC, with “None” (the table's own word and style) if no private address is listed. |
| the VPC's name, with its network icon, on its own line under the addresses of the same row | `VpcBadge`, the component the table's “Private IP / VPC” column uses, in the `VPC IP` row only | New on the tile. Same icon and same name (from the VPC list by `vpc_id`), and “VPC #id” while the list is loading or if the id is not in it. The table also puts it under the private address; here it is a line of the same row (no divider between it and the addresses), starting at the row's left edge under its label, and a long name is cut with an ellipsis at the card's width. A click on it opens the server, as it does in the table (it is not a link in either). |
| `None` | the Public IP row when the server has no `type: 'public'` entry, for example a server in a VPC whose public addresses were removed, or one still being built; the `VPC IP` row of a server in a VPC with no private address | The table's own word and style (`text-[#6c757d] text-[11px]`). Before, a tile for a server in a VPC with no public address showed its private address with no label, as if it were the public one, and a server with no address at all showed no address line. |
| `Copy <address>`, the tooltip and accessible name of each copy button in the tile | the buttons beside every address in those rows | New. The tile's buttons had no tooltip or accessible name; each now says which address it copies. The table's buttons still say “Copy IP” and are not touched. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (every guard and test), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build` pass on this commit.
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test` with a throwaway copy of its mock (kept outside the repo, fictitious data: every server has a private IPv4, on a private network for a server outside a VPC and a 10.x address with its `vpc_id` for one in a VPC), a build of `main` (`616b6e5`) next to this branch, the same steps on both. For 24 servers (1 to 8 public addresses with and without a VPC, two VPC IPs, two private IPs outside a VPC, private only in a VPC, no address at all with and without a VPC, a VPC with no private address listed, no VPC and no private address, an unknown VPC id, a 60 character VPC name), the table's Public IP and Private IP / VPC columns, the API's addresses and the tile agree: the same addresses, the same “None” (same colour and size), the same VPC text, the private row called “VPC IP” / “VPC IPs” exactly for the servers that have a `vpc_id`, and no “VPC:” row (0 mismatches). The tile leaves out an empty Secondary row, and an empty private row of a server outside a VPC, where the table shows “None” in its Private column. On `main` the tile showed one address for 22 of the 24 and none for the two with no address.
- The VPC's name, read from the page: for every server in a VPC it is a child of the private row, not of its address line, below the last address, 4 px under the address line and 8 px above the row's bottom, with no divider of its own, and its icon is at the same left edge as the row's label. A server in a VPC with no private address listed shows “VPC IP: None” with the name below it.
- A click on the VPC name opens the server in both views, for a known name and for “VPC #999”; with the VPC list delayed, both views show “VPC #901” first and the name after.
- Search by address finds the same servers in both views, and all nine searches show the address that matched in the tile; on `main` three of the nine did, six did not.
- The copy buttons put the right address on the clipboard (nine cases, the clipboard was saved and put back), only the clicked card shows the tick (two servers hold the same private address), a click does not open the server, a server with no address has no copy button, and every one of the 122 copy buttons in the tile (all 49 tiles) has the title and accessible name `Copy <its address>`. All 272 requests the app made in the run were GETs; the only other two were the harness's own control calls to the mock.
- Spacing, measured with `getBoundingClientRect` on all 49 tiles at 1280 (three columns) and at 375 px, light and dark: 8 px above and below every divider (header to the CPU/RAM/DISK block, that block to the first row, between rows, and the last row to the footer's divider). The one difference is deliberate: the footer (region and SSH) is pinned to the bottom of its card, as it is on `main`, so in a card that is shorter than the tallest in its grid row the extra space is above the footer's divider (10 of the 49 tiles at 1280, in 6 of the 17 grid rows, 20 to 73 px), and the 8 px under the divider is unchanged. In the tallest card of every row, and in every tile at 375 px (one column, so there is no extra space), the footer's divider is 8 px under the last row. The same with local tags on three tiles: 8 px from the header to the tags, and 8 / 8 around the divider under them (up to 125 px of extra space above a footer divider, in 5 of the 7 rows).
- Equal height and the footer: the grid has no `align-items` override (computed `normal`, so every card fills its grid area), and the card keeps its `flex flex-col justify-between`, so its footer stays at the bottom. In the 49 tiles at 1280 (16 rows of three cards and one of one) no row has cards of different height, and in every row the footers' bottom edges are at the same y, each at the bottom of its card's content box; in the 6 rows whose cards would differ without stretching, the shorter cards have the extra space between the last row and the footer's divider, up to 73 px (up to 125 px with the tags). At 375 px every tile is its own height.
- Layout, real Electron zoom through `webContents.sendInputEvent` (80, 100, 125, 150%) in a 1280x840 and a 1024x680 window, light and dark (16 measurements: three columns, two, and one in 1024x680 at 150%), 800 and 900 px wide viewports (two columns), and an emulated 375 px viewport (with 20 tiles and with all 49), light and dark: no horizontal scroll of the page or the scrolling pane; every label, “None”, address, VPC icon and name and copy icon inside its card's content box; none overlapping another; every button reachable (the topmost element at its icon); no row of cards with cards of different height; in every one of the 24 measurements the footers of a grid row end at the same y, each at the bottom of its card, and every grid row has at least one card whose footer's divider is 8 px under its last row. The 60 character VPC name is cut with an ellipsis on its own line inside the card (267 px wide at 375 px, 269 to 275 at 1280, 189 in the three-column 1024 layout).
- Touch area, by hit-testing around each icon: 12 x 12 px on desktop (13 x 13 at 80%), as on `main`; on the phone layout (375 px, and 1024x680 at 150%, which is 693 css px) at least 28 x 20 px (12 x 12 on `main`).
- Not checked: a real phone, macOS and Linux fonts, and the live API.

## Create Server without a hostname (14 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.27, #310)

Branch: `fix/create-server-optional-hostname`. No new runtime dependencies. One help page changed, `servers.md` ("Create and organise"), by added sentences. The strings below are rendered by `CreateServerModal.tsx`. What the form does with the hostname (the request's `name`, the History entry's target and its Hostname row, the History detail and target once the server exists, the name handed to `onCreated`) and whether cloud-init is sent comes from the helpers in `lib/createServerHostname.ts` (`hostnameFields`, `cloudInitToSend`, `createdDetail`, `createdTarget`, `createdName`), which `npm run test:create-hostname` covers, and the History entry is written by `recordChange` and `updateChange` in `handleSubmit`.

Service fact, from `openapi.json` only (version 0.43.3, `CreateServerRequest`): `name` is not required, and its description is "The hostname of your server, such as vps01.yourcompany.com. If not provided, the server will be created with a random name." The reference does not say when BinaryLane picks the name, only that the server is created with one. BLDesk therefore sends no `name` for a blank hostname and takes the name from the response (`server.name`).

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `servers.md`, "Create and organise": "The hostname is optional: leave it blank and BinaryLane gives the server a name of its own." | `hostnameFields` (`request`), spread into the create body by `handleSubmit`: `name` is left out when the trimmed hostname is empty; `CreateServerRequest.name` above | Changed. Before, the form stopped with "Enter a hostname for the server." and sent nothing. |
| `servers.md`: "When the hostname is blank and Cloud-init User Data is enabled with text in it, a note under the Hostname field says that BLDesk does not update a hostname already written into the cloud-init, including one a template filled in for the hostname variable." | The amber note directly under the Hostname field, so it shows in the simple layout and in View All: shown when `host.blank && userData`, where `userData` is `cloudInitToSend(acceptsUserData, cloudInitOn, cloudInit)`, the same value the request sends as `user_data`. So it shows exactly when cloud-init is going to be sent with no hostname: not with the box unticked, an empty box, or an image that does not take cloud-init. A template fills `{{hostname}}` when it is applied (`prefillFromTemplate` and `renderCloudInit` in `serverTemplates.ts`); the create form substitutes nothing, so what is already written in the cloud-init stays as it is | New. The note says only what the code does. It does not claim what name BinaryLane will pick, because the reference says no more than "a random name". |
| `Optional. Such as vps01.yourcompany.com. It does not matter if you do not yet own this domain name. If you leave it blank, BinaryLane names the server.` | The hint of the Hostname field | Changed. It began "Such as vps01.yourcompany.com." and had no last sentence. |
| `No hostname is set, so BinaryLane gives the server a name of its own. BLDesk does not update a hostname already written into the cloud-init, including one a template filled in for {{hostname}}.` | The amber note above | New. It stays out of the way: hidden as soon as a hostname is typed, and not shown when no cloud-init is going to be sent. |
| History: the entry's target is `New server`, its table has a `Hostname` row, `left blank, so BinaryLane names the server`, and its outcome is `BinaryLane accepted the request; server #<id> (<name>) is being built.` Once BinaryLane has returned the server, the entry's target becomes that server, by name and `#<id>` | `hostnameFields` (`historyTarget`, `changes`) for the target and the row, passed to `recordChange` before the request; `createdDetail` and `createdTarget` for the outcome and the new target, passed to `updateChange` after it, in `handleSubmit`. `updateChange` accepts a `target` in its patch (`lib/changelog.ts`); both the main-process store and the local fallback merge the patch into the entry. A failed create, or a response with no server id, leaves `New server` | New, for a blank hostname only. With a hostname the entry is as before: that hostname as its target, no Hostname row, and the outcome without the name in brackets. |
| Applying a template: "The new server needs a hostname." | `TemplatesView.tsx`, unchanged | Unchanged, rechecked, still true. A template's `{{hostname}}` is built in, so that dialog still asks for one. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (every guard and test, including the new `test:create-hostname`), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build` pass on this commit.
- `test:create-hostname`, ten tests: the reference does not require `name` on a create request; a blank hostname (empty, spaces, tab, newline, a no-break space) gives a request with no `name` key at all; a typed one is trimmed; spaces inside one are kept; a blank one is "New server" in History with the Hostname row and a typed one has neither; cloud-init is sent only when the image takes it, the box is ticked and it holds text; the History detail names the returned server only for a blank hostname, and a response with no server or no id is still an accepted request; the History target becomes the returned server for a blank hostname and is left alone for a typed one, a missing response or one with no id; the name handed to `onCreated` is never empty; and a source check that the form builds the request and the History entry from the helpers and that the note sits under the Hostname field and shares the request's cloud-init test. Each of eight deliberate breakages (a new target for a typed hostname, no guard for a missing server id, an empty name for `onCreated`, cloud-init sent for an image that does not take it, the name in brackets for a typed hostname, an empty target name, the request no longer using the shared cloud-init test, and the note using a different test from the request) makes it fail.
- Real Electron through `scripts/gui-test` (fictitious data), the Add a Cloud Server form submitted four ways. A blank hostname and a hostname of spaces each sent a create request with no `name`, the form closed, the list showed the generated name, and there was no hostname message. A hostname with spaces around it was sent trimmed. With the terms unticked nothing was sent and the form said what was missing. Applying a template with the hostname blank still said "The new server needs a hostname." `check-requests.mjs` found 0 invalid bodies. History showed each blank-hostname entry as the server BinaryLane named, by name and #id, with the Hostname row and the outcome, and no entry left as "New server".
- The amber note, read from the page in both layouts: shown with the exact text, in the Hostname field below the input, when the hostname is blank and cloud-init is going to be sent; hidden once a hostname is typed and back when it is cleared (spaces count as blank). Not shown with Cloud-init User Data off, with its box empty or holding only spaces, with the text kept but the box unticked, or for an image that does not accept cloud-init (the form unticks the box and says so). A template that has only cloud-init, applied from the Templates tab, opened in the simple layout with the hostname filled in and no note; clearing the hostname there showed the note, and the create request had no `name` and still carried the cloud-init with the old hostname in it. With the note changed to show only in View All, three of the five checks failed, so they would have caught it.
- Layout, real Electron zoom through `webContents.sendInputEvent` (80, 125 and 150%) in a 1280x840 and a 1024x680 window, in the simple layout and in View All: in all twelve, the hint and the note are inside the viewport with no clipped text (the hint takes one or two lines, the note two), the page does not scroll sideways and Add Server is reachable. The zoom floor, cap and reset are as they were.
- The suites that exercise the create form and the other mutations (19 steps, 12 cases, 13 firewall and template cases, and the archive wording) give the same checksums as the runs before this change.
- A physical Android tablet, with a throwaway build under its own application id: twelve cases with real taps on the form's controls (the open button, the terms checkbox, Add Server, View All and Show Less, the Cloud-init checkbox and box, the Windows and Ubuntu tiles, Continue to create) and real keyboard input for the hostname and the cloud-init box. They cover the same behaviour as the desktop checks, and the note in both layouts, for an image without cloud-init and from a template that has only cloud-init, all passing. The 15 firewall, template and action cases send the same requests as the desktop; the template apply requests are byte-identical.
- Not checked: the live API (what name BinaryLane really gives a server created without one), a phone, and macOS and Linux fonts.

## Linux package updates: the password prompt no longer freezes the window (21 October 2026, after 1.0.62-beta.31)

Branch: `feat/linux-update-flow`. No new dependencies. One help page changed, `troubleshooting.md` ("Updates"), by one added paragraph; `docs/AUTO_UPDATE.md` has the full description. The sources are `installLinuxPackage`, `startLinuxSession`, `pollLinuxSession`, `takeLeftoverSession` and `onAppQuit` in `src/main/updater.ts`, the messages in `src/main/linuxInstall.ts`, and `UpdateMenu.tsx`.

| String | Rendered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `troubleshooting.md`, "Updates": "Restart to update opens your system's password prompt. BLDesk stays open behind it, and the title bar says “Waiting for password” until you answer." | `installLinuxPackage` starts `update-session.sh` and sets `installPhase: 'authorising'` without quitting; `UpdateMenu.tsx` renders the trigger label `Waiting for password` for that phase. The prompt itself is polkit's (pkexec on `install-update.sh`) | New. Before, `quitAndInstall` ran the install synchronously, so the window froze and the prompt was the generic one for `/bin/bash`. |
| "Restart to update first checks once more whether a newer version has been published; if one has, BLDesk downloads that instead and goes on to install it when it arrives." | `installLinuxPackage`: it calls `checkNow()` (at most six seconds), and if `held` no longer holds the version it started with, sets `restartWhenReady`; the `update-downloaded` handler then calls `install()` for the newer one. `UpdateMenu.tsx` renders `Checking` and the popover sentence for `installPhase: 'checking'` | New. Before, a download hours old was installed even when a newer release had been published, and the next check needed a second download, password and restart. |
| "When the password is accepted, BLDesk closes, installs the update and opens again by itself." | `pollLinuxSession` reads `AUTHORIZED`, sets `installPhase: 'installing'` and calls `app.quit()` 600 ms later; `install-update.sh` waits for the process, then runs `apt-get install`; `update-session.sh` runs the restart command once the process has exited | Unchanged in effect (it also restarted before); the window no longer freezes first. |
| "If you close the prompt, or the password is not accepted, BLDesk stays open, the update stays ready, and a notice says it was not installed." | `pkexec` exit 126 or 127 is `RESULT cancelled`; `pollLinuxSession` returns to `ready` (`held` is kept) with `installError` from `describeFailure`; `UpdateMenu.tsx` shows it with `notifyFailure("Couldn't install the update", …)` | New. Before, a dismissed prompt left the status at `installing` after a freeze, with no message. |
| "Quitting BLDesk with an update waiting opens the same prompt as it closes, without opening BLDesk again." | `onAppQuit` calls `startLinuxSession(false)`: the session's RELAUNCH argument is `0` | Changed: before, the install blocked the quit for about ten seconds. |
| "What the updater did is written to updater.log in BLDesk's data folder, which is ~/.config/bldesk on Linux." | `log()` in `updater.ts` appends to `app.getPath('userData')/updater.log`; `~/.config/bldesk` is that folder on this machine | New. |
| The popover: "Waiting for you to approve the install of BLDesk <version> in the system's password prompt. BLDesk stays open until you do, and closing the prompt leaves it as it is." and "Password accepted. BLDesk is closing to install <version>, and opens again by itself when it is done." | `UpdateMenu.tsx`, by `installPhase` | New. The older text, "This window may not respond for a few seconds…", is kept for Windows and the AppImage, which still use the library's install. |
| The notices under "Couldn't install the update" | `CANCELLED_MESSAGE` and `describeFailure` in `linuxInstall.ts`; the checksum, missing-file, `no_new_privs` and missing-installer messages in `installInputProblem` and `startLinuxSession`; the reasons from `install-update.sh`'s `FAILED` lines | New. |

### Checks performed

- `npm run typecheck` (every guard and test, including `test:linux-update-scripts`, `test:linux-install` and the extended `check-updater-guards.mjs`), `npm run test:terminal` and `npm run build` pass on this branch.
- `test:linux-update-scripts`, 50 tests, run `install-update.sh` and `update-session.sh` with a fake `pkexec`, `apt-get` and `dpkg-deb`: a good package gives `AUTHORIZED` then `INSTALLED` and nothing else on standard output; `apt-get` is given a root-owned copy (mode 644 in a 755 folder) whose checksum is the one checked, and a file swapped after `AUTHORIZED` is not what is installed; `AUTHORIZED` comes before BLDesk has exited and `apt-get` runs only after it has; a wrong checksum, a symlink, a file owned by another user, a package that is not `bldesk`, missing or malformed arguments (including a shell command in the checksum, a newline in the path) and a missing `PKEXEC_UID` are each refused with one `FAILED` line and nothing installed; an awkward file name is not run as a command; the scratch folder is removed on every path; an app that never exits does not hold the install up; exit 126 and 127 are `cancelled`; `RELAUNCH=0`, or the old BLDesk still running, never opens BLDesk. Mutation check: 17 deliberate breakages of the two scripts (no checksum check, no owner check, no symlink check, installing the original, apt output on standard output, no wait, `AUTHORIZED` too early, no clean-up, no package-name check, no `pkexec` requirement, no process-id check, the folder never opened, cancelled treated as failed, always relaunching, relaunch ignoring RELAUNCH, stderr into the output file, exit 0 alone counting as installed) are each caught by at least one test.
- `test:linux-install`, 19 tests, and 11 mutations of `linuxInstall.ts`, all caught; the guard additions, 15 mutations (a `dpkg -i` in the helper, no `set -e`, no `sha512sum`, a policy path or an `APP_DIR` that disagrees with the package layout, a policy that allows without a password, the helper or the policy not packaged, the `chmod`s in `after-install.sh` loosened, the library's install at quit switched back on, `install()` not using the helper), all caught.
- `apt-get -s -y install` of an older `.deb` over the installed one ends "E: Packages were downgraded and -y was used without --allow-downgrades." (exit 100), so the helper cannot be made to downgrade.
- The deb built by `electron-builder --linux deb` contains `/opt/BLDesk/resources/linux/install-update.sh` and `update-session.sh` (mode 755) and `/usr/share/polkit-1/actions/com.termau.bldesk.policy`.
- Real packaged Electron (a `--dir` build, isolated user data and cache folders, fictitious data, the mock feed from `scripts/gui-test`, a stand-in `pkexec` that waits three seconds and then answers as told, and a stand-in `apt-get` that takes eight seconds): (1) password accepted: the title bar showed “Waiting for password” and the window stayed responsive for the three seconds; then “Installing”; the app closed within about a second; the stand-in `apt-get` started about a second after the password was accepted, once the old process had gone (the unit tests check that order directly); the session wrote `RESULT installed`; BLDesk was started again with its own arguments and showed its window. (2) Prompt dismissed (exit 126): the status returned to `ready`, Restart to update was back, and “Couldn't install the update” with the cancelled message was shown in the corner. (3) Password accepted, `apt-get` fails: the app closed, `RESULT failed apt-get could not install the package` was written, BLDesk was started again, and its first screen showed the same notice (the stand-in's debug port had to be moved for that run, see below). (4) Quit from the tray with an update waiting: the prompt opened while the app closed (it exited in about a second); accepted, the update installed and BLDesk was not started again; dismissed, `RESULT cancelled` was left and the next start showed the notice and removed the folder. A screenshot of the title bar in states (1) and (2) was read.
- The re-check before installing, in the same packaged build: (a) the mock feed was switched to a newer version after the download and Restart chosen: the state went `installing`/`checking` (1.0.62-beta.31), then straight to `installing`/`authorising` for 1.0.62-beta.32 about 0.3 s later with no second click, and on to installed; (b) with the feed answering every request with an error, Restart went `checking`, then `authorising` for the downloaded version within 0.2 s; (c) with nothing newer, `checking` then `authorising`. A first run of (a) refused to install: both stand-in versions have the same bytes, so electron-updater handed back the file it already held under the older name, and the lookup by file name found no checksum; it now falls back to the release's only `.deb` entry (and gives none when there are several), which test:linux-install covers.
- Not a product problem, but worth knowing for the next person: the remote-debugging listener of the old instance leaks into the process BLDesk starts, so the restarted instance cannot open the same debug port ("bind() failed: Address already in use") and Playwright cannot attach to it; the restarted instance worked, and was inspected on a second port. Also, the harness app on Linux keeps its downloaded update in `~/.cache/bldesk-updater`, shared with an installed BLDesk: a first run of this test wrote its stand-in package there (removed afterwards); `scripts/gui-test/README.md` now says to set `XDG_CACHE_HOME`.
- The roughly six seconds the package's post-install script took on every install and upgrade was found with `strace -f -ttt` on the installed script, run as root: `update-mime-database /usr/share/mime` took 6.74 s of the script's 6.82 s, and everything else (the launcher link, `update-desktop-database`, the permission fixes, the AppArmor reload) took under 0.1 s. On a copy of `/usr/share/mime` on the NVMe disk it took 6.1 s on each of three runs, with or without touching its package files, and 0.14 s on a copy in `/tmp` (which is in memory), which is why an earlier measurement missed it. The package ships nothing under `/usr/share/mime`, and its desktop entry's `MimeType=x-scheme-handler/bldesk;` is handled by `update-desktop-database`, so the call was removed from `linux/after-install.sh`; dpkg's `shared-mime-info` trigger on `/usr/share/mime/packages` rebuilds the database for a package that does ship a file there. The new test in `test:linux-package-scripts` fails on the old script. The shortened install was then measured on the real system, below.
- The real system, 21 October 2026: Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS, GNOME on Wayland, apt 3.2.0, with the real polkit, `pkexec` and `apt-get`. Test builds were installed from `.deb` files with versions just above the installed 1.0.62-beta.31 (1.0.62-beta.31.1 to .2, then .3 to .4), and the installed app was run against the mock feed from `scripts/gui-test`, which served the real 101 MB package. The password prompt showed the policy's message (the user read it back) and polkit logged the action `com.termau.bldesk.install-update`. Dismissing the prompt, twice, left BLDesk open with the notice and Restart to update back.
